# Ember — defect ledger

## Which ledger a finding belongs in

Every finding sorts into one of four, and the sort decides who moves. Getting
this wrong in either direction is how an implementation workaround quietly
becomes the language's semantics.

| Kind | Evidence | Where it goes | Who moves |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Implementation defect** | the rule is clear, the compiler violates it | `DEFECTS.md` (here) | the compiler |
| **Missing coverage** | the compiler may be right and nobody had proved it | a conformance case; no ledger entry unless it fails | nobody |
| **Normative contradiction** | two rules require different things, neither marked as governing | `spec-errata.md` | **neither, until the owner rules** |
| **Deliberate divergence** | the rule is clear, the compiler knowingly differs, with a reason | `DEVIATIONS.md` | the compiler, later |
| **Waiting on another feature** | the rule is clear, and a feature it needs is not built yet | `NOT-IMPLEMENTED.md` | the compiler, once that feature exists |

The third is the one that needs discipline, because a compiler cannot be called
wrong against a document that says both things — so the temptation is to pick
the reading that matches what is already built and move on. ERR-044 is the
worked example: `[TYP-15]`'s enumeration forbids a static-region `str` in a
class field, `[LT-3]` permits it in as many words, and `[TYP-15]`'s own
principle sides with `[LT-3]`. The compiler follows the enumeration and stays
there, unchanged, until the owner decides.


Every defect found and what closed it. One row per defect, newest first.

**Why this exists.** Defects were recorded in prose, spread across
`docs/HANDOFF.md`'s per-block sections, where "found" and "fixed" read the
same. A reader could not tell which of them are closed. This file answers that
one question, and links to where the reasoning lives.

**How to use it.** Fixing a defect updates four documents, and the row records
which:

1. **this ledger** — the row, with how the fix was *verified*: a program that
   failed before and passes now, or a test that goes red when the fix is
   removed;
2. **`docs/HANDOFF.md`** — the reasoning, in the section for the block it
   belongs to;
3. **`docs/DECISIONS.md`** — an ADR, where the fix took a decision the
   specification does not force;
4. **the specification** — `docs/spec-source/ember-spec.md`, regenerated into
   `docs/spec/`, where the document's own wording admitted the wrong reading.
   That goes through `docs/spec-errata.md` and its procedure, never by hand.

The fourth is the one to be careful with: **an implementation gap is not a spec
defect.** Where the document was right and the compiler was wrong, say so in the
row and leave the specification alone — ERR-014, ERR-019 and ERR-022 record what
the opposite mistake costs.

Status is one of **fixed**, **open**, or **won't fix** with the reason.

---

## 2026-09-23 — 0.9.9 audit of the existing implementation

| # | Defect | Rule | Status | Fixed in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D-544 | **Generic comparison diagnostics depended on operand order and cascaded after an unresolved name.** `0 < x` for unbounded T reported E2020, while the reverse named the missing Ord bound. A generic operand beside an unknown name could add a second bound error. | `[TYP-17]`, `[TYP-21]`, `[DIA-14]` | **fixed** | Check both generic operands for Eq/Ord and stop after one missing bound; skip this check when either operand already has the error type. TYP-17 literal-left rejection/Float acceptance tests and DIA-14 unknown-operand controls reproduce the old diagnostics. Float NaN/signed-zero semantics remain unchanged. |
| D-543 | **Imported associated values lost formatting and Float capabilities inside generic-owner methods.** A field of imported TextValue[S] kept S.Item at a slot from another signature; index-only predicates rejected plain/debug/padded formatting and `+ 1.0` despite declared text/Float bounds. Free-function counterparts passed. | `[IFC-4]`, `[TYP-17]`, `[STD-27]`, `[LEX-19]` | **fixed** | `param_bound_named` and `float_param` use the established `generic_bounds(index, name)` recovery, including Display detection and format-spec checking. IFC-4 imported-projection tests cover free/owner methods, f32/f64 numeric operations, and rejection when only unrelated parameters have text bounds. |
| D-542 | **Missing generic text bounds looked like unimplemented formatting, and f-strings emitted dependent format-spec errors.** Unbounded parameters in f-strings emitted E1010; aggregates containing them emitted E0900. Unsupported concrete formatters retain E0900. |
| D-541 | **A generic owner method left its associated projection pointing at the old parameter slot.** `Holder[T].item[S: Source](self, source: S) -> S.Item` failed on `Holder[str]` with E2060 for S.Item and E2020 reporting the same displayed type on both sides. Method type values were rebased after substituting owner parameters, but projection metadata retained the old owner-prefixed base. | `[IFC-4]`, `[TYP-16]`, `[TYP-17]`, `[TYP-18]` | **fixed** | Rebase each method-owned projection base in `register_recipe_method`, as interface-method registration already does. `IFC-4/accept_generic_owner_methods_rebase_associated_results` fails independently on cb9d2df and passes with the fix; the D-540 method-prefix test exercises the combined path. Positional arguments were resolved before hidden projection parameters were declared; equality binding payloads already deferred this work. | `[IFC-4]`, `[TYP-17]` | **fixed** | Reserve written parameter slots, then declare positional-argument projections before resolving each interface instance. Keep equality and callable payloads deferred. Five IFC-4 positional-bound tests cover int/str concrete calls, nested Option, sibling projections, later written parameters, enclosing generic methods, equality/callable coexistence and single-error rejection of missing members. Baseline cb9d2df reproduces all five failures. ADR-146. |
| D-539 | **Float's generic bound check granted undeclared text capabilities and printing used an implementation-limit diagnostic.** Float alone incorrectly met Display/Debug helper bounds; printing the same parameter/projection emitted E0900. | `[STD-27]`, `[STD-9]`, `[TYP-17]` | **fixed** | Float implies only its specified capabilities under their canonical standard names; direct missing text bounds report E2040. Five STD-27 Float tests cover direct/projected rejection and explicit Display/Debug with f32/f64. The original review's expected acceptance was corrected against the specification, which is unchanged. |
| D-538 | **Associated types were left unresolved inside constructed types.** `Result[Self.Item, int]`, user enums/classes and built-in wrappers failed interface signatures or generic calls; a nested `Option[Self.Atom]` equality retained the declaration's placeholder. | `[IFC-4]`, `[TYP-16]`, `[TYP-17]` | **fixed** | ADR-145: associated resolution, replacement and parameter substitution share constructor traversal. | D-537 | **A bound method retained its receiver's unresolved associated type inside its own generic constraints.** A call requiring `Build[int]` was checked against `Build[Self.Item]`. | `[IFC-4]`, `[TYP-17]` | **fixed** | `bound_call_signature` resolves positional bounds and equality-bound instances for the receiver. IFC-4 `accept_method_bounds_resolve_the_receivers_associated_type` exercises fixed and opaque caller types, RED before the fix. |
| D-536 | **Generic interface instances copied associated constraints without substituting their arguments.** `Outer[int].Member: Tag[T]` and nested equality values kept T instead of int. | `[IFC-4]`, `[TYP-17]` | **fixed** | Instantiate associated bounds, equality instances and equality values with the interface arguments. | D-535 | **Concrete associated values could violate equality requirements in their declared bounds.** A value meeting Element but binding Item=str was accepted when Element[Item=int] was required, including defaults and nested Option values. | `[IFC-4]`, `[TYP-17]` | **fixed** | Implementation checking resolves the outer owner's requirements and projects each actual value through the specific required interface instance. | D-533 | **Operator-shaped methods on unrelated generic bounds enabled operators.** Lookalike add/neg/pow/add_assign methods supplied operators without the nominal standard interface. | `[TYP-17]`, `[TYP-21]`, `[TYP-40]` | **fixed** | Filter generic operator lookup by canonical interface origin, including indexed assignment; use recovered parameter bounds. TYP-17 paired lookalike/nominal tests cover direct/projected arithmetic, unary, power and compound assignment, plus ordinary explicit method calls. |
| D-532 | **An associated type's bound did not bring its parents, so a generic function could not number an iterator it was given.** `for i, x in it.enumerate(start=s)` inside `fn f[I: Iterator](owned it: I, s: int)` was refused ("`Enumerate[I, I.Position]` cannot be iterated: it has no `next` method"), and so was `e.next()`: `Enumerate`'s `next` needs `P: ItemCount`, whose parents `Ord` and `Copy` `I.Position` (declared `type Position: ItemCount`) did not have, because the bounds of an associated type of a type parameter were its declared ones only, while a parameter's own bounds bring their parents (`[IFC-3]`). The same refused `h.get().area()` for `H: Holder` with `type Thing: Named` and `interface Named: Shape` in user code. Found 2026-10-06 while building 0.9.10_Hardened_2's per-use numbering (a probe). | `[IFC-3]`, `[IFC-4]`, `[STD-19]`, `[TYP-17]` | **fixed** | ADR-143: `assoc_bounds_of`, which every way of giving `T.Name` its bounds goes through (the bounds of a name, a hidden parameter a signature declares, one a body reaches, and their closing once the interfaces are known), now closes them with their parents; where a hidden parameter is made, its bindings reach the parents too, as a parameter's do. The specification already said it (`[IFC-3]` a bound brings its parents, `[IFC-4]` `T.Name` has what its declared bounds provide): unchanged. `STD-19/accept_a_generic_function_numbers_an_iterator_it_is_given`, `IFC-3/accept_an_associated_types_bound_brings_its_parents` (both refused by the compiler before the fix), `IFC-3/reject_an_associated_types_bound_brings_only_its_parents`. |
| D-531 | **A C++ program could not include the runtime header.** G8-4's 256-bit integers declared `typedef struct ember_i256 { _Alignas(16) uint64_t w[4]; }`, and `_Alignas` is C11 only: g++ refused the export header that includes `ember_rt.h` ("ISO C++ forbids declaration of `_Alignas` with no type"). MSVC and clang accept it, so only CI's Linux gcc job failed, on `export_headers_link_from_separate_c_and_cpp_translation_units` (c19dc27). Found 2026-10-06 by that CI run. | `[BLD-FFI-5]` | **fixed** | `EMBER_ALIGNED16` is `alignas(16)` in C++ and `_Alignas(16)` in C, for all four 128- and 256-bit structs. Checked with g++ `-std=c++17` and gcc `-std=c11 -Wall -Wextra` in WSL; the milestone test above is the regression test (CI's gcc job). |
| D-530 | **A running total in an inner loop lost its overflow check in the outer loop.** `[RNG-4]`'s running-total bound took the one checked `+` that changes a total as running once per turn of every counted loop around it, so for `for round in 0..5: y = total * 1000; for i in 0..1000000: total = total + 1` (`i32`) the outer loop's header was given `total ≤ 5` instead of 5,000,000, and `total * 1000` lost its check: it printed `-1294967296` where it must panic. The inner loop's own widening hid it in most programs (the inner `+` kept its check). Found 2026-10-06 reading the bound while extending it for the p1 benchmark. | `[RNG-4]`, `[TYP-8]` | **fixed** | ADR-142: the chain's runs per outer turn are counted (`runs_per_turn`): once outside any inner loop, an inner counted loop's trip count times its runs inside it for each entry, and no bound through any other cycle (a `while`). The seeds are made again from the seeded facts until none is new, so an inner loop starts from its outer loop's bound. `RNG-4/run_fail_a_running_total_in_an_inner_loop_counts_every_inner_turn`. |
| D-529 | **An inner loop over a string's characters ran on the first turn of the loop around it only.** `hoist_invariant_views_all` (faa5df2, the particles benchmark) moves the setting of an inner loop's iterator out of an outer loop when the outer loop writes the local once; the iterator's step writes its position through `ref mut position`, which was not counted, so `position = 0` was done once: `for _ in 0..4: for c in "abc".chars(): n += 1` gave 3, not 12 (`char_indices` too; `bytes`, a list and `enumerate` were not affected). Benchmarks were not: every output is compared with C's. Found 2026-10-06 by the frame-buffer test's 100-character string coming out 25 long. | `[CTL-2]` | **fixed** | A local whose own storage is lent mutably in the loop (or lent at all while it holds a `Cell`) is changed there: neither its setting nor a read of it leaves the loop. Its address still may (`&mut ps` for `ps.iter_mut()` is made once, as the particles benchmark needs), and a loan of an element of a list or a view (`&mut v[i]`) changes the elements, not the local. `run-pass/an_inner_loops_iterator_starts_again_each_turn` (fails without the fix: 3 for 12); `OPT-2/accept_a_view_an_outer_loop_leaves_alone_is_made_once` keeps its seven views. |
| D-528 | **`parse[i256]()` stopped the compiler, and `parse[u256]()` stopped at `u64.MAX`.** `parse`'s target match read every signed type's largest value as `(1 << (bits - 1)) - 1` in 128 bits, which overflows at 256 ("attempt to shift left with overflow"), and every unsigned type of 64 bits or more as a `u64`, so `"340282366920938463463374607431768211460".parse[u256]()` was `Overflow`. Found 2026-10-06 auditing the delegated rulings (ODR-029). | `[TYP-42]`, `[TXT-10]` | **fixed** | ADR-140 (the owner's ruling of 2026-10-06, reversing ADR-139's refusal of both with `E2020`): `parse` reads `u256` and `i256` over their whole range, with the narrower integers' statuses (`Overflow` only past the real top), as `[TXT-10]` and `[TYP-42]` say in 0.9.10_Hardened_2. `TXT-10/accept_parse_256_bit_counts`. |
| D-527 | **An index or a size wider than `usize` was cut to its low bits.** `integer_as_usize` converted any integer index with `as`, so `xs[(u64.MAX as u128) + 2]` read `xs[1]` and `xs[-(2^64) as i128]` read `xs[0]`, where `[TYP-31]` says an index of any integer type that is negative or not less than the length panics; `insert`, `reserve`, `repeat` and the other sizes it reads did the same. Found 2026-10-06 auditing the delegated rulings (G8-4's question: a value of a supported type that fails). | `[TYP-31]`, `[TYP-1]` | **fixed** | ADR-139: a value wider than `usize` is converted only when `usize` holds it (below zero, `isize`, so a narrow negative index is still printed back with the length); otherwise it panics with the whole value, `index … is out of bounds` (`size … is negative or too large` for a size). `TYP-31/run_fail_an_index_wider_than_usize_is_out_of_bounds`, `TYP-31/run_fail_a_wide_index_below_isize_is_out_of_bounds`, `TYP-31/accept_a_wide_index_that_fits_reads_its_element`. |
| D-526 | **`a..` stopped before its type's maximum.** The `for` over `a..` stepped its counter before running the body (`local = next; next = next + 1`), and std's `RangeFromIter.next` computed the successor of the value it gave, so `for i in 250u8..` panicked giving `255`, and `(254u8..).iter()` gave `254` and then panicked. ODR-027 had ruled "counting up to its type's maximum is an overflow", which reads as this behaviour; it is the G8-4 question again, a value of the type that a loop cannot give. Found 2026-10-06 auditing the delegated rulings (ODR-027). | `[CTL-3]`, `[TYP-8]`, ODR-027 | **fixed** | ADR-139: `a..` gives the maximum, and the step past it is made, and panics, when another value is asked for (a flag in the loop; `RangeFromIter` holds whether its value was given); `RangeFromIter.skip_front` leaves values out at once (`Integer.count_to_top`). `[CTL-3]`'s words changed. `CTL-3/run_fail_counting_from_past_the_maximum` (now prints `255`), `CTL-3/run_fail_an_open_range_iterator_asks_past_the_maximum`, `CTL-3/accept_an_open_range_gives_its_types_top`. |
| D-525 | **`for i in a..=T.MAX` ran forever.** `lower_for_range` tested the end only at the head and stepped with a plain add, so after `T.MAX` the counter wrapped to the type's bottom and the head's test passed again; its comment said the inclusive form "stops short of the type's maximum", where `[CTL-3]` says it terminates after `MAX`. The range facts, which read the loop as ending, then removed the body's own `break` test: `for i in (u64.MAX - 2) ..= u64.MAX` with a `break` after six turns printed forever. Found 2026-10-06 auditing the delegated rulings. | `[CTL-3]`, `[RNG-4]` | **fixed** | ADR-139: an inclusive loop whose end is not written as a number below the top tests for its last turn before stepping. The loop passes drop the test where the range facts prove the end below the top, and otherwise run a copy without it, entered when the end is below the top (`settle_inclusive_loops`), so every counted-loop pass sees the loop it saw before; `[EXC-8]`'s hoisting reads the test as part of the step. `CTL-3/accept_an_inclusive_loop_ends_at_its_types_top`. |
| D-524 | **A type argument written in an expression could not be a dotted path.** `left.as_count[CountSum[I.Count, J.Count]]()` was "expected a type argument" at `I.Count`: a method call's type arguments arrive as expressions, and `type_from_expr` read a path, an instantiation, a tuple or a fixed array, not `T.Name` (nor `module.Type`). Found 2026-10-06 building G8-4's `chain` counts. | `[GRM-8a]`, `[IFC-4]` | **fixed** | ADR-138: a field chain over a path is read as the type path it spells. `STD-19/accept_a_chain_counts_a_level_above_its_sides` (std's `Chain.len` names its count that way). |
| D-523 | **A generic instance's `mut self` default was copied before the instance's associated types were known.** `apply_generic_extensions` made the defaults' copies (`register_instantiated_defaults`) before recording the extension's `type Name = …` (`record_extension_assoc`) and filling the ones left to the interface's default, so `ArrayIntoIter[i64]`'s copy of `skip_front(mut self, n: Count) -> Count` kept `n: Self.Count` where its body's `Count.of(0)` was the default `int` ("`>` cannot be applied to `Self.Count` and `i64`"). An `owned self` default waits for its first call and was not affected; D-516 was the same defect on the non-generic path. Found 2026-10-06 building G8-4. | `[IFC-4]`, ODR-049 | **fixed** | ADR-138: an instance's implementations are recorded, its associated types recorded and the defaulted ones filled, and only then its defaults copied (`collect_instantiated_implements`). Every std iterator's `skip_front` copy (the `Iterator` default) type-checks. |
| D-522 | **A hidden parameter made while another list of parameters was current lost its bounds.** In `fn pairs[I: Iterator, J: Iterator](…) -> int: return it.zip(other).count().to_int()`, `count()` gives `Zip[I, J]`'s `Count`, the hidden `I.Count` that applying `Zip`'s extension made (with `ItemCount`, the bound `Iterator` declares of it) in the parameter list current then, at its slot there; read in `pairs`' own list, where that slot is not it, it had no bounds: "`I.Count` has no method `to_int`; its bounds do not provide one". Found 2026-10-05 building G8-4. | `[IFC-4]`, `[TYP-17]` | **fixed** | ADR-138: a type parameter's bounds are read by its name when the slot holds another (`generic_bounds`): a hidden one's are what its base's bounds declare of the associated type (method calls through a bound, associated functions, and `implements`). `IFC-4/accept_a_generic_method_through_a_bound_reads_self_as_the_parameter` (with `count().to_int()`). |
| D-521 | **Whether an extension's method applies inside a generic body was asked of a hidden parameter's bounds, read against that body's parameters.** `struct Peek[I: Seq]` with an extension implementing `Seq` that says `type Size = I.Size`, where `Seq` declares `type Size: Ord = int`: in `Peek[I]`'s other block, `self.next()` was "`Peek[I]` has no method named `next`" (without the bound on `Size` it worked), because the extension's hidden `I.Size` was checked against `Ord` in the calling block's list, where it is not. Found 2026-10-05 building G8-4 (`Peekable`'s `Count`). | `[IFC-4]`, `[TYP-17]` | **fixed** | ADR-138: a hidden parameter's bounds are what its base's bound declares of the associated type, met wherever the base's bounds are, so `generic_extension_bounds_hold` checks the written parameters only. `IFC-4/accept_a_bounded_associated_type_keeps_the_other_blocks_methods`. |
| D-520 | **A call through a type parameter's bound read `Self.Name` as the parameter's `T.Name` in its result only.** `it.nth_back(n)` on an `I: DoubleEndedIterator`, where `nth_back(mut self, n: Count)`, took a `Self.Count` ("expected `Self.Count`, found `I.Count`"), and an associated function called through the parameter (`T.widen_count(gap)`) read neither its parameters nor its result. Found 2026-10-05 building G8-4. | `[IFC-4]`, `[TYP-17]` | **fixed** | ADR-138: `synth_bound_method` and `synth_bound_associated` resolve the callee's associated types through the bound for every parameter and the result. `IFC-4/accept_a_projection_names_its_associated_functions` (`it.skip_back(n)`, `I.grow(n)`). |
| D-519 | **An associated type of a type parameter could not be named in an expression.** `I.Size.of(2)` in a generic body was "cannot find `I` in this scope": `T.default()` resolved, and `Item.default()` in a default method (D-381), but `T.Name` only in a type. Found 2026-10-05 building G8-4. | `[IFC-4]` | **fixed** | ADR-138: `associated_type_named` reads `T.Name` as `resolve_projection` reads it in a type. `IFC-4/accept_a_projection_names_its_associated_functions`. |
| D-518 | **Two `extend` blocks of one generic type compared one projection as two.** Each block makes its own hidden parameter for `I.Size`, numbered in its own order: `Wrap[I]`'s `Seq` block has `I.Item` before `I.Size`, its `Measured` block only `I.Size`, so `Measured`'s `fn size(self) -> Size`, read as the `Seq` block's `I.Size`, was not the `Measured` block's: "`Wrap[I].size` does not match the signature required by `Measured`". It held in std only where the numbers happened to agree. Found 2026-10-05 building G8-4 (`Take[I]`'s `len`). | `[IFC-4]`, `[IFC-3]` | **fixed** | ADR-138: an implementation's signature is compared with hidden parameters of the owner's own parameters matched by name (`same_across_blocks`). `IFC-4/accept_two_blocks_of_one_type_share_a_projection`. |
| D-517 | **An associated type's bound was checked before every implementation was known.** An instance a generic extension's signature names (`Boxy[T]` in `Holder[T].boxed`, or std's `ArrayIntoIter[T]`) is made while the declarations are still read; its implementation was checked at once, so a default `Size = int` was found not to implement `Counted` when `extend int implements Counted` came later in the source: "`Boxy[T]`'s `Size` is `i64`, which does not implement `Counted`". Found 2026-10-05 building G8-4 (std's `Iterator.Count`). | `[IFC-4]`, `[IFC-3]` | **fixed** | ADR-138: the associated types' bounds and the parents are checked once `bounds_known`; `check_implementations` checks every implementation then, these instances' included. `IFC-4/accept_a_bound_on_an_associated_type_met_by_a_later_extension`. |
| D-516 | **A default method's copy kept `Self.Name` where the implementation leaves `Name` to the interface's default.** The copy's signature was made before the defaults were filled in (`register_defaults`), so a default method returning `Count` gave `Self.Count` where its body gave `int`. Found 2026-10-05 building G8-4. | `[IFC-4]`, ODR-049 | **fixed** | ADR-138: each implementation's defaulted associated types are filled before its defaults' copies are made. `IFC-4/accept_a_default_methods_copy_takes_the_defaulted_associated_type`. |
| D-515 | **A `for` over `zip` with a range's iterator was never one counted loop.** `zip` takes its argument borrowed (`[FN-2]`), so `(5..7).iter()` arrives as a borrow of a temporary, which the fused-loop builder (`fused_links_shape`, `fused_link_kinds`, `take_fused_chain`) did not look through: every `zip` whose argument was a range's iterator ran std's `Zip`, forwards and backwards, against ADR-087's "a chain of `take`, `skip`, `step_by`, `enumerate`, `zip` and `copied` over a range or a view is a counted loop". So `for a, b in (0 as u64 .. u64.MAX).iter().zip(…).rev()` panicked asking the range for an `int` length (G8-4's case 5). Found 2026-10-05 while measuring G8-4 for the owner. | `[CTL-3b]`, `[STD-19]`, `[FN-2]` | **fixed** | ADR-137: the builder reads through a borrow of a temporary (`zip_argument`); a borrow of a place (a named iterator) is kept, and that chain still runs std's `Zip`, since the place stays the caller's. `CTL-3b/accept_zip_with_a_temporary_range_is_one_counted_loop` (the giant range panicked and std's `Zip` was called without it; the named iterator's program, which the first version of the fix rejected with `E3050`, still compiles and gives the same output). |
| D-514 | **MSVC's `/O2` does not finish compiling a value 256 boxes deep.** `x = Box(Box(…256…("s".to_string())))`, `y = x.clone()`, `println(x == y)` in the release profile: `cl.exe /O2` ran 50 minutes on its C before the test run was stopped, and both the compiler before 2026-10-05's batch and after it time out at two minutes, so it is older than D-511 (which only changed how the C reads the boxes); the debug profile (`/Od`) builds and runs it at once. Each box level has its own clone, equality and drop code, so the optimiser likely inlines 256 levels. `[GRM-39]` wants such a program to compile on every C compiler. Found 2026-10-05 (autopilot): D-511's test was the first 256-deep box program run in the release profile. | `[GRM-39]`, `[CG-C-2]` | **open** | **Narrowed (2026-10-05 08:53):** MSVC's automatic inlining. The same program builds in 1.6 s with `/O2 /Ob1` or `/O2 /Ob0` (`_CL_=-Ob1`) and in 1.7 s with clang `-O2`; `/O2` (which inlines any function, `/Ob2`) does not finish in 2 minutes. The only inlinable chain in its C is the 255 drop-glue functions, each calling the next (`em_drop_glue_k` drops level k+1 and frees level k). Next: confirm on the drop glue (`__declspec(noinline)` on every sixteenth level of the saved C), then a general bound in the backend: a generated helper chain kept out of line every sixteen levels, as D-358 bounded nesting; gcc in WSL too. |
| D-513 | **Nested literals took the frame quadratically (G7-2).** Each level of `Some(Some(…))` was its own temporary, each larger than the one inside, all in one frame: four 256-deep `Option[String]` literals in one function overflowed a 1 MB stack in the debug profile (`[GRM-39]` requires 256 levels). Found by the second review (G7-2). | `[GRM-39]` | **fixed** | ADR-135: a literal's temporary the next literal reads once is written into that literal's field and never declared (`foldable_aggregates`, `write_fields`), its own retains made there. `GRM-39/accept_eight_values_nested_256_deep_in_one_function` overflows without it; `GRM-39/accept_nested_literals_are_written_in_place`. |
| D-512 | **A wide signed range's length overflowed instead of saying it has none (G8-4).** `(i64.MIN..i64.MAX).iter().len()` panicked `integer overflow in -` where the unsigned widths say `a range of more than int.MAX values has no length`, and an inclusive range exactly `int.MAX` long overflowed its `+ 1`. Found by the second review (G8-4). | `[STD-19]` | **fixed** (the message) | ADR-135: counted in `i128`. `STD-19/run_fail_a_wide_signed_range_has_no_length`. Answered by the owner's design G8-4 (0.9.10, ADR-138): every range has its length, in a type that holds it; the test is now `STD-19/accept_a_wide_range_has_its_length`. |
| D-511 | **A value read through 256 boxes was one C expression 261 brackets deep (G7-3).** Past clang's default bracket limit of 256, which D-358 set out to respect. Found by the second review (G7-3). | `[GRM-39]`, `[CG-C-2]` | **fixed** | ADR-135: past sixteen dereferences in one place, `p[0]` for `(*p)`. `GRM-39/accept_a_value_boxed_256_deep_reads_without_deep_brackets` (with `clang -fbracket-depth=256` checked by hand: accepted after, refused before). |
| D-510 | **The gcc linker probe kept an answer it could not finish (ADR-118).** When GCC or its linker could not be started, `probe_linker` answered "no" and the cache kept it until the compiler file changed, turning the linker's section collection off. It showed as a failure of `the_linker_probe_is_cached_until_the_linker_changes` in a WSL run on 2026-10-05: the test runs its fake GCC and linker just after writing them, and Linux refuses to `exec` a file another thread's new child still holds open ("text file busy"), which happens more often with every core running tests. Introduced 2026-10-04 with the cache (`73766e1`). | ADR-118 | **fixed** | A program that does not start is tried again (four times, 10 to 80 ms apart, `output_started`), and only an answer the probe finished is cached. The test now also makes the linker unexecutable: no answer may be kept, and once it can run it is asked (with the old caching the test fails there, run in WSL). |
| D-509 | **A Sync object's retain was an out-of-line compare-exchange loop (`[RT-10]`).** `[RT-10]`: copying a strong handle of a `@sync` object "is one atomic `fetch_add`, never a compare-exchange loop", and retain's and release's fast paths are inline in `ember_rt.h`, with `[OBJ-5]`'s deinitialising checks on the release-to-zero path only. A Sync retain called `ember_obj_retain`, which ran `Weak.upgrade`'s compare-exchange loop (on MSVC its two loads were locked instructions as well), a Sync release called `ember_obj_release` out of line, and the plain inline retain tested the deinitialising flag on every copy. Found 2026-10-04 (autopilot) writing the `[RC-4]` thread test. | `[RT-10]`, `[RC-4]`, `[RT-8]` | **fixed** | ADR-133: inline `ember_retain_sync`/`ember_release_sync` (one atomic add, relaxed; one atomic subtract, acquire-release), only a count at its end out of line; the C backend calls them for a Sync class, `ember_retain` picks between the inline forms by type information, and the plain retain's flag test is gone. `RC-3/accept_fresh_sync_temporaries_keep_atomic_counts` counts `ember_retain_sync(` (it failed with the backend's old choice); the thread test `sync_counts_hold_under_threads` fails with the add made plain. No spec change. |
| D-508 | **A call on a final class's handle read the object's table (`[DSP-1]`).** `[DSP-1]`: "A call is dispatched statically when the receiver's static type is a final class or the method is not `virtual`." Only the second half was built: every call to a virtual method loaded the object's type information and table and called through it, so `child.tick()` on a final `Child`, and `child.label()` that `Child` inherits from its open base, were indirect calls the C compiler could not inline (the same body ran, so only speed showed it). Found 2026-10-04 (autopilot) probing `[DSP-5]`. | `[DSP-1]` | **fixed** | ADR-132: MIR records the receiver's class as the program wrote it (under the upcast to the method's class), and the C backend calls the one body that class and every class below it hold in the slot; a final class has only itself. `DSP-1/accept_a_call_on_a_final_class_is_direct` counts the table reads in the C (four before, two after: the calls through the open base, which still run the overrides); with the receiver's class left out it fails on `child.label()`, inherited from `Base` where `Other` replaces it. No spec change. |
| D-507 | **A debug run did not report leaked cycles unless asked, and the report named no object's shortest cycle (`[WK-15]`, `[WK-4]`).** `ember run` in the debug profile printed nothing for a leaked strong cycle without `--leak-check`, and `--no-leak-check` did not exist; the report listed each component's objects and edges but not, for each object, its shortest strong cycle as `Type.field` edges under `L3017`. The live-object registry behind it was a list, so every release walked every live object, and the report searched the whole edge list from every node: neither could be on by default. Found 2026-10-04 (autopilot) auditing Part VIII: WK-4 and WK-15 had no case. | `[WK-15]`, `[WK-4]`, `[WK-8]` | **fixed** | ADR-130: the registry is an open-addressing set (O(1) track and untrack, a spin lock for attached host threads), the report finds components with an iterative Tarjan over edges grouped by owner, names each object's shortest cycle (a breadth-first search inside its component, grouped by shape, at most 64 objects listed per component), and `ember run` in the debug profile has it unless `--no-leak-check`. `tests/conformance/WK-15/`, `WK-4/`, the milestone `debug_runs_report_leaks_unless_turned_off` and the L3017 page (run, not checked) fail without it. No spec change. `ember test` does not exist yet; when it does it takes the same default. |
| D-506 | **A copy of a class handle that ended while its source still held the object was retained and released (`[RC-2c]`).** `b = a` in a loop body retained and `b`'s end released, with nothing between them that could end the object: `a` kept its own count and was not re-pointed. `[RC-2]` guarantees no retain or release there. Found 2026-10-04 (autopilot) auditing Part VIII: RC-2c had no test. | `[RC-2c]`, `[RC-3]` | **fixed** | ADR-129: the `[RC-3]` uncounted-handle analysis, which proved this for a copy out of a list, takes a local as the copy's source too: a user-written local or `owned` parameter holding a class handle, never assigned a copy (its count is its own), and on every path from the copy to its end not re-pointed, moved, dropped or mutably lent (a field written through it is fine). `tests/conformance/RC-2c/accept_a_copy_ending_while_its_source_holds_the_object_is_not_counted.em` counts one retain (the copy whose source is assigned anew keeps it); two without the change. No benchmark program's C changes. No spec change. |
| D-505 | **A class handle stored from a temporary was retained, and the temporary released it again (`[RC-2d]`).** `t.leaf = Leaf(7)`, `leaves[0] = make(8)` and a literal's element (`[Leaf(1)]`, `Some(Leaf(1))`) copied the fresh handle into place, which retains a counted `Copy` type, and the temporary's end released it: two count operations `[RC-2]` guarantees are not emitted. Found 2026-10-04 (autopilot) auditing Part VIII: RC-2d had no test. | `[RC-2d]`, `[RC-2]`, `[OWN-5]` | **fixed** | ADR-129: an assignment moves its own temporary into the place (after dropping the old value, `[OWN-5]`), and a struct, tuple, array or enum literal moves a field or element made in a temporary; a place is still copied (retained), its owner keeping its own count. `tests/conformance/RC-2d/accept_a_temporary_stored_into_a_field_is_moved.em` asserts no retain (two with the store copying, one with the literal copying); `RC-2b/` gains its first case. Eight cases pinned the retains a literal made copying a fresh handle in (seven in `RC-2e/`, `run-pass/array_copy_struct_class_handle.em`) and now count without them; the retains they exist for (an owned call, an insertion, a capture) remain. No benchmark program's C changes. No spec change. |
| D-504 | **A `return`, `break` or `continue` never dropped the temporaries of the statements it left.** `return number(Marker(1)) + number(Marker(2))` ran neither destructor; nor did a `for` bound's temporary (`for i in 0..number(Marker(11)): return i`), a `match` scrutinee's when an arm returned, or one made in the statement a `break`/`continue` left. A destructor was skipped and what it owned leaked (a lock guard made there would stay held): early exits ended the scopes' locals only. Found 2026-10-04 (autopilot) while fixing D-488. | `[EXP-4]`, `[CTL-8]`, `[DRP-3]` | **fixed** | ADR-127: a `return`'s own temporaries end first, then every pending `defer` (`[CTL-8]`), then the locals of the scopes left and the temporaries of the statements left together, the last registered first; a `break`/`continue` ends the ones made inside its loop. `tests/conformance/EXP-4/accept_an_early_exit_drops_the_temporaries_of_what_it_leaves.em` fails with the early exits ending only the locals. No spec change. |
| D-503 | **A condition's temporaries outlived the block it chose, and a `while` dropped only its last condition's.** `if positive(Marker(3))` dropped the marker after the `then` block, where `[EXP-4]` says before it; `while positive(Marker(n))` made one each turn and dropped only the last, after the loop: the others leaked. The other agent's D-488 notes recorded the case. | `[EXP-4]`, `[CTL-4]`, `[DRP-3]` | **fixed** | ADR-127: the condition's value is copied out, its temporaries are dropped, then the branch, on every `while` evaluation (the final false one before `else`). A condition that makes no temporary with a destructor is lowered as before, so the loop headers the analyses read are unchanged. `tests/conformance/EXP-4/accept_condition_temporaries_drop_before_each_body.em` fails without it. No spec change. |
| D-502 | **Every Linux program loaded libm at start-up, even one that does no maths: the empty program ran 1.08x to 1.12x its C twin with gcc.** The runtime's `T.clamped` used `fmin`/`fmax` (and an unoptimised build `copysign`), which glibc keeps in libm; `--as-needed` decides before `--gc-sections` drops the unused function, so libm stayed needed. The C twin built with libm forced in took the same time as Ember (317.7 against 315.2 µs, the plain C 291.0). Found investigating `a00_empty`, a new over-10% row with gcc (D-492's follow-up). | `[RT-1]`, `[RNG-3a]` | **fixed** | ADR-126: the clamp is written with comparisons (`v >= lo ? v : lo`, then `<= hi`), which gives `fmin(fmax(v, lo), hi)` exactly for finite endpoints, NaN to `lo` included; `copysign(65536.0, sign)` is `signbit(sign) ? -65536.0 : 65536.0`. The runtime now refers to no libm symbol at `-O0`, `-O2` or `-O3`; a program that does maths still links it. `a00_empty` with gcc 0.989x the C (283.7 against 280.5 µs, 401 interleaved runs, guest CPU 0, mains). `gcc_managed_linking_preserves_runtime_roots_and_host_linking` fails with either call restored (`readelf -d` lists libm). No spec change. |
| D-501 | **ADR-117's copy chains copied register locals by their fields: `split_whitespace` ran 1.04x the previous compiler with MSVC.** A view copied from a place written by fields is copied by fields so that MSVC does not read back whole what it stored narrow (D-475); ADR-117 followed that through every copy, including copies of a plain local whose address is never taken, which MSVC holds in registers, where the field copies only got in the way. Found comparing every benchmark built by `e2cbba3` and the batch. | `[CG-C-3]` (the speed the backend owes) | **fixed** | ADR-125: the copy is by fields only when its source is read from memory: a projection (a field, a niche `Option`'s payload, a dereference) or a local whose address the body takes. Hand-reverting just those four copies in `split_whitespace`'s C gave 61.5 ms against 61.3 for the previous compiler; built: MSVC 0.998, `split()` 0.927, `a05_structs` 1.00. Treating a niche `Option` local's payload as a register was measured and refused (`split()` 1.23x: MSVC keeps that `Option` in memory). `aggregates_and_views_are_written_field_by_field` fails with the rule removed. No spec change. |
| D-500 | **The one-byte search returned its answer as `hit ? hit - s.ptr : SIZE_MAX`, which MSVC compiled to a `cmov` after `memchr` and a taken jump on every call: `lines()` 1.05x.** ADR-111's one-byte path is right (a separator known only at run time skips the generic loop: `split()` 0.93x), but its shape cost the constant `"\n"` search in `lines()`. | `[TXT-10]` | **fixed** | ADR-125: the found case falls through (`if (from < len) { hit = memchr(...); if (hit != NULL) return ...; } return SIZE_MAX;`). The early-return form still gave the `cmov`. `lines()`'s loop is then instruction for instruction the previous compiler's except `test rax, rax` for `cmp rax, 1` (one byte shorter); what is left of `t3_lines` against the previous compiler with MSVC (about 4%: 65.1-66.6 ms against 63.4-63.7) is that loop's placement, measured, not work: the old C on the same runtime moves by the same amount. `split()` keeps 0.93x. No spec change. |
| D-499 | **A running total updated on only some turns got `[SIMD-7]`'s 64-turn blocks, which only added work: `a03_strings` (`if b == 111: count += 1` over the bytes) ran 1.27x the previous compiler with MSVC and 1.02x with clang.** ADR-109 let `for b in text.as_bytes()` reach the block proof (ODR-086); a loop with a branch is not vectorised by the C compiler, so the blocks add their entry test, per-value bound and end test for nothing. Found comparing every benchmark built by `e2cbba3` and `37f4420`. | `[SIMD-7]`, `[OPT-2]` | **fixed** | ADR-124: a total whose update does not dominate the loop's step keeps its check on each operation; the unchecked copy (one entry test, no per-block work) is still tried first. MSVC `a03_strings` 86.5 ms → 57.0 ms (68.5 with ADR-109 reverted alone; the rest is ADR-123's append), clang 68.7 → 67.5 ms. `SIMD-7/accept_a_total_updated_on_some_turns_keeps_its_check` fails with the rule removed; `accept_a_sum_is_checked_block_by_block` keeps an unconditional total's blocks. No spec change. |
| D-498 | **The progress reduction's division guard costs more than the original checked loop on short dynamic text.** One-byte guarded calls cost 2.1–2.2 ns under MSVC/clang/GCC, versus 0.8–1.2 ns for the retained checked control; the 60-byte MSVC/GCC gaps to C also remain open. | `[RNG-4]`, `[TYP-8]` | **open** | Actual emitted noinline kernels, a one-entry-only checked control and an independent C twin are measured in six eleven-pair cycles on mains, with exact power audits and fresh GCC short-input continuation. Next: price a general profitability fallback before division, including ASCII/Unicode crossover lengths, and independently verify any adopted guard condition. No required-safety-overhead verdict or threshold implementation. See TEXT-PERFORMANCE-2026-10-04.md. **Priced 2026-10-04 (autopilot), still open for the owner.** The division replaced by a checked multiply (`remaining * bound <= room`, the zero-bound test dropped; verifier, unit tests and a milestone assertion that fails with the division back) takes a one-byte `i ^ c` call from 2.23/2.12/2.11 ns to 1.41/1.36/1.22 ns (MSVC/clang/gcc; checked loop 1.19/1.17/1.03, C 0.79/1.17/1.03), 11 interleaved runs per cell from the compilers' own C, mains. A 16-byte gate before the division was no better on short text and worse with MSVC from 2 to 12 bytes. Not adopted: it moves the long loops' code. Against `1ff1c8c`, 21 interleaved runs: `t6_char_indices` 1.07-1.08x MSVC and 1.03-1.05x clang, `t1_chars` 1.05x MSVC, `t1`/`t1b` 1.02-1.03x gcc. The instructions are the same: in MSVC's `t6` the ASCII path's `mov edx,1` now crosses a 64-byte boundary, so each ASCII character fetches from three 64-byte windows instead of two. The gate moved clang's `t6` 1.12x the same way. Owner decision: adopt the multiply and accept the placement, or first lay the character loop out so the ASCII path runs straight into the loop's tail (then neither guard shape moves it). |
| D-497 | **MSVC still called the byte append for every small String append, hiding the checked reservation and copy from its caller.** | `[TXT-11]`, `[HEAP-8]` | **fixed** | ADR-123: StringPush uses a private header route that exposes the identical zero-count/reservation/copy/length behavior on MSVC and aliases the existing exported append on clang/GCC. The forced GNU body was measured and rejected after GCC regressed. The new regression was RED on the original emission and passes growth, empty/NUL/Unicode appends and single receiver/argument evaluation in all nine compiler/profile combinations. All 54 focused native cases pass; complete current suites remain required before push. |
| D-496 | **MSVC stopped inlining the typed UTF-8 decoder when loop versioning enlarged its caller.** Actual t6 assembly called the decoder on every character and its Ember/C ratio was 1.186. | `[TXT-10]`, `[CG-C-3]` | **fixed** | ADR-122: the MSVC cursor codecs are forced inline (EMBER_INLINED); gcc and clang keep `static inline`, since forcing gcc's costs `split_whitespace` 10% (45.25 → 40.48 ms without it, the other text programs ±1%). The same MSVC call cost `split_whitespace` 1.84x in the committed `37f4420` (ADR-116's shape, inside the step ADR-107 copies into the loop): 61.9 ms at `e2cbba3`, 114.9 ms at `37f4420`, 64.1 ms forced inline. Actual MSVC hot-loop calls disappear; the isolated whole-program ratio is 1.006 before the append improvement. Exhaustive Unicode/guard-page oracles pass on all three compilers, as do the focused overflow/order/borrow cases. The wider performance audit, including D-498, remains OPEN. |
| D-495 | **Borrowed String-to-str construction used an out-of-line descriptor-copy helper.** | `[SPN-1]`, `[CG-C-3]` | **fixed** | ADR-121: actual shared text references write paired same-source pointer/native-length fields through the existing view-copy route; unsupported forms retain the helper. Public helper ABI, provenance and access scopes remain. Four backend tests and nine selector regressions were break-tested; the pause checkpoint records the completed historical full suites/annotations/gates. Current combined native evidence is 54 cases across all profiles and three compilers. No isolated speedup or full audit closure is claimed. |
| D-494 | **Pure text reductions retained per-iteration overflow checks where decoder progress supplies a guarded bound; integration also exposed str length emitted as tuple member `._1`.** | `[RNG-4]`, `[TYP-8]`, `[EFF-10]` | **open** | ADR-120: implemented and correctness validated with opaque immutable originals, independent guard/clone verification and unconditional pre-codegen checking. Original checked fallback, spans, effects and signed starts remain. Str projection is corrected to `.ptr`/`.len` with native length type. Eighteen MIR tests and preserved RED controls, historical complete suites and 54 current native cases support correctness. Performance completion is still blocked by measured removable short-input guard overhead (D-498), rather than assumed safety cost. |
| D-387 | **A module-interface cache file a crash left damaged stopped every later build with `internal compiler error: [LT-40] cannot load interface cache: ... invalid stored module interface: not an Ember module-interface artifact`.** Found 2026-09-29 after the owner's laptop crashed mid-run: two records in benchmark build directories held bytes that were not a record, and the builds kept failing until the files were deleted by hand. The compiler computes every record fresh and uses the stored one only to check it against, and bytes that do not decode are damage (the encoder and decoder are round-tripped on every compilation), not compiler metadata that disagrees. | `[LT-40]` | **fixed** | A stored record that does not decode is replaced by the fresh one, as a superseded schema already was; one that decodes and disagrees is still the hard error it was. The now unused `StoredArtifact` error is gone. Test: `ember_build` `a_damaged_record_is_replaced_by_the_fresh_one` (zeros, and a record cut in half); break-tested. |
| D-388 | **A program with a drop flag ran a drop twice on Linux (`free(): double free detected`, `TYP-17/accept_compiler_provided_capabilities_meet_bounds`, CI gcc, 5f1de49).** ADR-075's clearing of unread temporaries, run after a fold, asked `stmt_reads` what each statement reads, and it did not count a drop's flag: the flag's `= true`/`= false` assignments looked unread and went, so the drop tested a flag never set. Windows' allocators did not report the second free. | `[OWN-3]`, `[DRP-2]` | **fixed** | `stmt_reads` counts a drop's flag as read, which every user of it (the temporaries cleared after a fold, copy propagation, strength reduction's dead temporaries) now sees. The emitted C of the failing program sets both flags again. |
| D-389 | **A bound's binding could not name another type parameter's associated type, nor an interface's method be bounded by its own interface with a binding.** `fn f[I: Iterator, J: Iterator[Item = I.Item]]` reported "`I` has no associated type `Item`" (the bounds were read one parameter at a time, before `I.Item` had its hidden parameter); inside `interface Walk`, `fn m[W: Walk[Step = Step]]` overflowed the compiler's stack (the bound, read before `Walk` was registered, named a no-argument copy `Walk[]` that was then made again and again); and a default method's binding kept `Self.Item` instead of the implementing type's ("`J`'s bound needs `Self.Item`"). Found building `[STD-19]`'s `chain` (ADR-072 had recorded it as blocked). | `[GRM-8c]`, `[TYP-17]`, `[STD-19]` | **fixed** | `declare_generics_from` reads every parameter's interfaces first, gives each `T.Name` the bounds mention its hidden parameter, then reads the bindings; `interface_instance_name` returns a no-argument interface itself; `register_instantiated_default` fills `Self`'s associated types into a bound's bindings as it already did into parameters and callable bounds. Tests: `TYP-17/accept_a_binding_names_another_parameters_associated_type` and `STD-19/accept_chain_gives_one_iterator_then_the_other`; each of the three fixes undone makes one or both fail (break-tested one at a time). |
| D-390 | **An interface with type parameters lost its defaults and its methods' own parameters.** With `interface Pair[T]` and `P implements Pair[int]`: a default body was never given to `P` ("`P` implements `Pair_i64` but does not define `again`", at 2a75810 too: the defaults pass matched implementations to the interface by name, and `P` implements the instance `Pair_i64`); a default with type parameters of its own could not be called ("cannot take `P` where it expects `U`") and a type's own such method was "does not match the signature required" (the instance kept the method's parameters numbered after the interface's, which it no longer has); and a method bounded by the same instance (`same[U: Pair[T]]`) overflowed the compiler's stack (making `Pair[int]` made its methods' signatures, which asked for `Pair[int]` again). Found 2026-09-30 while break-testing D-389. | `[TYP-17]`, `[IFC-2]` | **fixed** | `register_defaults` gives a type implementing an instance that instance's defaults through `register_instantiated_default`, and their bodies are checked with the interface's parameters bound to the instance's arguments (`interface_instance_bindings`, on `PendingDefaultMethod` and `MethodSource`); `instantiate_interface` renumbers a method's own parameters to come first, as a generic type's methods' are, and enters the instance before making its methods so a second request is answered by name. Test: `TYP-17/accept_a_generic_interfaces_defaults_and_its_methods_own_parameters` (a default naming `T`, a default with its own parameter, a defaulted and a written method bounded by the same instance); each of the four fixes undone makes it fail with its own error (break-tested one at a time). |
| D-391 | **A module-interface cache left by an earlier build of the compiler stopped a later build with an internal error.** 2026-09-30, after a change to the type checker (D-390) that altered `std.collections`' interface summary: the next `ember run` into the same output folder (`target/debug/…` under the working directory) failed with `internal compiler error: [LT-40] cannot load interface cache: cached callable-region metadata disagrees with the current verified summary for `std.collections``. `compiler_identity()` names the executable's size and modification time so that a different build is a different cache key, so the old record should not have been compared. The same program built into a fresh `--out-dir` worked, and built again into that folder worked (the summary is the same from run to run). CI is not affected (it starts with no cache). | `[LT-40]` | **open** | Not investigated yet; the owner asked for it to be looked into later (2026-09-30). First questions: whether the identity reaches the record's key or only its contents (`cache_key`, `prepare_interface_cache` in `compiler/ember_build/src/interface.rs`), and whether cargo's hard-linked `target/release/ember.exe` keeps an old modification time. Probed 2026-10-04 (autopilot): seven builds of four programs (a sort, a map, string splitting, arithmetic) into one shared output folder, in turn, all worked, so a cached summary does not depend on which program wrote it; what is left is a compiler change that keeps the cache key, which needs two builds of the compiler to test. |
| D-392 | **A float range type could hold NaN.** With `type Unit = f32 in 0.0 ..= 1.0`, `if x < 0.0: … else: if x > 1.0: … else: return x` returned `x` as a `Unit` with no check, and `as_unit(0.0 / 0.0)` printed `nan`. The checker takes a float fact from a comparison's false arm (`x >= 0.0` from `not (x < 0.0)`), which NaN satisfies, since every comparison with NaN is false. `[RNG-4a]` (since 0.9.9_Hardened_1): "For floats, a range fact comes only from the true arm of a comparison and records whether NaN is excluded; a fact that does not exclude NaN never removes a construction check." The checker also derives float facts from arithmetic (`r * 0.5` of a `Roughness` is in `0.0 ..= 0.5`, D-018, `RNG-4/accept_a_derived_range_needs_no_check.em`), which the 0.9.9 rule does not allow; the older rule it was built to (quoted in `range_of`) did. Found 2026-09-30 building ODR-090's `@fastmath` facts. Present before the 0.9.9 work (D-150 built the false-arm facts). A one-sided fact also took the type's largest finite value as its other end, so `if x >= 0.0: return x` made a `f64 in 0.0 ..= 1.7976931348623157e308` of infinity. | `[RNG-4a]`, `[RNG-4]`, `[RNG-9]`, `[RNG-10]` | **fixed** | `refine_ranges_from_condition` takes a float fact from the true arm only; a float's unknown range has infinite ends, so one comparison leaves an end open and `fits_repr` (both ends finite) refuses it; `range_of` takes no float fact from `Binary`, `Unary` or the `min`/`max`/`clamp` family (a range value's own range and a constant stay). This supersedes the float halves of D-018, D-142, D-143 and D-150, built to the older rule: their tests keep their integer cases, and the float ones are `RNG-4a/reject_a_float_fact_not_from_a_true_comparison` (six `E2215`, each accepted before; `in_unit(0.0 / 0.0)` printed `nan` and the infinity case `inf`). `RNG-4a/accept_a_float_fact_comes_from_a_true_comparison` shows what stays. Break-tested: with the old facts back, all six reject cases compile. No spec change and no ADR: the rule says it. |
| D-393 | **A class's field default saw the constructing function's locals.** With `class Acc: k: int = scale * 2` and `fn main(): scale = 21; a = Acc()`, the program printed 42: a class default was checked where the object was built, in the caller's scope, module and overflow policy, where a struct's is read as its declaration reads (`check_field_default`, D-267). A mistake in one was also reported once per construction. Found 2026-09-30 by the review of ADR-085, following a class default that was not strict inside `@fastmath`. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[STR-2]`, `[CLS-3]`, `[DIA-14]` | **fixed** | Both class constructions (a user `init`'s and the memberwise one) check each default with `check_field_default` and the declaring class's module (a base's field, its base's), with a generic class's parameters bound as a generic struct's are, and report a mistake once. `STR-2/reject_a_class_default_does_not_see_the_constructors_locals` (two constructions, one `E1010`) compiled and printed 42 before. |
| D-394 | **A child interface could not name its parent's associated type when the parent was collected after it.** `interface Backwards: Iterator: fn next_back(mut self) -> Option[Item]` in a program was `E1010` "cannot find type `Item`", and so was `interface Weighed: Measured` naming `Measured`'s `Unit` with `Measured` declared later in the file. The parents' associated types are added from the interfaces already collected (`interface_assoc`), and interfaces were collected importers first, so std's `Iterator` came after every user interface. Found 2026-10-01 writing ODR-091's `DoubleEndedIterator`. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[IFC-3]`, `[IFC-4]` | **fixed** | `collect_interfaces` runs dependencies first, as the recipe passes do, and within a module collects a parent before its children (in rounds; a cycle is collected as written, where it is reported). `IFC-3/accept_a_child_interface_names_its_parents_associated_type` (both cases) failed before. |
| D-395 | **An untyped literal on a shift's left took the shift amount's type.** With `z: u8 = 9`, `1 << z` panicked (`integer overflow in `shift``: the `1` was a `u8`, shifted by 9) and `1024 >> z` was `E2010` ("the literal `1024` does not fit in `u8`"), where `[TYP-10]` gives the amount a type of its own and the literal the type of its use, else `int`. The operator rule that an untyped literal adopts the other side's type ran for shifts too. Found 2026-10-01 probing `[TYP-10]`'s audit row. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[TYP-10]`, `[LEX-16]` | **fixed** | In `synth_binary` a shift's untyped left operand no longer adopts the amount's type: it stays untyped, and so does the shift, which `adopt_literal` then gives the type of its use (a new arm adopts only the left side); an untyped amount that does not fit the shifted type is an `int`. `TYP-10/accept_a_shifted_literal_takes_its_type_from_its_use` panicked and failed to compile before. |
| D-396 | **Overlapping implementations were found only where an instance met both, and `E2041` named one.** `extend[T: Copy] Wrap[T] implements First` with `extend[U: Clone] Wrap[U] implements First`, `extend[T] Pair[T, int]` with `extend[U] Pair[str, U]`, and `extend[T] Wrap[T]` with `extend Wrap[int]` of one interface each compiled while no program made an instance both applied to; one that did was reported at that use, once per instance (two `E2041` for one overlap, `[DIA-14]`), and the concrete-against-generic pair reached C as a function defined twice when the diagnostics were skipped. `[TYP-19]` makes the overlap itself `E2041` and `[TYP-20]` has the error name both; which bounds separate two was not said (ODR-092). Found 2026-10-01 probing `[TYP-20]`'s audit row. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[TYP-19]`, `[TYP-20]`, `[DIA-14]` | **fixed** | ODR-092 (Hardened_47) and ADR-088: one pass after collection unifies every pair of implementations of one interface, parameters kept apart, and checks the bounds of what became a type written out in full; an overlap is one `E2041` at the later implementation with the earlier labelled. A collision met at an instance is left to that pass when a generic recipe is in it. `TYP-19/reject_overlapping_implementations_are_found_where_declared` (five overlaps; each compiled before), `TYP-19/accept_implementations_that_cannot_meet_are_both_kept`, `TYP-20/reject_two_implementations_of_one_interface_name_both` (the label was missing). |
| D-397 | **`E2040` named a generic interface by its instance's internal name.** An implementation of `Conv[int]` whose method did not match was "does not match the signature required by `Conv_i64`", and `Conv[Array[T]]` was `Conv_Array_T0_6`. `check_implementation_of` printed the instance's symbol. Found 2026-10-01 with D-396. | `[DIA-2]`, `[IFC-1]` | **fixed** | The four `E2040` messages there, and the parent a bound requires, print the interface as written (`interface_shown`: `Conv[i64]`). Seen in the D-396 probes; no test pins the old text. |
| D-398 | **Negating a range value kept the range type.** With `type Unit = f64 in 0.0 ..= 1.0` and `type Level = i32 in 0 ..= 10`, `u: Unit = -a` and `m: Level = -l` compiled and held -0.25 and -3: values outside their ranges, which `[RNG-9]` makes invalid (undefined behaviour) and `[RNG-10]` lets safe code make only by a construction. `[RNG-5]`: arithmetic on a range value yields its representation; unary `-` (and `~`) took the operand's type. Found 2026-10-01 probing `[RNG-5a1]`. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[RNG-5]`, `[RNG-9]`, `[RNG-10]` | **fixed** | The operand is erased to its representation (`erase_operand`) before `-` or `~`, so `-a` is an `f64` and an integer one is checked as `0 - x`. `RNG-5/reject_negating_a_range_value_gives_its_representation` (two `E2215`; both compiled and printed -0.25 and -3 before) and `RNG-5/accept_negating_a_range_value_gives_its_representation`. |
| D-399 | **`E2214`'s help suggested `x as f32`, which does not compile.** For `Roughness + Metallic` the help read "convert one side explicitly: `x as f32`", and `r as f32` of a range value is `E2020` (`[TYP-6]` lists no range type; a range value becomes its representation at a coercion site, `[RNG-2]`). Found 2026-10-01 with D-398. | `[RNG-2]`, `[RNG-5]`, `[DIA-15]` | **fixed** | The help names a coercion: "convert one side to `f32` at a place of that type first, as `x: f32 = a` does". `RNG-5/reject_two_distinct_range_types` asserts it. |
| D-400 | **A generic extension implementing an instance of a generic interface gave its methods to the type.** `extend[T: Copy] Wrap[T] implements Conv[Array[T]]` and `extend[U] Wrap[U] implements Conv[bool]` were `E1030` (`Wrap[T]` already has `conv`) and `E2040` for each, where the same two blocks on a concrete type work (D-313), and so did `Add[int]` with `Add[Wrap[T]]`. A generic extension's methods took the first interface written without arguments as theirs, so an instance's methods were the type's own. Found 2026-10-01 with D-396. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[TYP-21]`, `[TYP-16]`, `[IFC-1]` | **fixed** | Applying a generic extension resolves the block's interfaces as the instance has them (`block_interfaces_of`, with the extension's bindings) and gives each method to the interface that declares it, else the first, as a concrete block does. `TYP-21/accept_a_generic_type_implements_two_instances_of_an_interface` (a bound on each instance, `w + 1` and `w + w`; `E1030` and `E2040` before). |
| D-401 | **`Weak.empty()` where the type is known was `E1010`.** `[WK-11]`: "`Weak[O].empty()` (or `Weak.empty()` where the type is known)"; the bare form was "cannot find `Weak`" in a typed local, a return, a field default and an argument. Found 2026-10-01 probing `[WK-11]`'s audit row. | `[WK-11]` | **fixed** | `Weak.empty()` is `Weak[O].empty()` of the `Weak[O]` expected; with none it is `E2060`, cannot infer which `Weak` this is, with the help to name it. `WK-11/accept_weak_empty_takes_the_type_it_is_used_as` (all four places; `E1010` before), `WK-11/reject_weak_empty_with_no_type_to_take`. |
| D-402 | **A call two instances of one generic interface both fit took the last one, and no call could name an instance.** With `W: Conv[Array[int]]` and `W: Conv[bool]`, `a: Array[int] = w.conv()` was a type mismatch against whichever `conv` was registered last, and `Conv[bool].conv(w)`, `[TYP-24]`'s `I.m(recv, …)` for an instance, was `E1010`. D-313 made sibling instances no rivals because their arguments choose (`m * v`, `m * m`); arguments that fit both chose nothing and fell to the last. Found 2026-10-01 with D-400. | `[TYP-24]` | **fixed** | Arguments that fit two instances are `E2070` naming both, with the help `Conv[bool].conv(…)`; a generic interface with its arguments on the left of a call names that instance. Candidates are compared in declaration order (they came from a map). `TYP-24/reject_a_call_two_instances_both_fit`, `TYP-24/accept_an_instance_of_a_generic_interface_names_the_call` (`E1010` before). |
| D-403 | **A bound naming no interface was taken as met by every type.** `struct S[T: Nope]` and `fn g[T: Hashr + Eq]` compiled and ran; a misspelt bound, or an interface never imported (`H: Hasher` without the import, `Hasher` not being a prelude name), bounded nothing. A type's bounds are read before the interfaces are collected, so the name was stored unchecked, and `implements` answers an interface nothing declares with yes. Found 2026-10-01 probing `[HASH-3]`. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[TYP-17]`, `[MOD-5]` | **fixed** | A bound whose name is no declared interface is kept aside and reported once every interface is collected (`E1010`, cannot find interface); `Display`, `Debug`, `Copy` and `Zeroable`, which the table answers without a declaration, are exempt. `TYP-17/reject_a_bound_naming_no_interface` (both compiled before). It exposed D-404. |
| D-404 | **`EMBER_STD` naming the `std` package loaded no `std`, silently.** The variable was taken as the sources' directory; `std/` (not `std/src/`) exists, holds no module, and every `std` import was then an import of a module not written yet, which the driver deliberately accepts. Three multi-module tests (`visible_member_declarations_preserve_generic_owner_interface_identity` and two others) set it so and had run with no `std` since they were written, passing because of D-403. The bisection note had the same trap. Found 2026-10-01 when D-403's fix failed them. | `[TOOL-1]`, `[MOD-5]` | **fixed** | `std_root` takes `<path>/src` when it holds `core`'s source, else the path as before. The three tests now load `std` and pass. |
| D-405 | **`DefaultHasher` gave byte strings differing only in trailing zero bytes one hash.** `write_bytes` packs bytes eight to a word and pads the last one to seven with zeros, so `"a"` and `"a\0"` (both then ended by `str`'s `0xff`) were one message: a collision under any key, which a keyed hasher built the same way (`RandomState`) could not have prevented. Found 2026-10-01 building `RandomState`. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[HASH-1]`, `[HASH-2]` | **fixed** | The tail word carries its byte count in its top byte (`tail_word`), in both hashers. `DefaultHasher`'s values change, which `[HASH-2]` allows between versions. `HASH-2/accept_bytes_differing_in_their_tail_hash_apart` (four pairs, each equal before). |
| D-406 | **A callable-type bound was dropped.** `fn apply[F: fn(int) -> int](f: F, x: int)` compiled with `F` bounded by nothing, so `f(x)` was `E2020` ("`f` has type `ref F`, which cannot be called"), and so was a field of a `struct Holder[F: fn(int) -> int]`; any bound that was neither an interface nor an instance of a generic one was dropped without a word. `[CLO-14]`: "a parameter so bounded is called like a function". Found 2026-10-01 writing `[STD-19]`'s `map`. | `[CLO-14]`, `[CLO-11]`, `[TYP-17]` | **fixed** | `declare_generics_from` gives the parameter the callable bound the parameter form gives its implicit one (`GenericParam.callable`), read once every parameter and projection is declared (`F: fn(I.Item) -> R`); a bound that is neither is `E2020`. `CLO-14/accept_an_explicit_callable_bound_is_called_like_a_function` (`E2020` before), `CLO-14/reject_a_bound_that_is_no_interface_or_callable`. |
| D-407 | **Associated types in generic std code: six limits that blocked `flat_map`, `flatten`, `peekable`, `collect` and `join`.** (1) An interface default method returning a generic type over its own associated type keeps `Self.Item` in the implementation's instance: with `fn wrapped(owned self) -> Wrap[Self, Item]` and `extend[T, S: Source[Item = T]] Wrap[S, T]: fn first(mut self) -> T: return self.s.get()`, `first` is `E2020` expected `Self.Item`, found `i64`: comparing the implementation's signature substituted `Self` before `Item` and made `Wrap[Ints, Self.Item]`, whose extension bodies were checked. **Fixed 2026-10-01**: an instance over an associated type not yet read (`mentions_assoc`) is as opaque as one over a parameter, signatures only; `IFC-4/accept_a_default_returns_a_type_over_its_item` failed before. (2) A generic struct's field cannot name a projection of its own parameter (`current: Option[U.Iter]` with `U: IntoIterator` is `E2040`): generic structs are collected before interfaces. (3) An interface method's signature cannot name the associated type of an interface collected after it (`Iterator.flat_map`'s `U.Iter`, `IntoIterator` coming later). (4) A projection of an associated type (`Item.Iter`) is `E1010`. (5) `where Item: Display` does not let the body format an `Item`, and `C: FromIterator[Item]` in a default method does not match `Self.Item`. (6) A reference into a `Copy` payload held in a place cannot be written: `[GRM-13]` binds it by value, so `Peekable.peek(mut self) -> Option[ref T]` (and `Option.as_ref`) cannot be written in Ember for a `Copy` `T` (`E3060`). Found 2026-10-01 writing the rest of `[STD-19]`. | `[IFC-4]`, `[GRM-8c]`, `[STD-19]` | **fixed** | Repro for (1): the program above. A general fix for (2) is a hidden parameter per projection in a generic type's recipe, bound from the arguments at instantiation (as D-380 does for extensions); for (3) collecting every interface's associated types before any method signature. Wanted for the owner's review before building: it changes how generic types are instantiated. **Owner's decisions (2026-10-01):** build the general fix for (2), (3) and (4) as proposed (interfaces' associated types read before generic structs; a hidden parameter per projection in a generic struct, filled from the arguments at instantiation; chained projections); for (6), `Option.as_ref` (and `as_mut`) are compiler-provided and `Peekable.peek` is written in Ember over them (not a by-reference pattern, not a built-in `peek`, not a change to `[GRM-13]`). (5) needs no ruling. **Built 2026-10-01 (ADR-095): (2), (3), (4) and (6); then (5).** (2) A generic struct's field or method signature naming `U.Iter` gets a hidden parameter after the written ones (`declare_projections` in `collect_generic_struct`), filled from the arguments where an instance is made (`with_projections`), the instance named by them too; a signature or an extension naming `Each[U]` takes the same hidden parameters (`induced_projections`, `declare_target_projections`). (3) Each interface's associated types are noted before any generic type is collected (`noted_assoc`), so a projection of an interface collected later is declared, its bounds closed once interfaces are (`close_projection_bounds`); a method's own projections are declared in its signature and filled at its call. (4) Chains (`S.Item.Iter`) in signatures, bodies and hidden parameters. Found on the way: an associated type's bound writing a binding (`type Iter: Iterator[Item = Item]`) was dropped, so a generic body could not call `next` on `u.into_iter()`; the bound and its binding are kept and carried onto hidden parameters. (6) `Option.as_ref`/`as_mut` are compiler-provided (a by-reference binding of the payload where it is). `IFC-4/accept_a_generic_struct_field_names_a_projection`, `IFC-4/accept_a_generic_body_iterates_through_into_iterator`, `IFC-4/accept_a_projection_of_a_projection`, `IFC-4/accept_a_method_names_a_projection_of_its_own_parameter`, `ERR-4/accept_option_as_ref_and_as_mut`; twelve breaks, each failing its test. (5) A default method's `where` clause bounds its parameters and the interface's associated types while its body is checked for each implementing type (`apply_where_bounds`), so `where Item: Display` lets it format an `Item`. A bound of a default method's own parameter over the interface's associated types (`C: Build[Item]`) is read for the implementing type twice: in the signature its calls use (`Build[int]`, `bound_resolved_for`), and where an implementation's method is compared with the interface's (`bound_for_owner`; it was `E2040`). Found on the way: a generic method's parameter that only its result names (`C`) was `E2060` with the result's type written at the call (`b: Bag = s.gather()`); the type expected of a method call is now a hint for what its arguments leave open, as at a function's call (it reaches only that call). `IFC-4/accept_a_default_method_bounds_an_associated_type_in_its_where_clause` and `IFC-4/accept_a_default_method_bounded_over_its_associated_type`; five breaks, each failing its test. |
| D-408 | **A generic view parameter could not infer from an array argument.** With `fn pick[T: Copy](xs: Span[T]) -> T`, `pick(ys)` for `ys: Array[int]` was `E2060` (cannot tell what `T` is), though the argument becomes a `Span[int]` at the call (`[TYP-5]` rule 6) and `[TYP-18]` infers generic arguments from a call's arguments; so for `MutSpan[T]` and a `[T; N]`. Found 2026-10-01 probing `[FN-5]`. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[TYP-18]`, `[TYP-5]` | **fixed** | `unify_generic_argument` reads an `Array[T]` or `[T; N]` argument (through a reference) as its element type for a view parameter, as it reads a `T` for a `ref T` (rule 7). `TYP-18/accept_a_view_parameter_infers_from_an_array` (`E2060` before). |
| D-409 | **A generic implementation whose interface did not resolve in the overlap pass was dropped, so the overlap reached C as a function defined twice.** `interface Combine[R = Self]` with `extend[T] Array[T] implements Combine` and `extend Array[int] implements Combine`, or `extend[I: Iterator] Wrap[I] implements Conv[I.Item]` beside `extend[U] Wrap[U] implements Conv[int]`: `ember check` printed nothing and the C defined `em_Array_i64_combine` twice. `recipe_interface` gave up on any report (a built-in generic has no owner to resolve `Self` against; an associated-type argument had no projection parameters), the record was skipped, and ADR-088 had removed the instance-time `E2041` for those pairs. Found by the 2026-10-01 agent review (`docs/REVIEW-2026-10-01.md`, G1-1). | `[TYP-19]`, `[TYP-20]` | **fixed** | The pass reads a recipe's interface as the recipe's methods are read: a built-in generic's against its instance over its own parameters (`builtin_generic_instance`, so `Self` resolves), with the recipe's parameters, projections and bounds in scope (`recipe_interface`), so `Conv[I.Item]` resolves to the hidden `I.Item`. And a collision at an instance the pass did not report is `E2041` there ("`Array[i64]` gets `Combine[Array[i64]]` from two implementations"): `collect_implements` notes each (`note_instance_collision`), checked against the pairs the pass reported once it has run (`report_instance_collisions`). `TYP-19/reject_overlaps_whose_interface_names_self_or_an_associated_type` (`Self` on `Array`, `Conv[I.Item]`, and `Tag[I.Item]` named only by the `implements`; no error before, the C defining each method twice). Break-tested: without the built-in owner the instance-time report fires instead; without it too, nothing is reported; without the recipe's scope (both projection routes), the instance report fires. |
| D-410 | **The overlap pass's bound check was incomplete.** (a) A bound with generic interface arguments, or with an associated-type equality, counted as unknown, so pairs it keeps apart were `E2041` (`extend[T, U: Conv[T]] Pair[T, U]` against `Pair[int, Gadget]`, Gadget implementing only `Conv[bool]`; ODR-042's `Index[Q: AsKey[K]]` shape). (b) A bound met only through a blanket implementation counted as unmet, so a real overlap was missed. (c) Only a parameter's top-level binding was substituted, so a type unification made concrete (`Wrap[V]` with `V = int`) counted as open. Found by the 2026-10-01 agent review (`docs/REVIEW-2026-10-01.md`, G1-2, G1-3, G1-8). | `[TYP-19]`, ODR-092 | **fixed** | `known_bounds_hold` closes each bounded type and each bound's interface arguments through every binding the unification made (`closed_on_side`, `closed_instance`, which instantiates the closed instance so `implements` knows it), answers the bound as a call does (`implements`, or a blanket implementation through `matching_blanket`, which records nothing), and compares the bound's associated-type bindings through `project`. `TYP-19/accept_implementations_their_bounds_keep_apart` (the three pairs, `E2041` each before) and `TYP-19/reject_a_bound_met_through_a_blanket_overlaps` (no `E2041` before). Break-tested: without the closed instance being instantiated, `Conv[int]` counted as met (an interface `implements` does not know answers yes); without the blanket answer, the overlap is missed; recording the blanket's instance there (`implements_or_blanket`) made `check_implementations` reject its method over the free `Q` (`E2040`). |
| D-411 | **A diagnostic's labels in another file were not rendered.** `ember_diag` kept only labels in the primary span's file, so `E2041` across modules named one implementation, and a user's `extend int implements Default` pointed only into `std/src/core.em`, never at the user's line. `--json` had both. Found by the 2026-10-01 agent review (`docs/REVIEW-2026-10-01.md`, G1-4, G1-N2). | `[TYP-20]`, `[DIA-14]` | **fixed** | `ember_diag`'s renderer shows a label in another file under that file's own `:::` header after the primary's group (ADR-094); `ember_diag` test `a_label_in_another_file_has_its_own_header`. Break-tested. The row's second half, pointing a program's collision with std at the program's line, was built in the second batch and removed in the third: ODR-097 makes the program's implementation of a standard interface for a standard type `E2121` before any collision (D-413), and nothing else a program writes can meet one of std's. |
| D-412 | **Instance names were not injective: `Wrap[*int]` and `Wrap[int]` were one type.** `generic_instance_name` joined `type_stem`s, which drop pointer, tuple and array structure (`*i64` is `i64`; `Pair[(int, int), int]` and `Pair[int, (int, int)]` are both `Pair_i64_i64_i64`), so a second instantiation took the first's fields (`expected bool, found *i64`) and the concrete `E2041` fired for two different types. Older than 0.9.9's work. Found by the 2026-10-01 agent review (`docs/REVIEW-2026-10-01.md`, G1-5). | `[TYP-16]` | **fixed** | One way to name an instance (`unique_instance_name`, ADR-092), used by the generic recipes, the interface instances and every compiler-known wrapper (`Option`, `Result`, `Box`, `Shared`, `Weak`, `Cell`, `RefCell`, `Ref`/`RefMut`, `MaybeUninit`, `UnsafeCell`): the arguments spelled with their structure (`instance_stem`: `ptr_i64`, `tup2_i64_i64`, `arr3_i64`, `fn1_i64_i64`; scalars, declared types, `Array_T` and `ref_T` as before), and the name recorded against what it names, a taken spelling (a declared `struct Wrap_i64` beside `Wrap[int]`) getting a number. `TYP-16/accept_instances_over_pointers_and_tuples_are_distinct_types` (`expected i64, found *i64` and `i64 is not a tuple` before; asserts `em_Wrap_ptr_i64`, `em_Pair_tup2_i64_i64_i64`, `em_Pair_i64_tup2_i64_i64` in the C). Break-tested: without the numbering, the declared `Wrap_i64` takes `Wrap[int]`'s place; without both, every symptom returns. |
