Found 2026-10-01 writing ODR-094's `collect`, whose `C.from_iter(self)` is such a call. | `[TYP-17]`, `[IFC-4]`, `[TYP-19]` | **fixed** | Two parts (ADR-096). An interface instance over the caller's parameters numbers each method's own parameters after those its arguments mention and records them as the method's prefix (`instantiate_interface`, D-280's rule for a generic type's methods); `implementation_signature_matches` and `register_instantiated_default` follow that prefix, and a method's own projection moves with its base. A call through a bound (`synth_bound_method`, `synth_bound_associated`) is checked against `bound_call_signature`: `Self` is the parameter, its associated types the parameter's through the bound, and the method's own parameters come after every one of the caller's, which the call solves to themselves. Tests: `IFC-4/accept_a_generic_method_through_a_bound_reads_self_as_the_parameter` (1), `IFC-4/accept_an_instance_over_the_callers_parameter_keeps_the_methods_own_apart` (2), `IFC-4/accept_an_implementation_of_a_generic_method_naming_the_extensions_parameter` (3); without the bound reading the first two fail, without the instance's numbering the last two. The full suite passes with MSVC and clang. |
| D-438 | **A type parameter fixed only by another parameter's binding is not inferred.** With `fn build[T, C: Gather[T], I: Iterator[Item = T]](owned it: I) -> C`, `b: Bag = build((1..4).iter())` is `E2060`, cannot tell what `T` is, although `I` is solved as the range's iterator, whose `Item` is `int`, and `I`'s bound says `Item = T`. `[TYP-18]`: generic arguments are inferred from the arguments of a call; Rust infers through such a binding. Found 2026-10-01 writing D-437's tests. | `[TYP-18]`, `[TYP-17]` | **fixed** | `solve_from_bindings`: once a parameter is solved, each binding of its bounds unifies the binding's value with the solved type's associated type (through the bound's instance as solved), until nothing more is solved; at a function's call, at a method's (after the expected type's hint), and where an extension is matched to a type (`generic_extension_bindings_solved`), so `extend[T: Eq, I: Iterator[Item = T]] Peekable[I]` applies. `TYP-18/accept_a_parameter_a_binding_fixes_is_inferred` (both calls were `E2060`, and the extension's method `E1010`). |
| D-439 | **A default method's bound over the interface's associated types was not read for a generic type's instance.** D-407 (5) read `C: FromIterator[Item]` for a concrete type's copy of `collect` (`resolve_signature_assoc`) but not for an instance's (`register_instantiated_default`), so `xs.iter().copied().collect[Array[int]]()` was `E2040`, "`Array[i64]` does not implement `FromIterator[Self.Item]`". Found 2026-10-01 building ODR-094. | `[IFC-4]`, `[TYP-17]` | **fixed** | The instance's copy reads each bound for the type (`bound_read_for`: `Self` the type, its associated types the type's). `STD-19/accept_collect_builds_any_from_iterator_target` (every `collect` after `copied` failed without it). |
| D-440 | **Two instances of one generic interface giving an associated function of one name clashed.** With `extend S implements Make[int]` and `extend S implements Make[bool]`, each with `fn make(x) -> S`, the second replaced the first: the first's body was checked against the second's signature (`E2040` "does not match"), a call took the second whatever its arguments, and the C defined `em_S_make` twice. D-313 had kept methods apart, not associated functions. `String` implements `FromIterator[char]` and `FromIterator[String]`. Found 2026-10-01 building ODR-094. | `[TYP-24]`, `[TYP-21]` | **fixed** | An instance's associated function is kept by instance (`interface_associated`), read by its block's body check, the implementation check and the symbol; a call chooses by its arguments as a method does (`choose_instance_associated`, ODR-096), and a generic candidate fits when the arguments solve it and meet its bounds and their bindings (`generic_candidate_fits`), which methods now get too. `TYP-24/accept_sibling_instances_associated_functions_chosen_by_arguments`. |
| D-441 | **A struct's bounds were asked of an associated type not yet read.** A default method's signature `fn flatten(owned self) -> Flatten[Self, Item] where Item: IntoIterator` was `E2040`, "`Self.Item` does not implement `IntoIterator`", although the `where` clause says it does: the struct's bound was checked against `Self.Item` itself. Found 2026-10-01 building `flatten`. | `[TYP-17]`, `[IFC-4]` | **fixed** | As for an argument that is a type parameter, a struct's bounds wait while an argument names an associated type not yet read (`mentions_assoc`): they are met for each type the default is read for. `STD-19/accept_flat_map_flatten_and_join` (`flatten`'s signature is `E2040` without it); `IFC-4/accept_a_default_over_its_item_is_checked_for_each_type`. |
| D-442 | **A default method returning a type over `Self` with a projection did not match its own body.** With `struct P[I: Src]: saved: Option[I.Item]` and `fn wrap(owned self) -> P[Self]: return P(self, None)`, the default was `E2020`, "expected `P[Self]`, found `P[Self]`": the opaque check read the signature before `Self` was a parameter of the body (its hidden `Self.Item` an error) and the body after; `FlatMap[Self, U, F]`'s hidden `U.Iter` differed the same way in an instance's copy, read with none of the method's parameters in scope. Found 2026-10-01 building `peekable` and `flat_map`. | `[IFC-4]`, `[STD-19]` | **fixed** | The opaque check reads the signature once `Self` and the `where` clause are in scope, as the body is; an instance's copy is read with the method's own parameters in scope. `IFC-4/accept_a_default_returns_a_type_over_self_with_a_projection`, `STD-19/accept_peekable_peeks_and_takes_conditionally`, `STD-19/accept_flat_map_flatten_and_join`. |
| D-443 | **A default whose `where` clause a type missed was given to it anyway, and a default's copy was compared with its own declaration.** `Iterator.flatten` (`where Item: IntoIterator`) on a program's iterator of numbers was `E2040` at the library's line, and its `flat_map` copy "does not match the signature required by `Iterator`". Found 2026-10-01 building ODR-094. | `[IFC-4]`, `[TYP-17]` | **fixed** | A default whose `where` clause on the interface's associated types the type misses is not the type's (`where_clauses_hold`), as an extension whose bound it misses gives it nothing, and implementing the interface does not ask for it (`defaults_not_met`); a default's copy is its declaration read for the type, so it is not compared with it (`default_copies`). `IFC-4/accept_a_default_over_its_item_is_checked_for_each_type`, `IFC-4/reject_a_default_whose_where_clause_the_type_misses`. |
| D-444 | **A range was not `IntoIterator`.** `[CTL-1]` makes ranges iterable and consumes a value that is `IntoIterator`, but `(1..4).into_iter()` was `E1010` and `flat_map(fn(x: int) => 0..x)` `E2040`. Writing the implementation found the reason it was missing: an opaque instance's associated type's bound was asked while a parameter was open (`Range[T]`'s `Iter`, `RangeIter[T]`, is an `Iterator` by an extension bounded `T: Integer`). Found 2026-10-01 testing `flat_map`. | `[CTL-1]`, `[STD-19]`, `[IFC-4]` | **fixed** | `Range`, `RangeInclusive` and `RangeFrom` implement `IntoIterator` as their `iter()`; over parameters still open the associated type's bound is asked of each instance where they are known, as for every opaque instance (D-250). `CTL-1/accept_a_range_is_consumed_through_into_iter`, `STD-19/accept_flat_map_flatten_and_join`. |
| D-445 | **Printing a class handle passed C an incompatible pointer.** A printing helper took its value as `const T*`, which for a class handle (`struct X*`) is `const struct X**`; `&handle` is a `struct X**`, which C does not convert to it. gcc before 14 warns (no run sees it), gcc 14 and later reject it: `TYP-36/accept_a_class_handle_prints_its_class_and_address` and `accept_a_class_handle_hashes_by_identity` failed to build with gcc 15.2. Found 2026-10-01 running the conformance suite with gcc under WSL. | `[TYP-36]`, `[CG-C-1]` | **fixed** | The printing, equality and clone helpers take `T const*` (`struct X* const*` for a handle, the conversion C allows; the same type as before for anything else). The milestone `printing_a_class_handle_passes_a_pointer_c_accepts`; the conformance suite passes with gcc 15.2. |
| D-446 | **Wrapping arithmetic reached C as signed arithmetic, whose overflow C leaves undefined.** `@overflow(wrap)` (and `#! module overflow(wrap)`) lowers `+`, `-` and `*` to plain C operators on `int64_t`, `int32_t` and the rest, and a negation to `0 - x`; C may assume a signed operation never overflows. gcc 15 `-O2` did: a hash key `(i * 0x5851F42D4C957F2D) >> 8` computed inside a loop came out different from the same call elsewhere, so every lookup of the scrambled-key `Map` program missed (an overflow panic in release, a hang in shipping); MSVC and clang happened to keep the wrapped value. Found 2026-10-01 pricing `Map` against libstdc++ under WSL. | `[TYP-8]`, `[CG-C-1]` | **fixed** | A signed `+`, `-` or `*` that reaches C unchecked is done in the unsigned type of its width (`uint32_t` for 8 to 32 bits, so the promotion to `int` cannot overflow; `uint64_t`; `size_t`) and converted back: two's-complement wrapping, and the same value wherever nothing overflows. The conformance test `TYP-8/accept_wrapping_arithmetic_in_a_loop_is_never_undefined` (the loop, and the least value of `i32`, `int` and `i16` wrapping, in debug, release and shipping; it failed with gcc) and the milestone `wrapping_arithmetic_is_unsigned_in_c` (the C itself, so MSVC and clang catch it too; fails with the fix off). Measured: no benchmark row slower (gcc, all 39; MSVC and clang, the wrapping, list and checked-sum rows, against the 2026-09-30 run). |
| D-447 | **With MSVC, the `mut self` programs got slower since the README's 2026-09-30 run.** Calling a `mut self` method on list elements in a loop (one field, six fields, a method calling a function) measured 1.10x, 1.17x, 1.13x the C++ on 2026-09-30 and 1.20x to 1.30x on 2026-10-01 (21 runs, twice; the C++ twin 0.031 s then, 0.036 s now; Ember 0.034 s then, 0.044 s to 0.047 s now). Not the 2026-10-01 gcc work: with D-446 switched off and with the runtime of `fa40f25` each is slower still. Found running the README's benchmarks. | — (speed, no rule) | **fixed** | Not a commit: run to run, MSVC's build of the loop took 35 to 65 ms on one core, where the twin held 31 ms (one add instruction updated two objects in turn and the processor mispredicted which store each load read), and unpinned runs moved between fast and slow cores. ADR-103 writes the loop eight turns to a pass for MSVC: 0.82x, steady; the README's runs are held to the performance cores. `msvc_writes_small_checked_loops_several_turns_a_pass` fails without it. |
| D-448 | **A printing test generated C that gcc rejects, with one shape of std's `Map`.** (closed: no longer reproduces) While measuring ADR-100's map, a test copy of std whose position table was `Array[i32]` read through `(self.slots[at] as int)` (`scratchpad/wsl/map_variants.py`, variant `v3`) made `IFC-4/accept_a_projection_of_a_projection` and two other conformance tests fail to compile with gcc: `incompatible type for argument 1 of 'ember_print_str'`. Closed; something between 2026-10-02 and now changed the path, not found which. |
| D-449 | **A borrow of a `void` place gave C that does not compile.** `Rvalue::Ref` always emitted `&place`, but a `void` local is never declared in C, and `*p` of a `void*` is not C: `ref u` of a `void` local, hashing an `Option[void]` or an `Array[void]` (a `Set` or a `Map` of them borrows the `void` inside), and `x.hash(h)` on a `void` a `match` bound all failed in the C compiler (`'_3': undeclared identifier`, MSVC's C2100). Found by the second review (G7-1, `docs/REVIEW-2026-10-01b.md`). | `[TYP-36]`, `[TYP-1]`, `[CG-C-2]` | **fixed** | A borrow of a `void` place takes `ember_void_place()`, one byte in the runtime header that lives as long as the program (ADR-062: nothing is read through it; ODR-068: zero-sized values need not have distinct addresses). `TYP-36/accept_a_borrow_of_a_void_value_compiles` covers the review's seven probes' shapes; it fails without the fix. |
| D-450 | **`input()` returned a line that is not UTF-8 as a `String`.** `ember_input` pushed every byte of the line into the `String` without validating it, so a `String` could break `[TXT-1]`'s invariant, and the `str` operations that rely on it read past the end of the buffer (a truncated `E2 82` decoded as U+2085, its third byte from beyond the string). Found by the second review (G6-1). | `[STD-10]`, `[TXT-1]`, `[TXT-2]` | **fixed** | A line that is not UTF-8 panics, as end of input does: `input: the line is not valid UTF-8; `std.io.stdin().read_line()` returns it as a `Result` instead`. No ODR: `[TXT-2]` lets only a `_lossy` form substitute U+FFFD and makes every other conversion from bytes return a `Result`; `input` is neither, so such a line is a console failure, which `[STD-10]` makes fatal. The milestone `input_panics_on_a_line_that_is_not_utf8` pipes the bytes in (a conformance file cannot hold them) and checks that a valid line of several-byte characters reads whole; it fails without the check. |
| D-451 | **Every `mut self` method that returns a view or a `ref` of a field panicked when called.** The access a call's result carries to the caller (`[EXC-18]`) began at the start of the call's next block, before the call's own whole-object write (`[EXC-15]`) ended there, so it met that write: `v = b.sorted_view()` panicked with `read access to Bag.items while a write access to Bag.items is active`. Found by the second review (G5-3). | `[EXC-18]`, `[EXC-15]`, `[LT-1]` | **fixed** | A call result's carried access begins after the `EndAccess` run lowering puts first in the call's next block (`after_call_ends` in `borrows.rs`), for the `[EXC-18]` field accesses and the two `Shared` payload ones. `EXC-18/accept_a_mut_self_getter_returns_a_view_of_a_field` (a view getter and a `ref mut` getter) and `EXC-18/run_fail_a_view_from_a_mut_self_getter_holds_its_field` (the access still lasts to the view's last use); both fail without the fix. |
| D-452 | **A mutable view inside an `Option` or a tuple returned by a virtual or interface call carried only a read access.** `call_result_accesses` made the object's access a write only when the result itself was a `MutSpan` or a `ref mut`; an `Option[ref mut String]` got a read, so a view of the same field through another handle could begin, and the write through the `ref mut` freed the text it pointed into: a use-after-free in safe code. Found by the second review (G5-2). | `[EXC-18]`, `[EXC-2]` | **fixed** | The access is a write when any view reachable in the result's type lends mutably (`TypeTable::has_mutable_view`, the walk `is_view` makes). `EXC-18/run_fail_a_mutable_view_inside_an_option_from_an_interface_call_holds_a_write`; it fails without the fix. |
| D-453 | **A class's `drop` ran without its object's write access, so a view taken in `drop` could be freed under it.** `[EXC-15]` gives a `mut self` method (`drop(mut self)` is one, `[DRP-1]`) a write access to every field from entry to return, begun by its caller; the runtime calls `drop` and began none. A view of a field taken in `drop`, then a write to that field through another path (a plain `self` method), passed every check, and the view read freed memory; the whole-program check removal (`[EXC-3]`) had also removed that write's check, since nothing in the MIR held the object. Found by the second review (G5-1). | `[EXC-15]`, `[DRP-1]`, `[EXC-3]` | **fixed** | The drop adapter the runtime calls begins and ends the object's write around the whole destructor chain (the class's `drop` and its bases', `[CLS-6]`), located at the first `drop`; `remove_never_firing_checks_all` counts every class with a `drop` as holding its family's object write. `EXC-15/run_fail_a_drop_holds_its_objects_write` fails with either half removed; `EXC-15/accept_a_drop_calls_mut_self_methods_on_itself` checks that a legal `drop` still runs. |
| D-454 | **A `RefCell` in a class field could not be borrowed.** `borrow()` and `borrow_mut()` update the cell's counter and record where the borrow began through places under the class field, and the per-field access words made each of those stores a write access to the field while the guard's loan held the field's access (a write, for `borrow_mut`): every `borrow` of a `RefCell` class field panicked with an exclusivity violation. Found by the second review (G5-5). | `[CELL-5]`, `[CELL-6]`, `[EXC-18]` | **fixed** | Reaching inside a `RefCell` (its counter, where its last borrow began, or a guard's loan of its value) is a read of the class field that holds it; the cell's own counter rules what is borrowed inside it (`inside_a_cell` in `borrows.rs`). Replacing the whole cell is still a write. `CELL-5/accept_a_refcell_class_field_borrows`, `CELL-5/run_fail_a_refcell_class_field_counts_its_borrows` (the cell's own panic, through another handle) and `EXC-18/run_fail_replacing_a_refcell_field_while_its_guard_lives`; the first two fail without the fix. |
| D-455 | **A view of a class field made on only one path crashed the compiler.** The access a loan of a class field holds was begun where the loan was made and ended where the loan's region stops being live, and a region covers every path to the view's later uses, including paths where that loan was never made: `v = b.items[..] if c else b.log[..]`, a view assigned in one branch and used after it, an `Option` of a view made on one path, a `match` choosing views, and every `try_borrow` of a `RefCell` class field failed MIR verification (`receives incompatible dynamic-access states`, `ends dynamic access … but that access is not open`). Found by the second review (G5-4). | `[EXC-18]`, `[CELL-6]` | **fixed** | ADR-102: each access's placement is planned first and checked by a forward pass over the blocks; one begun on only some of the paths its region covers keeps a flag, cleared on entry and set as it begins, and each of its ends runs only where the flag is set. The MIR verifier follows those flags. `EXC-18/accept_a_view_made_on_one_path_holds_its_field_on_that_path`, `EXC-18/run_fail_a_view_made_on_one_path_holds_its_field_past_the_join` and `CELL-6/accept_try_borrow_of_a_class_field` fail without it; the verifier's unit tests `a_flagged_access_ends_only_where_it_began` and `an_access_open_on_one_path_must_still_close` keep it rejecting an end on a path that never began the access and an access left open at a return. |
| D-456 | **`Array[T]()` ignored its written type argument.** The `Array`/`String` constructor took its type only from the context: `x: Array[String] = Array[u8]()` compiled (and `x.push("hello")` ran), `Array[str]()` with nothing to annotate was `E2060`, and `String[int, bool]()` and `Array[u8, u8]()` were accepted. Found by the second review (G6-2). | `[TYP-18]` | **fixed** | A written type argument fixes the element type, and the context is then checked against it like any other type (`expected Array[String], found Array[u8]`); `Array` takes one type argument and `String` none (`E2020`). `TYP-18/accept_an_array_constructors_type_argument_fixes_it` and `TYP-18/reject_an_array_constructor_of_another_element_type`; both fail without the fix. |
| D-457 | **A fused `for` numbered a backwards `enumerate` differently from std's adapters.** The fused loop took any `rev` above an `enumerate` as running it backwards and checked its greatest number before the loop, gated on the loop having a turn. Two `rev`s cancel (`Rev(Rev(e)).next()` is `e.next()`), so `enumerate(start=int.MAX - 1).rev().rev().take(2)` panicked before its first turn where std gives two items (G8-1); and std pulls a backwards `enumerate` even when the loop gets no turn (a `skip` pulls what it passes over; a `zip` asks its first side before the second, and trims the longer side when run backwards), so `enumerate(start=int.MAX - 1).rev().skip(3)` over three items ran without the panic std raises (G8-2). Found by the second review. | `[CTL-3b]`, ODR-091 | **fixed** | A chain is fused only where its numbering is exact (`fused_counters_exact`): an `enumerate` under an even, non-zero number of `rev`s, or a backwards one with a `zip` above it or a `skip` above it pulled forwards, runs through std's adapters instead, which give the items and the panics by definition. `CTL-3b/accept_an_enumerate_under_two_revs_numbers_forwards`, `CTL-3b/run_fail_a_backwards_enumerate_a_skip_passes_over_still_numbers` and `CTL-3b/run_fail_a_backwards_enumerate_zipped_with_nothing_still_numbers`; all fail without the fix. No benchmark program's C changes. |
| D-458 | **A child interface naming its parent's associated type from another module failed, depending on import order.** D-394 collected each module's interfaces parents first, but the modules themselves in load order, which is not the order of their parents: with the child's module imported before the parent's (or the two importing each other, as `[MOD-4]` allows), the parent's `Item` was `cannot find type`. Found by the second review (G8-3). | `[IFC-3]`, `[MOD-4]` | **fixed** | Interfaces are collected in rounds across every module, each round taking those none of whose parents, named through the child's own imports, is still waiting; a cycle of parents is collected as written, where it is reported. `IFC-3/accept_a_child_interface_in_another_module_names_its_parents_type` (the child's module imported first) and `IFC-3/accept_a_parents_type_across_an_import_cycle`; both fail without the fix. |
| D-459 | **A `match` on a class field still moved out of it (`E3012`).** D-302 made a `match` on a place through a class handle's field bind by move, because a binding by reference would have needed a read access the handle's other aliases respect, not built then (D-202). D-202 built it, but the restriction stayed: `match b.name: Some(s):` was `cannot move out of a class field`. Found by the second review (G5-8). | `[GRM-13]`, `[EXC-18]` | **fixed** | Such a `match` binds by reference, as on any place that is not consumed; the binding's loan holds the field's read access while it lives, so a write to the field through another handle meanwhile panics. `GRM-13/accept_matching_a_class_field_binds_by_reference` and `GRM-13/run_fail_a_write_while_a_class_field_binding_lives` (D-302's use-after-free, caught at run time); both fail with the restriction back. `EXP-6/reject_moving_out_of_a_class_field` keeps its two moves. |
| D-460 | **A base class's view getter called on a derived-class handle is `E3060`.** `d.view()`, with `view(self) -> Span[int]` declared on the base, upcasts `d` into a temporary handle the statement's end releases (`[EXP-4]`), and the view borrows that temporary, so keeping the view past the statement is `E3060`, whose help (`c = p.child`) describes a handle stored in an object, which this is not. Binding the handle to a base-typed local first (`b: Base = d; v = b.view()`) works. Found by the second review (G5-7). | `[LT-1]`, `[RC-5]` | **fixed** | 2026-10-05, ADR-134: the borrow is a loan of `d` itself. The type checker borrows a derived local and upcasts the borrow (`ref Derived` to `ref Base`, as for an inherited `mut self` method) when the inherited method takes its receiver by address; lowering makes a shared borrow's cast a `ClassUpcastBorrowed` too; in C every borrowed upcast points at a base-typed copy of the handle (`_N_up`), never at the derived handle reinterpreted, which also removes that aliasing fault from inherited `mut self` calls. `RC-5/accept_an_inherited_view_getter_on_a_derived_local_borrows_the_local` (`E3060` before), `RC-5/reject_a_derived_local_viewed_through_an_inherited_getter_is_not_replaced` (`E3021` while the view lives), `CLS-4/accept_an_inherited_mut_self_method_reads_a_base_typed_copy` (no `struct em_obj_Base**` cast in the C). Break-tested, each half alone. |
| D-461 | **An exclusivity panic named a generic class instance by its C name.** The access label was the instance's symbol, so a field of `Bag[int]` was `Bag_i64.items` in the panic, where `[DIA-23]` names Ember entities, never a C symbol or a mangled name. Found by the second review (G5-10). | `[DIA-23]`, `[EXC-6]` | **fixed** | The label is the class as the user writes it, with its arguments (`TypeTable::class_display_name`: `Bag[i64].items`). `EXC-6/run_fail_a_generic_class_field_is_named_as_written`; it fails without the fix. |
| D-462 | **A class field could be written through a non-`self` handle only when `Copy`.** Assigning a field (`c.items = [9]`), `ref mut c.items` and `iter_mut` over one through any handle but `self` were `E1010` "mutable class-field access requires a `mut self` class method in this phase" (a plain `self` method could write one, so the words were wrong too), though `[CLS-7]` gives handles reference semantics and VIII.3 lists these as write accesses. Two compile-fail tests pinned the refusal. Found by the second review (G5-9, G5-N1). | `[CLS-7]`, `[EXC-16]`, `[EXC-18]` | **fixed** | The refusal is gone: such a write takes the field's access like any other, checked at run time (the per-field words of D-202). `CLS-7/accept_a_field_is_written_through_any_handle` (an assignment, a nested class's field through a field, `ref mut`, `iter_mut`) and `CLS-7/run_fail_a_field_written_through_another_handle_while_viewed` replace the two compile-fail tests; both fail with a refusal put back. |
| D-463 | **`void` did not implement `Default`.** `[TYP-36]`'s table gives `void` the `Default` `()`, but `T: Default` refused `void` (`void does not implement Default`), where D-355 had brought its `Eq`, `Ord`, `Hash` and `Debug` in line with the table. Found by the second review (G7-6). | `[TYP-36]` | **fixed** | `void` cannot be extended in source, so the compiler answers it: the bound is met, and `T.default()` is `()` in an instance for `void`. `TYP-36/accept_void_has_a_default`; it fails without the fix. |
| D-464 | **`ember run` turned a crash into a silent exit 1.** A program stopped by a stack overflow (`0xC00000FD`) or a segmentation fault exited 1 with nothing printed, so a crash looked like a quiet failure. Found by the second review (G7-7). | `[GRM-39]` | **fixed** | When the program's status is not an exit code, `ember run` names what stopped it (`error: the program was stopped by a stack overflow (0xC00000FD)`; on Unix, the signal) and exits non-zero; a panic, which ends through `abort()` after printing its own message, gets nothing more. The driver's unit test `a_crash_is_named_and_a_panic_or_an_exit_is_not` builds the statuses (a real crash could open a Windows error dialog); it fails without the fix, on Windows and on Linux. |
| D-465 | **The sort comparison took every `Array` for text.** `less_behind_pointers` compared any `Vec` as text by its bytes, keyed on the kind, not on ADR-061's text flag. Only `String` reaches it today (an `Array` is not `Ord`), but an `Array[u8]` given an order would have been sorted by its raw bytes. Found by the second review (G6-3). | `[TYP-36]`, ADR-061 | **fixed** | Text is matched by its flag; a non-text `Vec` there is a compiler error (`unreachable!`). No test can reach that arm while `Array` has no order; the `String` sorting tests cover the text one. |
| D-466 | **Borrow diagnostics named a class field `b.0`, and a conflict through one handle was never `E3080`.** The borrow checker's place names knew struct fields only, so a class field printed as its index (`b.0 is borrowed here and mutably borrowed elsewhere`), and a same-handle conflict on a class field was the ordinary `E3021`; `E3080`, which `[EXC-3]` names for it (shape X1), was declared and emitted nowhere. Found by the second review (G5-11). | `[LT-5]`, `[EXC-3]` | **fixed** (in part) | A class field is named (`b.items`), and a conflict whose loan goes through a class field is `E3080`. `EXC-3/reject_a_conflict_through_one_handle_is_e3080` fails with either half removed. Not done: a compiler temporary still prints as `_3` (`_3.items does not live long enough`, a handle stored in an object and copied for the statement); naming it by its source expression is still to do. |
| D-467 | **A view from a virtual getter read-locked every field of the object.** A call the caller cannot see into began a read of the whole receiver object for its result, so writing another field while the view lived panicked (`write access to Person.tags while a read access to Person.tags is active`, though the getter returned `name`), against `[EXC-19]`. ADR-060's over-approximation, with no owner ruling. Found by the second review (G5-6). | `[EXC-19]`, `[EXC-18]` | **fixed** (virtual calls; interface calls open) | A virtual call's result holds the union of the field accesses of every body it can reach (the slot's in the static class, its bases and the classes below it), when each is a field of the static class; otherwise, and for a call through an interface (whose static type has no fields), the whole object as before. `EXC-18/accept_a_view_from_a_virtual_getter_holds_only_its_fields` and `EXC-18/run_fail_a_view_from_a_virtual_getter_holds_its_overrides_fields` (a derived override's field is held through the base's call); the first fails without the union, the second with derived overrides left out of it. Open: an interface call still holds the whole object; the fix needs a per-class accessor in the type information that begins exactly what that class's implementation borrows. |
| D-468 | **A tuple or fixed-array type argument in expression position was refused.** `[GRM-8a]` parses a type argument that does not start with a type-only word as an expression and reads it back as a type, but the reading back knew names and instantiations only: `mem.size_of[(int, int)]()` and `mem.size_of[[int; 4]]()` were `E1010 expected a type argument`. Found by the second review's checker (G7-N1). | `[GRM-8a]` | **fixed** | A tuple of types and a fixed array (its length a constant) are read back too. `GRM-8a/accept_a_tuple_or_array_type_argument_in_expression_position`; it fails without the fix. `mem.size_of[(int, void)]()` still does not parse: `void` is not an expression and `[GRM-8a]` commits on the first word only, a gap for the spec rather than the compiler. |
| D-469 | **`char.from_u32` did not exist.** `[TYP-6]` converts to `char` with `as` from a `u8` only and sends every wider integer through `char.from_u32() -> Option[char]`, but `char.from_u32(x)` was `E2020` "`char` has no associated function `from_u32`", so a code point computed or read as a number could not become a `char` at all. | `[TYP-6]` | **fixed** | The compiler builds it (std cannot write it: `x as char` from a `u32` is refused by the same rule): `Some(x as char)` when `x` is at most `0x10FFFF` and outside the surrogates `0xD800` to `0xDFFF`, `None` otherwise, with `x` evaluated once. `TYP-6/accept_char_from_u32_gives_a_character_or_none`; it fails without the fix (`E2020`). |
| D-470 | **An iterator whose items are views of the text it holds does not compile.** A struct holding a `str` whose `next(mut self) -> Option[str]` gives parts of it (as `[TXT-10]`'s `split` and `lines` do) fails inside std's `Iterator` defaults (`count`, `last`, `nth`, ...: `E3025` "cannot call a method on `__it` while `__it` is mutably borrowed" and `E3060` "`__it` does not live long enough", reported in `std/src/core.em`); keeping two items (`x = w.next()`, `y = w.next()`) is `E3025`; and with `@derive(Copy)`, storing the rest of the text back (`self.rest = rest[at + 1..]`) is `E3063`, a view of `self` stored in `self`. By `[LT-20]` a view copied out of a field carries only that field's region, the text's; but the region model gives a reference to a struct with view fields no region slot of its own (`view_region_paths` in `regions.rs`: the reference takes the slots of the views behind it), so the loan the call takes on the iterator and the text its items view share one region, and an item keeps the iterator borrowed. | `[LT-1]`, `[LT-20]`, `[TXT-10]` | **fixed** | A reference has a region slot of its own beside the slots of the views behind it (ADR-105): a borrow's loan, and what holds the borrowed place, go in the reference's own slot, and each slot behind it gets what that view of the place borrows; a value read through the reference carries the views' facts, not its loan (`[LT-20]`). By `[LT-1]`, a result tied to a `mut` parameter of a `Copy` view type (passed by reference for its mode only, a source of the first kind) borrows what the view borrows; a non-`Copy` `mut` receiver is the caller's place, and two kept items stay `E3025` (`LT-1/reject_items_of_a_non_copy_iterator_keep_it_borrowed`). `LT-20/accept_items_of_a_copy_view_iterator_outlive_the_next_call` (two items; std's `last`, `count`, `nth`) and `LT-20/reject_a_reference_through_a_held_mutable_reference_keeps_its_holder_borrowed`; each fails with its part undone. `[TXT-10]`'s iterators can now be written as `Copy` views. |
| D-471 | **`sum_f64()` did not exist.** `[STD-5]` gives an iterator of `f32`s `sum_f64()`, which adds them in an `f64`, but it was `E1010` "has no method named `sum_f64`", so totalling many `f32`s without dropping the small ones took a hand-written loop. | `[STD-5]` | **fixed** | On an iterator of `f32`s, `sum_f64()` is the loop `sum` uses, from `0.0`, with each item converted to `f64` before it is added, left to right. `STD-5/accept_sum_f64_adds_f32s_in_f64` (past 2^24, `sum` drops each `1.0` and `sum_f64` keeps them; an empty one is `0.0`); it fails without the fix. With `KahanSum`, already in `std.math`, `[STD-5]` is complete. |
| D-472 | **A `String` did not have std's `str` methods.** `[TXT-11]` gives a `String` everything a `str` has; the compiler forwarded only the `str` methods it knows itself, so `s.chars()`, `s.split(",")` or `s.lines()` on a `String` were `E1010` (`String` has no method). Found writing `[TXT-10]`'s iterators. | `[TXT-11]` | **fixed** | A method `str` has from an extension (std's) is found for a `String` through a view of its text, after the `String`'s own methods (`[TYP-24]`). `TXT-11/accept_a_string_has_every_str_method` fails with the forwarding removed. |
| D-473 | **A type whose `next` was one of its own methods broke every adapter in the program.** With `extend T implements Iterator:` naming no `next` and `next` declared in `T`'s body, the conformance check took the inherent method as `Iterator.next`, but a call through the bound (`[TYP-17]`, D-245) looked only among interface methods; std's adapters are checked for every implementer, so a program using an adapter on an `Array` alone failed with `E2040 `T` has no `next` from `Iterator``, reported in `std/src/core.em`. std's `lines`, `split` and `split_whitespace` iterators, written that way, broke 11 conformance programs. Found writing `[TXT-10]`. | `[TYP-17]`, `[TYP-24]`, `[IFC-1]` | **fixed** | An inherent method the conformance check accepts as an interface's is registered as that interface's method too (`interface_methods`, `interface_associated`), so a call through a bound, `I.m(x)` and a `dyn I` reach the function the check approved. `TYP-17/accept_an_inherent_next_is_the_iterators_for_a_bound_call` fails with the registration removed. The spec does not say whether a type's own method may implement an interface; the compiler has always accepted it and std relies on it (`ArrayIntoIter`, `MapIntoKeys`), so it stays, and the question is the owner's. |
| D-474 | **Every program took four times as long to compile.** ADR-106 gave std six iterators that are not generic (`Chars`, `CharIndices`, `Bytes`, `Lines`, `Split`, `SplitWhitespace`); each implementing type gets a copy of every one of `Iterator`'s provided methods (`count`, `enumerate`, `join`, `last`, `max`, `min`, `nth`, `peekable`, `skip`, `step_by`, `take`, `to_array`), and those name the adapters' own methods, so every program, text or not, borrow-checked about 150 more bodies: `ember check` on a 10-line program went from 0.08 s to 0.38 s (2.5 s with the debug compiler CI tests with), and CI's test jobs from 45 minutes to over 2 hours. Found when that CI run did not finish. | `[COST-1]` | **fixed** | A body instantiated from a `std` declaration (a generic function's instance, a generic type's method, an interface's provided method) is kept only if something reaches it: a call, a function value, an interface table's implementation (`CheckOutput::std_instances`, `note_std_instance`, `ember_mir::prune_unused_implicit`); a class's method (its vtable names it) and a `drop` (drop glue names it by its type) stay. 0.11 s release, 0.47 s debug; the pruning also drops the unused instances the std already had. The provided methods' copies are still type-checked (7 ms). `std_instances_nothing_reaches_are_not_kept` fails with the marking undone. No spec change: `[COST-1]` is about what a use emits. |
| D-475 | **`a05_structs` ran 1.47x the C with MSVC, 1.15x with clang and 1.28x with gcc (1.00x before).** ADR-106 copied a view assigned whole by its two fields, as it writes a view a built-in makes; MSVC then no longer interchanged the program's loop nest (each particle kept in registers for all its steps), and clang and gcc lost time too. Found by the README benchmark run. | `[CG-C-3]` (the speed the backend owes) | **fixed** | A view copied whole is copied by its fields only from a place its function writes by its fields (a built-in's view, an `Option` of a view's `None`: `parted_views`), where a whole read would wait for both stores; from a place written whole it is one assignment. Copied whole everywhere instead, `lines()` ran 1.98x the C with MSVC (its iterator's rest is written by a slice and read back each item). `a05_structs` 1.00x MSVC, 1.01x clang. `aggregates_and_views_are_written_field_by_field` fails with either direction undone (every copy by fields, or none). No spec change. |
| D-476 | **A 57-line program took 2.56 s to compile.** `CTL-3b/accept_an_adapter_chain_is_one_counted_loop` (seven adapter chains in one `main`, about 1,100 MIR statements once they are counted loops) spent nearly all of it in region inference: `infer_callable_summaries` infers every body again in each round of its fixpoint, and its fixpoint runs five times in a compilation (installing the summaries three times, verifying them twice), so this `main` was inferred 18 times there alone, and the debug compiler CI tests with takes several times as long. Found while measuring D-474. | `[MIR-REG-1]` (the compiler's own cost) | **fixed** | A body none of whose direct callees' summaries changed in the last round keeps the summary it has, since its inference reads nothing else but closure bodies' summaries (a change to one of those infers every body again): the same rounds and the same fixpoint, so the same C (all 52 benchmark programs' C, three compilers each, byte for byte). 1.94 s. `a_body_whose_callees_did_not_change_is_not_inferred_again` fails with the skip removed. One inference of a body that size is still slow (each point keeps every region's state), left for later. No spec change. |
| D-477 | **Ordinary Array and Span element iteration never received the running-total fast path.** Its MIR takes a read-only reference to each indexed element; `vectorisable` rejected every `Rvalue::Ref` and `vf_place` every dereference, although the access is the unit-stride index `[SIMD-5]` permits. `for b in text.as_bytes()` therefore kept a check on each addition even where the element width proves it safe. | `[SIMD-5]`, `[SIMD-7]` | **fixed** | ADR-109: read-only element references assigned once per turn are admitted; a dereference follows its dominating definition (and reference copies) to the indexed element. Carried references, unknown references, mutable references and strided sources remain excluded. Existing alias, width, block and first-overflow checks are reused. Six SIMD-5/SIMD-7 cases cover these proofs, including UTF-8 bytes and reads before rewriting a view. Before the fix, the Array/Span total case emits zero block proofs instead of two; with the fix it emits two. C assertions pass on MSVC and clang in debug/release/shipping. The full MSVC run passes every other target, and its full conformance target passes after correcting the chain-loop fixture; the full clang workspace suite, the quick annotation sweep and all repository gates pass. |
| D-478 | **Seven String mutation and capacity methods named by the spec were missing.** `String.with_capacity` was an unknown name, and `capacity`, `reserve`, `insert`, `remove`, `truncate`, `clear` were absent. | `[TXT-11]`, `[TXT-4]`, `[TYP-31]`, `[HEAP-8]`; ODR-098 | **implemented; correctness validated; performance audit open** | Hardened_52 supplies the missing contracts. ADR-110: shared byte-buffer growth/clear/capacity paths, inline UTF-8 insertion/removal/truncation, exact integer-size conversion before mutation, and receiver reservation before arguments with activation at the call. Twenty-one TXT-11 tests cover Unicode/NUL, capacity preservation, boundaries, wide and negative counts, and live-view rejection. The pre-fix combined probe fails to resolve `String.with_capacity`; the implementation runs it. Receiver ordering and mutation during reservation are break-tested. Validation before the final order review fix: full MSVC, clang and GCC workspace suites, MSVC/GCC annotation sweeps, all eleven gates and unchanged Appendix A generation pass. On mains and performance cores, the 40-million-edit C twin has matching output; msvc: Ember 0.4477 s, C 0.4703 s (0.95x); clang: Ember 0.4551 s, C 0.4579 s (0.99x). The checked reservation fast path removes Clang's priced call overhead for every Array and String (ADR-110). GCC: Ember 0.4166 s, C 0.4390 s (0.95x), matching output, with the same GCC release flags and 11 interleaved runs on mains, both pinned to WSL guest CPU 0; host performance-core affinity is unverified. |
| D-479 | **A mutable call reservation was activated before conditional arguments.** `append(values, values.len() if values.len() > 0 else 0)` rejected the shared reads with E3021, although both argument paths reach the same consuming call. The reservation search handled only a single-successor chain. Found while reviewing String checked-size argument lowering. | `[BRW-3]`, `[BCK-5]`, `[EXP-1]` | **implemented; correctness validated; performance audit open** | A general nonrecursive control-flow search requires the first borrower use on every returning path to be one consuming call, rejects cycles/reassignment/earlier uses, and permits panic exits. Method-autoref provenance uses the same search. User-bound mutable references remain active from creation. BRW-3 conditional-read acceptance is break-tested; conditional borrows live at activation and String argument mutation remain rejected. Focused annotations and final three-compiler correctness validation pass; the wider performance audit remains open. |
| D-480 | **Single-byte string searches paid for the multi-byte search loop.** A fresh MSVC `split` measurement crossed the over-10% threshold, so it was investigated as a new table entry. | `[TXT-10]`, `[TXT-4]` | **implemented; correctness validated; performance audit open** | ADR-111: a general one-byte `memchr` path, guarded before touching an empty/null view or an out-of-range starting offset. Empty and multi-byte needles keep their existing paths. A 51-run paired diagnostic measured Ember/C 1.1012x before, 1.0585x with the path, and 0.9943x with the path and checks removed only in the diagnostic, pricing the remaining gap as checks. The standalone panic and the new empty/UTF-8/NUL search case pass; final correctness validation passes; final measurements and the wider performance audit remain open. |
| D-481 | **Read-only byte reductions missed a much cheaper integer widening sum.** The initial MSVC runtime-count explanation was accepted too early: safe partial sums reach C speed, and SSE2 widens eight byte values with one SAD instruction instead of individual scalar additions. | `[SIMD-5]`, `[SIMD-7]`, `[CG-C-1]` | **implemented; correctness validated; performance audit open** | ADR-112: complete read-only byte-sum data flow selects a portable wrapping helper only after the existing bounds/arithmetic proofs. Signed and unsigned byte additions/subtractions into 64-bit totals, copied indices/references, arbitrary views and short/unaligned tails share the mechanism. Calls, mutations, prefix outputs, live intermediates and checked operations keep the original loop. The checked copy and width guards remain. Three new conformance cases pass under MSVC, clang and GCC in all profiles; the guarded scalar oracle passes on all three, and GCC also tests the portable fallback. Disabling the selection fails the new C assertion; dropping the vector length guard in an external header control faults at an inaccessible page. Final full correctness suites pass; final production timing and the wider performance audit remain open. |
| D-482 | **Every allocation wrote shared, non-atomic statistics in all profiles.** This violates the explicit runtime thread-local/debug-only requirement and races when independent threads allocate. | `[RT-11]` | **implemented; correctness validated; performance audit open** | ADR-113: keep the same statistics ABI with thread-local counters. Allocation/free totals describe the calling thread; live bytes are its balance, saturating at zero on cross-thread frees. A native two-thread oracle fails with the original shared counter and passes with MSVC, clang and GCC; a larger cross-thread free also fails before correcting the saturation. The standalone UTF-8 panic remains window-free. |
| D-483 | **Fresh class construction temporaries were copied into owning sinks and then dropped, adding a retain/release pair even when the new owner provably preserves their lifetime.** | `[RC-2]`, `[RC-3]`, `[OPT-2]` | **implemented; correctness validated; performance audit open** | ADR-114: transfer known fresh non-Sync temporary owners through class/interface temporaries and Array push, with unique use/drop, dominance and all-path lifetime proofs. Named/borrowed/unknown owners, Sync objects and owned calls stay counted. A callee can explicitly drop its argument before returning, so calls cannot take the caller's last temporary owner. Cached origin proofs and one block-dominator tree avoid repeated whole-body traversal. Fifteen unit tests and six new conformance cases cover ownership, CFG and scaling; the transfer-pass RED and final correctness validation are complete; the wider performance audit remains open. |
| D-484 | **Char values had no Unicode scalar upper bound in integer range facts, so safe widening arithmetic retained redundant overflow checks.** | `[RNG-4]`, `[TYP-3]`, `[TYP-6]`, `[FFI-8]` | **implemented; correctness validated; performance audit open** | ADR-115: char's interval is 0..=0x10FFFF, conservatively including the surrogate gap. FFI char32_t remains unrestricted u32; overflow-capable additions stay checked. The noinline fixture proves one removable check while preserving the two required ones. The disabled-Char-fact C assertion is RED: three additions checked instead of two. Restored code keeps the required two; final full correctness validation passes; dynamic text-reduction proof work remains open. |
| D-485 | **Native text decoding used a signed internal cursor and one decoder shape despite safe measured compiler-specific improvements.** | `[CTL-1]`, `[TXT-10]`, `[TYP-31]` | **implemented; correctness validated; performance audit open** | ADR-116: native loops use hidden usize state and explicitly cast public int offsets; typed int64_t*/size_t* helpers avoid pointer punning. Genuine MSVC uses common advance; clang/GCC keep original branch advances. Two run-pass fixtures and two privacy negatives cover UTF-8 widths/NUL/max scalar, native/stored state, empty views and loop control. An independent exhaustive typed-helper oracle passes prior validation and actual 32-bit MSVC. A deliberate page-end overread faults; restoring signed native lowering makes the unsigned-helper C assertion RED. Production comparisons are diagnostic; final full correctness suites pass while GCC text-reduction work remains open. |
| D-486 | **Split revalidated endpoints already proved by search, and its direct producer was not classified as writing nested view descriptors by fields.** MSVC then read those narrow stores and copied descriptors whole, causing a fresh production regression. | `[TXT-10]`, `[EXP-1]`, `[LT-20]`, `[CG-C-3]`, `[TYP-13]` | **implemented; correctness validated; performance audit open** | ADR-117: one valid-UTF-8 search constructs the existing split_once result used by std Split.next. Both evaluated inputs are captured before output writes; exact Some-field provenance ties both returned views only to text. General producer classification follows exact-type view copies and exact niche Some wrapping/extraction; real projections and wholly written unrelated sources remain. None writes only its live tag, with no inactive-payload observation. Nine TXT-10 fixtures and one projected shared-Span fixture cover behavior, copy chains and borrowing. The copy-chain milestone is RED before adoption; disabling propagation or niche canonicalization separately is RED, as are the independent result-field provenance and evaluation-order/check controls. Current 11-run diagnostic Ember/C ratios: MSVC 1.038572x, clang 0.949404x, GCC 0.941660x. Both accumulator checks remain. Final full correctness suites pass and the wider slowdown audit remains open; no reasonable/unavoidable-overhead claim follows. |
| D-487 | **Foreign byte/text views have size_t lengths, while public sizes/indices and stored text iterators use int; no universal INT64_MAX length limit is documented or checked.** A hypothetical allocation-backed 64-bit view exceeding INT64_MAX would expose unchecked public length conversion; managed buffers are capped at PTRDIFF_MAX and every 32-bit offset fits int. Found during D-485 proof review, pre-existing in size_as_int/stored iterators. | `[TYP-31]`, `[TXT-6]`, `[UNS-4]`, `[FFI-8]` | **open; no realizable supported-target reproduction** | Do not claim every valid foreign view fits PTRDIFF_MAX. Extent beyond the live allocation is unsafe UB, but oversize alone is not expressly ruled UB. If universal signed representability is required, establish/enforce INT64_MAX consistently at shared view/FFI boundaries rather than adding a native-loop-only restriction. D-485 usize advancement is independently bounded by remaining valid bytes. |
| D-488 | **Compiler-synthesized evaluation bindings end owned producer temporaries at hidden statement boundaries instead of their enclosing source evaluation endpoint.** String-to-text producers can die before the generated call consumes their view; synthesized owned bindings can also drop earlier than the source statement. Present in HEAD and current lowering. | `[EXP-1]`, `[EXP-4]`, `[DRP-3]` | **fixed** | ADR-127 (2026-10-04, autopilot, from the other agent's design and cases under d488-evaluation-lifetimes): every statement of a compiler-written evaluation (`ExprKind::Block`) is a step of the source statement, so its temporaries are not ended at the step and a hidden binding is registered as one of that statement's temporaries, ending in reverse order of creation. No HIR marker was needed: the AST has no expression block, and the user's statements sit only inside its statements' own blocks (a loop body), which keep their ends. The five cases are in `tests/conformance/EXP-4/` (the two refusals are still refused); with each step ending its own temporaries, the capacity/tuple-order and temporary-separator cases fail. The benchmark programs' C is unchanged (all 52 with MSVC, four checked with gcc). This is not D-466, whose remaining issue is temporary names in borrow diagnostics. |
| D-489 | **Structural Hash capability classified mutable Span as hashable although std supplies that structural implementation only for shared Span.** First Option[MutSpan[int]].take() lookup admitted an unused Option[T:Hash].hash body, which failed at MutSpan.hash with E2020 before pruning. Present at HEAD; D-318 is the earlier analogous capability error. | `[TYP-36]`, `[TYP-24]` | **fixed** | The minimal hashes() match restricts structural Span capability to mutable: false. Mutable Span continues through the existing explicit Hash-implementation fallback; no spec change or broader generic workaround. The shared-Span actual-method/generic-bound positive and two adapted mutable positives pass debug/release/shipping under MSVC and clang. The original larger mutable case gets past Hash but exposes separate D-490 E3040, so do not claim it GREEN. The generic Hash negative passes with sole E2040 in all Windows profiles. Disabling the shared-only guard makes valid Option.take fail E2020 in unused std Hash and incorrectly accepts the Hash-bound negative; restored full source is rebuilt. Final GCC and three-compiler full correctness suites pass. Evidence: hash-fixed-negative-results.json and hash-cache-break-results.json. |
| D-490 | **Branch-name hoisting appends reads of locals already consumed inside a completing owned match arm.** moved/wrapped/forwarded are consumed by later moves or owned matches, but the arm-end assignments reread them, causing E3040 at their original declarations. | `[CTL-10]`, `[OWN-1]` | **fixed** | ADR-128 (2026-10-04, autopilot): an arm's declaration of a hoisted name is now the hoisted variable itself (`hoisted_into` on the arm's local; MIR maps both to one local and lowers the arm's declaration as setting it), with no copy at the arm's end. A name an arm moves is then moved after the branch, an error only where it is read, and reported there: `tests/conformance/CTL-10/accept_a_name_moved_in_its_arm_is_still_hoisted.em` (refused before with E3040 and L1001) and `reject_a_hoisted_name_moved_in_one_arm_then_read.em` (E3040 at the read, where it was at the declaration) both fail with the copy back. Earlier notes: root reproduced the original larger mutable-view case after D-489's Hash fix. Source inspection finds the same unconditional hoist_branch_names assignments in HEAD; no baseline executable failure is claimed. The None arm panics, so Some alone qualifies as completing, and unused outer bindings also cause L1001. Evidence and minimal unexecuted candidate: hash-mut-span/MOVE-HOIST.md. This is distinct from D-489 and fixed D-260's completion classification. General hoisting/move-state correction and validation remain open. |
| D-491 | **MemReplace writes a direct Span result without refreshing its duplicate indexing-pointer cache.** Confirmed pre-fix C assigns the descriptor to _5 but indexes uninitialized _5_ptr. | `[CG-C-1]`, `[SPN-1]`, `[SPN-2]` | **fixed** | Root confirmed the direct result/cache path in view-copy-review/memreplace-before-fix.c: _5 = *((ember_span*)(&_3)); then _5_ptr[_18] without an intervening pointer assignment. The omission is also present in HEAD by source inspection. The minimal backend change calls the existing set_view_pointer(dest) after MemReplace completes its descriptor writes and before continuation. No provenance/alias inference or result ABI changes. The dynamic-index runtime fixture and cache milestone pass all Windows profiles. Disabling only the setter makes the cache milestone RED; the broken executable was never run. Full source is restored/rebuilt. Final GCC and three-compiler full correctness suites pass. Evidence: hash-cache-break-results.json. |
| D-492 | **Linux GCC executables retained unreachable runtime/program sections.** The empty-program gap includes process/loader work and has not been declared unavoidable. | `[CG-C-3]`, `[RT-1]`, `[RT-2]`, `[FFI-26]` | **implemented; correctness validated, performance audit OPEN** | ADR-118: executable-only section compilation/link removal, canonical defined C roots and linker-capability fallback; host linking and static archives keep their original policy. Cached runtime and relaxed-FP units opt in explicitly; the compile command changes the runtime cache key. The rt_init(NULL) entry experiment was neutral in paired GCC sampling and has been reverted; original configuration construction remains. The cached/uncached all-profile export/removal regression is RED on the checkpoint. Focused GCC CRT/hooks/TLS accounting/reinit/panic/stack/RELRO/root and unsupported-linker checks pass; removing export roots or GC separately is caught. Full MSVC/clang/GCC workspace suites, MSVC/GCC annotation sweeps, all eleven gates and byte-identical Appendix regeneration pass on fresh frozen source c936abc3, including the entry reversion and D-493. The preserved first launcher failed before GCC testing; its two completed Windows suites were reused only after source/tool/record-chain/log hash verification, and the corrected continuation completed every remaining group. Libm retention and other safe startup alternatives remain open. |
| D-493 | **Default C11 over-aligned allocation could wrap the size round-up near SIZE_MAX and call aligned_alloc with zero bytes.** | `[ALC-4]`, `[RT-1]`, `[RT-11]` | **fixed** | Reject an unrepresentable rounded size before the C11 allocator call. Ordinary malloc, zero-size normalization, MSVC forwarding and custom-hook arguments remain unchanged. Independent NULL-only allocator interception confirms GCC RED on the original and GREEN with the guard; MSVC/LLVM forwarding controls pass. The permanent public try_alloc regression is RED on the original GCC source and GREEN in all three full production workspace suites. MSVC/GCC annotations, all eleven gates and byte-identical Appendix regeneration pass on frozen source c936abc3. Template and generated-runtime guard agree; no real huge allocation is attempted. ADR-119 and external d493-validation/run-20261004-continuation-03 retain the complete verified continuation and the original launcher failure. |
| D-386 | **A view iterator's `next` gave C a `const T*` for a `ref T`.** A shared `ref T` is `T*` in C (`c_type`: a `Cell` in it is written through it), but `SpanIterNext` built the item as `const T*`, and every `it.next()` over a shared view drew MSVC's C4090, which `[CG-C-1]`'s warning-free output forbids; nothing failed, as the suites do not compile with warnings as errors. | `[SPN-5]`, `[CG-C-1]` | **fixed** | The item pointer is built without `const`, as `get_unchecked`'s is. Test: LEX-19 `accept_a_reference_formats_as_what_it_reaches` asserts no `const int64_t*` in the C; break-tested. |
| D-385 | **An f-string gave C a reference that was not a name as text.** `f"{p.1}"` with `p: (int, ref int)` (an `enumerate` pair, a parameter) emitted `ember_fmt_str(buffer, p._1)` on an `int64_t*`, which the C compiler refused; a `ref` name was read through (`{x}` is `*x`), a reference reached any other way was not. | `[LEX-19]` | **fixed** | An f-string part is read through (`read_through`) before it is formatted. Test: LEX-19 `accept_a_reference_formats_as_what_it_reaches`; break-tested. |
| D-384 | **A method of an interface private to another module could make a call ambiguous, or answer it.** `m.hidden()` on a type implementing both another module's private `Hidden` and this module's `Mine`, each with `hidden`, was `E2070 hidden is offered by both`, or chose the private one and then reported it private (`E1052`). Found when std's private `Integer` gained `successor` and a program's own `successor` on `int` became ambiguous. | `[MOD-2]`, `[TYP-24]` | **fixed** | An interface the caller cannot see (`interface_visible_here`) neither answers a call (`visible_interface_method` takes a visible interface's method instead) nor counts as a rival for `E2070`. Test: MOD-2 `accept_another_modules_private_interface_is_no_rival`. |
| D-383 | **A compilation could stop with `internal compiler error: [LT-40] cannot load interface cache: ... Access is denied (os error 5)`.** Programs of one directory share one interface cache, and the conformance suite compiles them side by side; on Windows, a record another compilation is renaming into place (D-189's atomic write) refuses a reader for a moment, and any read error but "not found" was fatal. Seen once, 2026-09-29, in the MSVC workspace suite (`TYP-22/accept_dyn_interface_box_class_mut`); the test passed alone. | `[LT-40]` | **fixed** | A refused read is tried again five times, 10 ms apart (`read_record`); if still refused, this compilation uses its fresh record (complete and current) and leaves the file to the other. A malformed or stale record is still the hard error it was. Test: `ember_build` `a_record_the_reader_is_refused_uses_the_fresh_one` (Windows: a directory where the record would be refuses the read); break-tested. |
| D-382 | **A `for` over a value whose type is a parameter bounded by `Iterator` was `E2040 cannot be iterated: it has no next method`.** `fn tally[I: Iterator](owned it: I)` could call `it.next()` but not write `for x in it`: the loop looked `next` up in the method table by the type, which a type parameter has no entry in; `[CTL-1]` iterates any `Iterator`. Found 2026-09-29: `Iterator`'s own default methods (`[STD-19]`) loop over `self`. | `[CTL-1]` | **fixed** | The loop builds `__it.next()` and checks it as a method call, so `next` comes from the bound (`check_for_iterator`). Test: CTL-1 `accept_a_for_over_a_type_parameter`. |
| D-381 | **A default method could not name its interface's associated type.** `interface Source: type Item; fn kept(self, keep: fn(Item) -> bool) -> Array[Item]` with a body declaring `out: Array[Item]` was `E1010 cannot find type Item` for every implementing type, and `expected Self.Item, found int` at calls through `keep`: the declaration resolved `Item`, but each implementing type's copy was built and checked without the interface's associated names in scope, and a callable parameter's function type (`[CLO-6]` makes it a generic bounded by that type) was never resolved against the implementing type. `Self.Item` does not parse, and the spec writes the bare name. Found 2026-09-29 writing `[STD-19]`'s consumers. | `[IFC-4]`, `[CLO-6]` | **fixed** | While one implementing type's copy of a default is made or checked, the interface's associated names are in scope and a bare one is that type's value (`enter_default` / `assoc_owner`, in the concrete path, the per-type body copy and the three queued-body loops); `resolve_assoc` now reaches function types, views and struct instances, and callable bounds are resolved too (`resolve_signature_assoc`); the opaque check reads `Item` as the opaque `Self`'s. Test: IFC-4 `accept_a_default_method_names_the_interfaces_associated_type` (`int` and `String` implementers). |
| D-380 | **A generic extension could not name its parameter's associated type.** `extend[I: Iterator] Take[I] implements Iterator: type Item = I.Item` and `fn next(mut self) -> Option[I.Item]` were `E2040 I has no associated type Item`, though `[IFC-4]` allows `T.Name` for any `T` whose bounds declare `Name`, and a generic function's signature could already say it. The extension's block was resolved over its written parameters only, and applying it bound only those. Found 2026-09-29 writing `[STD-19]`'s first adapter; every adapter in std needs it. | `[IFC-4]`, `[GRM-34]` | **fixed** | Collecting a generic extension declares one hidden parameter for each `P.Name` its signatures and `type` members name (`declare_projections`, as for a function); matching binds the written parameters only, and applying binds each hidden one to what the matched type says `Name` is (`project`). Test: IFC-4 `accept_a_generic_extension_names_its_parameters_associated_type` (applied to a concrete iterator and inside a generic function). Break-tested: without the declaration, the test fails with the old `E2040`. |
| D-379 | **An interface whose default method returns a generic of `Self` made checking loop forever.** `interface Counter: fn doubled(owned self) -> Doubled[Self]` with `extend[C: Counter] Doubled[C] implements Counter` never finished `ember check`, even with nothing calling `doubled`: registering an instance's interfaces instantiated every default method's signature at once, so `Doubled[C]`'s `doubled` named `Doubled[Doubled[C]]`, an instance implementing `Counter`, whose own `doubled` named the next. Found 2026-09-29 while probing how `[STD-19]`'s adapters (`take(owned self, n) -> Take[Self]`) could be written in std; it blocks all of them. | `[TYP-16]`, `[TYP-17]` | **fixed** | ADR-071: a generic instance's default whose signature holds `Self` inside another type is kept in `deferred_defaults` and made when a method call names it (`materialize_deferred_default`, from `synth_registered_method`); the implementation check counts it as defined. Test: TYP-17 `accept_a_default_method_returning_a_generic_of_self` (prints `4 8 5`). Break-tested: with the deferral off, `ember check` on it did not finish in 30 s; with it, it does. |
| D-374 | **A `Shared[T]` stored in a class field began no access.** `borrows.rs` kept its own copy of the place-type walk, and that copy had no class-field step, so `h.shared` had the class's type. `is_shared_owner` then missed the `Shared`, so `reader = h.shared.get()` followed by `alias.get_mut()` ran on, and printed 2, with a `ref` and a `ref mut` to one value alive at once. The same `Shared` in a local panicked. Found 2026-09-27, while building ODR-085's removal pass, whose access targets needed the field's real type. | `[HEAP-5]`, `[HEAP-6]`, `[EXC-1]` | **fixed** | `place_ty` is now the one walk in `regions.rs` (`place_type`), which has the class-field step. The C backend's `class_field_of_place` now answers only for a field with its own word, so the `Shared` access falls through to its payload header word, as it does for a local. Verified: `HEAP-5/run_fail_a_shared_in_a_class_field_conflicts.em` printed 2 before the fix and panics after it in debug and release. With the walk reverted, it fails again. All annotated tests and the workspace suite pass. |
| D-378 | **List capacity arithmetic could wrap into a heap overflow.** `[HEAP-8]` requires capacity arithmetic to be checked, panicking with `capacity overflow` before any allocation, and no length or capacity computation in the runtime to wrap; none of it was built. `ember_vec_reserve` doubled the capacity and multiplied it by the element size unchecked, and `reserve(n)` passed `len + n` formed in `size_t`. So a Safe `xs.reserve(2^62)` on an `Array[int]` wrapped the byte count (2^65) to a small one, allocated a tiny buffer and recorded a capacity of 2^62, and the pushes after it would write past the allocation. Found by reading the runtime while building range facts; not run, since it could crash. | `[HEAP-8]`, `[ALC-4]` | **fixed** 2026-09-28 | `ember_vec_reserve` refuses a capacity whose size in bytes would pass `PTRDIFF_MAX` (no allocator gives more), panicking `capacity overflow` before allocating; the doubled capacity is cut back to that limit, so doubling cannot wrap; elements of no size are counted to the same limit, so no length wraps either. `ember_vec_reserve_more(v, size, n)` checks `n` against the room left before forming `len + n`; `reserve(n)`, `extend` and the runtime's byte `extend` use it. Every other size computation in the list code multiplies an index no greater than the capacity, so it fits once the capacity does. The panic names `<array>` rather than the caller's line, as the arena's does. `Map`/`Set` `reserve(n)` with an impossible `n` still panics first in their own checked arithmetic (`integer overflow`), not with `capacity overflow`. Tests: `HEAP-8/run_fail_an_impossible_capacity_panics.em`, `HEAP-8/accept_a_capacity_within_the_limit_grows.em`. No specification, ADR or owner decision changed. |
| D-377 | **The 128-bit self-test could not be built by clang on Windows.** `ember_build`'s `the_128_bit_halves_agree_with_int128` checks the runtime's 128-bit halves against the C compiler's own `__int128`. clang for Windows compiles `__int128` division and float conversions into calls (`__divti3`, `__udivti3`, `__fixdfti`, `__fixunsdfti`, `__floattidf`, `__floattisf`, `__floatuntidf`, `__floatuntisf`) into its own runtime library, which it links only when asked, so the test's program failed to link and the local clang suite read 304 of 305. No Ember program was affected: on Windows every compiler builds `i128` from the halves, which need none of those functions. CI runs the test on Linux clang and gcc, where the library is linked by default; MSVC and clang-cl skip it. | `[TYP-1]`, `[TST-1]` | **fixed** 2026-09-28 | The test passes `-rtlib=compiler-rt` when clang builds it on Windows, which asks clang for its own library wherever it is installed. The clang suite on Windows: 305 of 305. No specification, ADR or owner decision changed. |
| D-376 | **The C of 67 test programs warns under clang `-std=c11 -Wall -Wextra`.** `[CG-C-1]` requires none. By kind (programs): an unused parameter, mostly a `dyn` adapter's empty drop (31); a `T**` passed where `const T**` is expected (13); `x <= x` in a slice's bounds check (11); an unused local (6); an unused type info (3); a constant or literal conversion that changes a value (3); a `void` local set but never read (2). The milestone test compiles one program with `-Werror`, and is skipped when MSVC is the C compiler. Found 2026-09-28 by a sweep of every test program while checking the MSVC loop work (ADR-070 item 7), none of it from that work: the committed compiler (`863b43d`) gives 69 programs, the same ones plus two interface view getters D-375 fixed. | `[CG-C-1]` | **open** | Not started. The sweep: emit each test program's C for clang and compile it with `-Werror`. |
| D-375 | **An interface call on a class ran the base class's method, not the override.** The adapter behind a class's interface table called the implementing method directly (`em_Base_id(...)`). A `Child` that overrides the virtual `id` and reaches the interface through a `Base`-typed handle got the `Base` table (it is the only one: the checker refuses `Child` to `Named` directly, `E2020`), so `show(as_base)` printed 1, not 2. Found 2026-09-27 while writing fix 3's test of inherited interface entries; the older compiler prints 1 too. | `[DSP-1]`, `[DSP-2]`, `[DSP-3]` | **fixed** | The adapter of an interface slot whose implementation is a virtual method of a class that is not final now calls through the object's own class table (`->ti->vtable->slotN`), so the override runs; a final class keeps the direct call. Class tables are emitted before the adapters. Verified: `run-pass/class_interface_handle_base_entries.em` printed `1 1` before and prints `1 2` in debug, release and shipping. All annotated tests and the workspace suite pass. |
| D-373 | **`MIN.rem_trunc(-1)` panicked under the truncating remainder contract.** For a signed minimum value, division by `-1` overflows but the truncating remainder is defined as zero; the checked helper's internal overflow signal was incorrectly treated as language-level overflow. | `[STD-20]` | **fixed** | Runner reproduced this at line 8 of `tests/conformance/STD-20/accept_fixed_integer_method_edges_under_lexical_policies.em`. The MIR boundary preserves safe checked-helper handling while suppressing this internal division-overflow signal as language overflow; zero-divisor checking remains. The edge case and `run_fail_rem_trunc_zero_divisor_under_wrap.em` pass focused verification; full workspace and repository validation passed (see current H40 verification in `HANDOFF.md`). |
| D-372 | **Fixed-contract integer methods inherited the caller's overflow policy.** `abs(MIN)` under `@overflow(wrap)` succeeded, and `pow` under `@overflow(saturate)` did not preserve the checked contract required by the fixed-method rules. | `[STD-20]` | **fixed** | The lowering now keeps fixed-method arithmetic under its checked policy while evaluating the receiver and supplied arguments under caller policy. The reproduced cases in `run_fail_abs_keeps_checked_contract_under_wrap.em` and `run_fail_pow_keeps_checked_contract_under_saturate.em`, plus the caller-policy boundary case, pass focused verification; full workspace and repository validation passed (see `HANDOFF.md`). |
| D-371 | **Negative integer literals were normalized incorrectly during constant evaluation.** Signed MIN constants could be rejected, while i128 MIN overflow and out-of-range negative values had incorrect classifications; typed unsigned `-1u8` remains policy-controlled arithmetic. | `[CT-4]`, `[CT-7]`, `[LEX-24]`, `[TYP-8]` | **fixed** | Constant evaluation now normalizes signed negative literals before range classification and retains the policy-controlled typed-unsigned case. `CT-4/`, `CT-7/accept_nested_minimum_integer_literals.em`, and the `LEX-24` signed-boundary cases pass focused verification; full workspace and repository validation passed (see `HANDOFF.md`). |
| D-370 | **Invalid shift amounts were masked or accepted under overflow policies.** Out-of-range shifts must panic regardless of `@overflow(wrap)` or `@overflow(saturate)`. | `[TYP-10]` | **fixed** | Shift-range checking is independent of arithmetic overflow policy. The wrap, saturate, negative, and compound-shift cases in `tests/conformance/TYP-10/` pass focused verification. The older integer-semantics expectation was corrected to use explicit `wrapping_shl`; the exact failed MSVC target then passed 1/1. Full workspace and repository validation passed (see `HANDOFF.md`). |
| D-369 | **Dropping an unrelated owning field unnecessarily kept a plain view borrowed.** `Plain[T]` contains `ref i32` and a `T` payload but has no destructor of its own. With `T = String`, its field cleanup incorrectly kept the scalar borrowed until scope exit, rejecting an otherwise legal source write with E3021. The region pass used the whole local's `needs_drop` flag for every projected drop. | `[DRP-6]`, `[DRP-7]` | **fixed** | The region pass derives reads from the actual destructor-bearing fields at each MIR drop, recursively through structs, enums and tuples; fixed-array slots and custom-destructor/guard requirements remain conservative. Focused direct and Option-wrapped cases pass. The mutation test that removes the region fix reproduces the failure; restoring it returns the focused cases to green. Full MSVC and clang-cl workspace suites, all 18 gates, H39 examples (46, `failing 0`), and the 287-directory annotation sweep (`failing 0`) pass. |
| D-368 | **A destructor could read a borrowed field after a plain local's scope ended.** A generic `Holder[T]` with a destructor, assigned into an outer `Option` from an inner scope, compiled without required E3060; the matching no-destructor control is valid. Plain local storage had no scope-end marker. A heap-backed String counterpart rejected with E3021 rather than the required E3060. | `[DRP-7]`, `[DRP-2]` | **fixed** | The implementation tracks plain-local and counted-loop-counter storage ends and distinguishes scope-exit drops from overwrites in MIR. Focused direct, generic payload, same-scope, return, break/continue, counter and guard cases pass. The mutation test that removes the lower fix reproduces the missing diagnostic; restoring it returns the focused set to green. Full MSVC and clang-cl workspace suites, all 18 gates, H39 examples (46, `failing 0`), and the 287-directory annotation sweep (`failing 0`) pass. Do not infer `needs_drop(Param)` as the cause. The adopted spec master is unchanged. |
| D-367 | **Combining static-library packages collided on generated C names.** Packages defining a same-named local record such as `HeaderLeaf` with different layouts, or the same structural `CFn`/`Option` shape, could collide in one host; private helper symbols also lacked internal linkage. | `[FFI-26]`, `[FFI-28]` | **fixed** | ADR-067 namespaces generated named and structural C types by package and gives private helpers internal linkage in static-library mode. A real linked C and C++ host test combines both package archives and the runtime archive, checking distinct `HeaderLeaf` layouts and duplicate function/option shapes; staticlib tests passed 3/3 and archive freshness passed 1/1. Header tests passed 3/3 under both MSVC and clang-cl. |
| D-366 | **A package named `ember_rt` made its generated header include itself.** With `package.name = 'ember_rt'` and an exported `i128` function, `--emit header` wrote `lib/ember_rt.h` containing a conditional `#include "ember_rt.h"`; a C host failed with unknown type `ember_i128`. Reproduced from the committed `fae734b` binary in `%TEMP%/ember-runtime-header-collision`. | `[FFI-26]`, `[FFI-9]` | **fixed** | ADR-066 places runtime support under `lib/ember_runtime/`, and generated 128-bit headers include `ember_runtime/ember_rt.h`. The linked C and C++ static-library host test compiles and runs with the package named `ember_rt`, exported 128-bit carriers, and the runtime archive. Header tests passed 3/3 under both MSVC and clang-cl. No language or ODR change was needed. |
| D-365 | **An ordinary function converted to a C record callback retained its native pointer ABI.** A non-Copy plain Pair argument becomes `Pair*` in its Ember body, but `extern "C" fn(Pair)` requires a C value. | `[FN-6]`, `[FFI-9]` | **fixed** | Native function values use descriptors with native and C entries; explicit conversion selects an ABI adapter that passes record values correctly while native calls retain borrowed-argument identity. Verified by `FFI-21/accept_native_callback_adapters.em` and the linked C-host milestone for Pair and 24-byte records, mutable arguments, record returns, void callbacks, and native identity. Full MSVC/clang-cl workspace suites, annotation sweep, and 18 gates passed. |
| D-364 | **Capture-free native function callbacks did not attach a foreign caller.** Named functions and capture-free lambdas used as C callbacks previously exposed raw native entry points without attaching. | `[FN-6]`, `[FFI-22]` | **fixed** | C callback adapters attach the calling thread before entering the native body, including for stored and runtime-selected conversions. Verified by `FFI-21/accept_native_callback_adapters.em`, `accept_stored_native_function_conversion.em`, and the linked host-thread milestone across debug, release, and shipping. Full MSVC/clang-cl workspace suites, annotation sweep, and 18 gates passed. |
| D-363 | **Directives after a leading comment were ignored.** The lexer recognized only a first-line directive, so a comment followed by `#! language "0.9.8"` bypassed the current-version check. A later thread directive would likewise be silently lost. | `[GRM-37]`, `[VER-8]` | **fixed** | Directives are lexical tokens on unindented lines; the parser accepts a sequence before imports/declarations and rejects late, duplicate and unknown directives with E0104. Regression cases reject an earlier language version and an unknown directive after a comment; combined language/thread directives and formatting roundtrip are covered. Break-test: restoring first-line-only recognition made the earlier-version case exit 0; restoring the fix produced E0006. |
| D-362 | **A plain class method could not write its own fields.** `fn replace(self): self.items = [2]` was rejected with `E1010` and `E3023`; even a `Copy` field assignment got `E3023`. `[CLS-7]` permits any class method to write through `self`, and `[EXC-16]` checks a non-`Copy` field at the store.
