The trigger was reachable after per-field accesses landed (2026-09-26). | `[CLS-7]`, `[EXC-16]`, `[EXC-17]`, `[EXC-19]` | **fixed** | Type checking tracks the class receiver in every method mode and exempts writes rooted in its object fields from the borrowed-parameter restriction, while keeping `let` and `pub(read)` rules. The existing MIR field access checks cover non-`Copy` writes. Verified: `CLS-7/accept_plain_class_method_writes_its_field.em` (assignment and `push`) and `CLS-7/accept_plain_class_method_writes_copy_field.em` both failed before the fix; `CLS-7/run_fail_plain_method_writes_a_viewed_field.em` panics when another handle writes a viewed field. |
| D-361 | **A `mut self` class method could re-point `self`.** `self = Counter(9)` inside `fn swap(mut self)` re-pointed the caller's handle; the rest of the method then wrote an object its caller did not hold, and the caller's access would have ended on the new object. Found fixing D-202 (2026-09-26). | `[CLS-7]`, `[EXC-15]` | **fixed** | ODR-072 (Hardened_29): assigning to `self` in a class method is `E2103`; re-pointing a caller's handle is a `mut` parameter's (`[FN-9]`). Verified: `CLS-7/reject_assigning_self_in_a_class_method.em`, and `CLS-7/accept_a_mut_self_method_writes_its_object.em` for the `mut` parameter. |
| D-360 | **Comparing or cloning a value nested by value hundreds of levels deep exhausted the stack.** A 256-level `Option[String]` comparison exited with `C00000FD` on Windows; its clone also moved an inner payload during matching, generated a large stack frame and hit MSVC's initializer-depth limit. Found fixing D-358 (2026-09-26). | `[GRM-39]`, `[STR-5]` | **fixed** | Generated equality functions borrow their operands by pointer. Tuple, `Option` and `Result` clones use per-type helpers that read a source pointer and write directly into the destination, including `Array` elements; the MIR reachability pass retains any implicit field clone they need. A nested enum-field match reads its place without moving it, and a unit-variant assignment zeroes storage and sets the tag without a deeply recursive C initializer. Verified: `GRM-39/accept_deep_option_equality.em` fails with `C00000FD` before the comparison fix and passes with a 256-level clone and comparison; `STR-5/accept_clone_of_option_with_implicit_struct_clone.em` fails without the reachability and helper changes. Full workspace suite passed on MSVC. |
| D-359 | **A `mut self` method calling another `mut self` method on `self` panicked.** `fn twice(mut self): self.bump()` panicked "exclusivity violation: overlapping access to em_Counter_bump" at `bump`'s entry: each `mut self` method took its whole object at its own entry, where a reborrow and an alias look alike. Found fixing D-202 (2026-09-26). | `[EXC-5]`, `[EXC-15]` | **fixed** | The whole-object write is taken by the caller around a `mut self` call, and a call on the caller's own `self` takes none (`unless_reborrow`); inside the method `self`'s fields are covered (`Body::mut_self`). Through an interface handle or a `dyn` box the dynamic adapter, which knows the class, takes it (ADR-060). Verified: `EXC-5/accept_a_mut_self_call_on_self_is_a_reborrow.em` panics before the fix; `EXC-5/run_fail_a_mut_self_call_on_an_alias_of_self.em`; `EXC-15/accept_a_mut_self_call_through_an_interface_writes_once.em` panics if the caller and the adapter both take the access. |
| D-358 | **Printing a deeply nested container generated C nested as deeply.** `println(x)` for an `Array` of `Array`s 256 deep failed clang ("bracket nesting level exceeded maximum of 256"; GCC compiled it). `[GRM-39]` requires 256 levels. Printing was already one helper per type: the nesting was the drop, each level's elements dropped in a loop inside the previous level's loop, an enum payload's in a `switch` inside its tag's, a `Box` payload behind one more dereference. Found fixing D-331 (2026-09-26). Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[GRM-39]`, `[CG-C-1]` | **fixed** | A part reached through an `Array` element, a `Box` payload or an enum payload whose own drop opens another level of nesting (`drop_nests`) is dropped by its own glue function (`drop_glue_call`), so no function nests more than one level. Verified: `GRM-39/accept_a_container_nested_256_deep.em` asserts no nested drop loop (`_di1`) and no nested `switch` in the C; without the fix its C nests 770 brackets deep and both assertions fail, with it 7. `DRP-6/accept_nested_boxes_drop_inside_out.em`'s order assertions follow the glue function. |
| D-357 | **A literal given to a function whose result has a field-by-field summary crashed the compiler.** `pair("x", "y")` for `fn pair(a: str, b: str) -> (str, str)` panicked in the callable-region verifier ("result provenance argument 0 field [] names no source region slot"): a literal has the `static` region and no region slot, and the verifier required one for every source the summary names. Found building SP-013 (`labels.entry("stop")`). Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[LT-22]`, `[LT-3]` | **fixed** | A literal, or a view read out of heap storage, is a `static` source with nothing to retain. Test: `LT-22/accept_literal_arguments_to_a_multi_region_result.em` (panics with the exemption removed). |
| D-356 | **A borrow through a reference to a view type counted as a borrow of the reference variable.** `ref e.a` with `e: ref Pair` (a local reference to a view struct) gave the loan the origin "`e`, a local", because `e`'s region slot's path already ends in the dereference, so nothing saw the borrow go through it. A function returning `(ref e.a, ref e.b)` had no field-by-field summary and its callers got `E3065`; `Map[str, V].items()` did (its `next` matches `ref e.key` through the entry). Found building SP-013. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[BRW-6]`, `[LT-22]` | **fixed** | A borrow whose place goes through a `Deref` borrows what the reference points to and carries the reference's origins (`borrows_own_storage`). Test: `LT-1/accept_a_borrow_through_a_reference_to_a_view.em` (fails with the fix removed). |
| D-355 | **An `Array` of `()` does not compile to C beyond indexing.** Printing one (`println(units)`), iterating it (`for u in units`, `units.as_span().iter()`) and reading an element generate C that assigns an `int` where an `ember_str` is expected, takes the address of an rvalue, or subscripts a `void*`; `units == other` is `E2040` naming the element type `void`; and every `Array[()]` uses `sizeof(void)`, which is 1 in GCC and Clang and 0 (with warning C4034) in MSVC. Found fixing D-354 (2026-09-26). Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[TYP-1]`, `[STD-15]` | **fixed** | `void` has one representation in the C backend (ADR-062): zero-sized in buffers and boxes (`c_size`/`c_align` answer 0 and 1 wherever the backend writes a size, `size_of[void]()` included; every element at the base pointer, `element_pointer`, D-354), a byte as a struct member or a parameter (`c_member_type`), `0` as a value, `&(uint8_t){0}` where a runtime call copies from its address. `void` is `Eq` and `Ord` and prints `()` (`[TYP-36]`); `remove`, `swap_remove` and `extend` of an `Array[void]` have their own helpers. The written type `()` is `void` (ODR-071, Hardened_29), so `Array[()]` and `Array[void]` are one type and diagnostics say `void`, as the specification names it. Verified: `STD-15/accept_an_array_of_void.em` (every method, `==`, `!=`, `sort`, `in`, no `sizeof(void)` in the C), `TYP-27/accept_the_type_written_as_unit.em`, `TYP-36/accept_void_prints_compares_and_orders.em`, `TYP-1/accept_void_in_fixed_arrays_maps_and_parameters.em`: each fails before the fix. |
| D-354 | **Indexing or splitting a view of a zero-sized element was arithmetic on `void*`.** An element of an `Array[()]` or a `Span[()]` was the C place `((void*)p.ptr)[i]`, and a split's second half `((void*)p.ptr) + k`: GCC and Clang accept both as an extension, MSVC rejects them (C2036). `get_pair_mut`'s test (SP-031) has an `Array[()]` and failed on the MSVC CI job of `953bd85`. | `[CG-C-1]`, `[BRW-5]` | **fixed** | Every element of a zero-sized type is the byte at the base pointer, and both halves of a split start there (ODR-068: distinct elements need not have distinct addresses). Verified: the test's C compiles under `clang -Werror=gnu-pointer-arith`, which fails before the fix; CI's MSVC job. |
| D-353 | **A class field, or a field of an `Array` element, takes a view that is not `static` (use after free in Safe code).** `[TYP-15]`: class fields and heap-collection elements "may hold only views whose every region is `static`". `class C: a: str` accepts `C(s.as_str())` and `c.a = s.as_str()` for a local `String` `s`, and `ps[0].a = s.as_str()` stores one into an element's field (only a whole element, `xs[i] = v`, is checked); after `s` is dropped the field reads freed memory. Found checking SP-013 (2026-09-26). Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[TYP-15]`, `[LT-3]` | **fixed** | Every store is followed to where it lands (`store_target` in `regions.rs`), and a store into a class object's field, an `Array`'s element (or a field of one), a span's element or a `Box`/`Shared` takes only `static` views (`stores.rs`, ODR-069); a constructor's argument reaches the field through the callee's summary. Tests: `TYP-15/reject_a_non_static_view_in_a_class_object.em`, `TYP-15/reject_a_non_static_view_through_an_element.em` (each fails with the requirement check removed). |
| D-352 | **A view stored through a `mut` parameter, a `ref mut`, `mem.replace` or `mem.swap` can outlive what it views (use after free in Safe code).** `fn put(mut x: str, v: str): x = v`, then `put(name, s.as_str())`, `mem.drop(s)`, `println(name)` reads freed memory; so do `mem.replace(name, s.as_str())`, `mem.swap(name, v)`, `r = ref mut name; r = s.as_str()`, a struct field written through a `mut` parameter, and each of these aimed at an `Array[str]` element (D-198's other half). The region check follows a view into a local it is assigned to (`[LT-21]`), but not into the place behind a reference or a `mut` parameter, and a heap element has no region at all. `[TYP-15]`: a view may be stored only in a place bounded by every region it carries. Found checking SP-013 (2026-09-26). Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[TYP-15]`, `[LT-21]`, `[FN-1]` | **fixed** | A store through a reference joins the place its loan borrows (or a reference parameter's slot, which `return` checks), as a weak update; a callee's stores reach its callers through its store summary (`static` slots, and flows from one argument into another's place), and `mem.replace`/`mem.swap` have fixed flows (ADR-059). Tests: `TYP-15/reject_a_view_stored_through_a_mut_parameter.em`, `TYP-15/reject_a_view_stored_by_mem_replace_or_swap.em`, `TYP-15/reject_swapped_fields_outliving_their_source.em`, `TYP-15/reject_a_local_view_stored_in_the_callers_place.em`, `TYP-15/reject_a_closure_storing_a_parameter_in_a_capture.em`, and `TYP-15/accept_static_views_stored_every_way.em`; each break (flows, loans followed, summaries, caller places, dynamic bodies) turns one red. |
| D-351 | **An `Array` had a `split_at_mut` the specification says does not exist.** `[SPN-5]` (F-075): "There is no separate `split_at_mut`"; an `Array` is split mutably by splitting its mutable view, `a.as_mut_span().split_at(i)`, which the compiler also accepted. The compiler kept a builtin `Array.split_at_mut` of its own, and shape B1's help named it. Found writing SP-031's help. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[SPN-5]`, `[DIA-10]` | **fixed** | The builtin is gone (typeck, HIR, MIR, the C backend); `xs.split_at_mut(k)` is `E1010` whose help is `.as_mut_span().split_at(k)`, and B1's help names that and `get_pair_mut`. Six tests that used it now split the view. Test: `SPN-5/reject_split_at_mut_on_an_array.em` (fails, on its help, with the diagnostic removed). |
| D-350 | **A `mut self` method of a `MutSpan` did not compile to C, and a view-producing receiver was refused.** A `mut` parameter of a view type is the view itself (`mut_param_ty`), but a `mut self` receiver of a `MutSpan` was passed as `ref mut MutSpan`, so `extend[T] MutSpan[T]: fn f(mut self)` produced C that did not compile; and `buf.as_mut_span().f()` was `E2140` ("needs a variable"), though `[FN-1a]` lets a `mut` view parameter take a view-producing expression. Found writing SP-031's `MutSpan.get_pair_mut`. | `[FN-1a]`, `[SPN-3]` | **fixed** | The receiver is passed as the view, as a `mut` argument of that type is; a view-producing receiver is passed as it is. Tests: `BRW-5/accept_get_pair_mut_of_a_mutable_view.em`, and every use of `Array.get_pair_mut`, which calls its view's. |
| D-349 | **Re-pointing a `mut` handle parameter panicked.** `fn repoint(mut n: Node): n = Node(42)` panicked "exclusivity violation: overlapping access to em_repoint" for any argument, a local included: MIR opened `mut self`'s whole-object write access at entry for any function whose first parameter was a `mut` class handle, and replacing the handle released the object inside that access. `[FN-9]`: `mut` on a handle re-points the handle and opens nothing on the object. Found in the SP-010 review. | `[FN-9]`, `[EXC-15]` | **fixed** | The entry access is taken only for a parameter named `self`. Test: `FN-9/accept_a_mut_handle_parameter_repoints_a_field_safely.em`. |
| D-348 | **A borrow through a handle stored in an object outlived the object (use after free in Safe code).** `r = ref p.child.v`, then `q.child = Node(2)` through another handle `q` of the same object: the old child was released and freed while `r` pointed into it, and `r` then read whatever reused the memory (`99`, from `Node(99)` allocated next). The same through a `Copy` struct field holding a handle (`h.pair.a.v`), through a method called on `p.child` whose body overwrites `p.child` (`self` freed mid-call; a `mut self` receiver instead became the new object and the call ended with an exclusivity panic), and through a `mut` handle parameter. `[RC-5]` keeps an object alive for a borrow through a handle, which only the borrow checker enforced, and it cannot see writes through other handles; `[EXC-17]` gives a `Copy` field (a handle is `Copy`) no access word. Found by the owner-requested review of SP-010's access question (ODR-051's review gate). | `[RC-5]`, `[EXC-17]`, `[PHIL-1]` | **fixed** | ODR-065. MIR lowering: a borrow of or through a handle stored where another handle can overwrite it (`handle_in_shared_memory`: in a class object's field or element, or behind a mutable reference other than a `mut` parameter) goes through a retained copy in a statement temporary (`keep_handle_alive`, `lower_borrowed_place`, via `place_overrides`); a borrowed argument or receiver read from such a place is passed the copy (`lower_operand_borrowed`); a `mut` argument is too, stored back after the call unless it is `self` (`lower_mut_argument_with_access`, `write_backs`), so a `mut` parameter never points into an object and needs no copy in the callee. A borrow kept past its statement outlives the copy: `E3060`, with a help naming the handle. The loop-hoisting proof (`[EXC-8]`) accepts the receiver copy and its release, which runs no code when the modelled callee leaves the object's fields alone. Two run-pass tests pinned the unsafe no-retain call (`class_interface_handle_field_mut.em`, now two retains) and two `EXC-8` tests the loop shape before the copy. Tests: `RC-5/reject_a_borrow_through_a_stored_handle_past_its_statement.em`, `RC-5/accept_a_handle_stored_in_an_object_is_kept_for_a_call.em`, `FN-9/accept_a_mut_handle_parameter_repoints_a_field_safely.em`. |
| D-347 | **`mem.drop` of a class handle ended nothing.** `mem.drop(owned value: T)` is ordinary Ember; a handle is `Copy`, so the call received a retained copy and released only that, and the variable kept the object to the end of its scope: `a = Node(1); mem.drop(a); println("after")` printed `after` before `drop 1`, and `a` stayed usable. `[OWN-6]`: it drops now; SP-014 named this interaction. Found in the same review. | `[OWN-6]`, `[RC-3]` | **fixed** | `mem.drop` is `hir::Builtin::MemDrop`, which MIR lowers as a move of the argument into a temporary the statement's end drops; a local is moved even when its type is `Copy` and needs drop, so `[OWN-3]` marks it moved (`E3040` on a later use) and it is not dropped again. Tests: `OWN-6/reject_a_handle_used_after_mem_drop.em`, `FN-9/accept_an_owned_handle_argument_the_caller_keeps.em`. |
| D-346 | **`L1001` called a handle only written through "never read".** `q = p; q.child = Node(2)` warned that `q` is never read, though the assignment reads `q` to reach the object. The lint counted any assignment into a place rooted at a local as a write to that local, which is right for a value's field and wrong for a class handle's. Found writing D-348's tests. | `[LNT-1]` | **fixed** | `unused::check` takes the type table and counts an assignment through a class handle as a read of the handle. Test: `RC-5/reject_a_borrow_through_a_stored_handle_past_its_statement.em` (no `L1001`). |
| D-345 | **An associated type reached through a bound is shown as `Self.Out`.** In `fn f[T: Combine](a: T, b: T) -> T: return a.combine(b)`, the error says "expected `T`, found `Self.Out`"; it should say `T.Out`. The type is right (abstract, as `[IFC-4]` says); only its display is. A bound method's result keeps the interface's `Assoc` kind unless the signature declared a projection parameter for it. Found writing ODR-049's tests; also without a default. | `[IFC-4]`, `[DIA-1]` | **fixed** | `project` makes the hidden parameter for `T.Out` when a body first reaches it through a bound (`GenericParam.projection`, bounded as `Out` is declared), so the value reads `T.Out` and its bounds' operations work; only the body sees it, no caller fills it in. An operator's unsaid `Output` keeps `[IFC-4]`'s `E2040` at the operator (`no_lazy_projection`). Test: `IFC-4/reject_code_that_assumes_an_associated_default.em` (was matched by prefix). |
| D-344 | **An interface's default body was duck-typed.** A default method was checked only as each implementing type's copy, with `Self` that type, so `fn shout(self) -> int: return self.secret()` compiled when the implementer happened to have `secret`, a default body no type implemented was never checked, and one could assume an associated type's default (ODR-049). `[TYP-17]`: only what the bounds provide. Found implementing ODR-049. | `[TYP-17]`, `[IFC-4]` | **fixed** | `check_default_bodies_opaquely`: each default body is checked once with `Self` an opaque parameter bounded by the interface (its parents closed, its associated names in scope as themselves, the receiver prepended to the declaration's signature); each implementation's copy is then checked quietly and reports only what the opaque check could not see (`check_default_copy`), as D-280 does for generic methods. Five `TYP-22` tests whose never-implemented default bodies were `pass` in a function returning `Self` (`E2182` now) got `todo()` bodies. Tests: `TYP-17/reject_a_default_body_uses_only_what_its_interface_provides.em`, `IFC-4/reject_code_that_assumes_an_associated_default.em`. |
| D-343 | **`alloc_array` in a generic body ignored the type parameter's bounds.** `fn make[T: Default](a: Arena, n: int) -> MutSpan[T]: return a.alloc_array[T](n)` was `E2040` "`T` has no available `Zeroable` or `Default` capability": the capability was looked up only among written implementations, which a type parameter has none of. Found checking ODR-064's compatibility. | `[ARN-3]`, `[TYP-17]` | **fixed** | `default_initialisation` answers a type parameter from its bounds (`implements`), and `zeroable` from a `Zeroable` bound; the body is checked again per instance, where `T` is known. Tests: `ARN-3/accept_alloc_array_calls_default_and_alloc_zeroed_fills_zeros.em` (`T: Default` and `T: Zeroable` wrappers), `reject_alloc_array_without_default_and_alloc_zeroed_without_zeroable.em`. |
| D-342 | **A temporary without a destructor may be borrowed past its statement.** `[EXP-4]` drops a statement's temporaries at its end, but `r = inner(W3(3))` followed by `println(r)`, where `inner(w: W3) -> ref i64` returns `ref w.item` and `W3` has no `drop`, compiles: nothing ends the temporary's storage for the borrow check, which sees the end only through a destructor (with a `String` field, the same program is `E3060`). The C local lives to the end of the function, so it does not fail at run time today. Found testing D-284; the generic form behaves the same. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[EXP-4]`, `[BRW-9]` | **fixed** | A statement's temporaries that need no drop and hold nothing that points elsewhere (no handle, reference, view, pointer or heap storage) end with a `StorageDead` at the statement's end, and the borrow check treats that end like a drop, against the loans on the temporary's own storage. Restricting it to such plain temporaries is what the first attempt lacked: a borrow of a temporary holding a handle points through it, and those are never ended. One conformance program relied on the defect (`Window(items = [1, 2, 3])`, a span of a temporary array kept in `w`, `[TYP-38]`) and now binds the array first. Tests: `EXP-4/reject_a_borrow_of_a_plain_temporary_past_its_statement.em` (red with either half removed), `EXP-4/accept_a_borrow_of_a_plain_temporary_within_its_statement.em`. |
| D-341 | **A generic enum's variant needed the enum's arguments written.** `Maybe.Just(3)`, and `Maybe.Nothing` where a `Maybe[int]` is expected, were `E1010` "cannot find `Maybe`"; only `Maybe[int].Just(3)` compiled, though `[TYP-18]` infers generic arguments from a call's. Found testing D-305. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[TYP-18]`, `[ENM-1]` | **fixed** | `synth_inferred_variant` solves the arguments from the expected type, then from the payload's values (checked without keeping their diagnostics), as D-334 does for a struct's associated function; a variant without a payload and no type expected is `E2020` naming both fixes. Tests: `TYP-18/accept_a_generic_enums_arguments_found_at_a_variant.em`, `TYP-18/reject_a_generic_enums_arguments_not_found.em`. |
| D-340 | **A generic body did not get a bounded extension of an instance over its parameter.** In `struct Outer[T: Eq]` holding an `Inner[T, bool]`, the methods of `extend[K: Eq, V] Inner[K, V] implements Make` were not there: the instance was made while the types were collected, when no declaration's bounds were in scope, so `T: Eq` was not known and the extension was refused, and it was never offered again. Found writing `Set`'s owned iteration over its `Map`. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[TYP-17]`, `[GRM-34]` | **fixed** | An instance whose arguments include a type parameter is offered the extensions it lacks again at each use inside a generic body (`extend_instance_at_use`, which built-in instances already went through), where `implements` sees the body's bounds. An extension so taken stays with that instance, and type parameters are shared across declarations, so another generic body naming the same instance without the bound can see its methods; its instances are checked again with concrete types, so the mistake is reported where it is instantiated. Tests: `TYP-17/accept_a_bounded_extension_on_an_instance_over_a_parameter.em`, `TYP-17/reject_an_extension_a_parameter_does_not_meet.em`. |
| D-339 | **`std`'s extensions of the built-in types were emitted into every program.** The driver drops the `std` bodies nothing reaches, and knew them by the `std.` prefix of their symbols; a body from an `extend` of a built-in type in `std` is named after the type (`i64_default`, `str_is_key`), so every one was kept: about twenty in each program, and `NonZero`'s division added 24 more. Found reading `NonZero`'s generated C. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[MOD-5]` | **fixed** | A body is `std`'s when its symbol is in `std`'s namespace or it was written in one of `std`'s files (`retain_referenced_standard_bodies`, given the `std` modules' files). A program that prints `1` now emits `main` and nothing else. Test: `MOD-5/accept_an_unused_std_extension_is_not_emitted.em`. |
| D-338 | **Another instance of a number's operator interface took its operator method.** ODR-040 makes `x.rem(y)` on a number the built-in `%` unless the type has a method of that name registered; once `std.core` implemented `Rem[NonZero[i64]]` (or a program `Rem[Steps]`), every `x.rem(y)` went to that instance, and `7.rem(2)` was `E2020`, "expected `NonZero[i64]`". The operator `x % d` already chose by the operand's type. Present since ODR-040. | `[TYP-21]`, `[TYP-24]`, ODR-040 | **fixed** | `method_claims_operator`: an instance over another right-hand type takes the call only when its argument is not the number's own type. Test: `TYP-21/accept_a_numbers_operator_method_beside_another_instance.em`. |
| D-337 | **A missed bound was reported again for each parent it brings.** `zero_of(Plain(1))` with `T: Whole` and `interface Whole: Default + Hash` was three `E2040`s, for `Whole`, `Default` and `Hash`: the parents `[IFC-3]` adds to a bound were checked as if written. D-335 would have done the same to a generic type's instances (`NonZero[f64]`). Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[DIA-14]`, `[IFC-3]` | **fixed** | `missed_bounds` reports a missed bound and skips the parents it brought, at a generic function's call, a generic method's call and a generic type's instance. Test: `IFC-3/reject_a_missed_bound_is_one_error.em`. |
| D-336 | **A derived `Clone` could be settled before the fields' `drop`s were collected.** Making an instance of a generic type that derives `Clone` resolved every pending derived `Clone`; an instance made while the types were still being collected (an extension naming `Tag[i32]`) settled them all early, and a `@derive(Clone)` over a field whose type has a `drop` was accepted. `std.core`'s `extend i8 implements FloorDiv[NonZero[i8]]` did this in every program: two `OWN-8` cases passed compilation. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[OWN-8]`, `[STR-5]`, ODR-026 | **fixed** | An instance made before the collection passes resolve the derived `Clone`s waits for them (`clones_resolved`, `resolve_instance_clones`). Test: `OWN-8/reject_derive_clone_settled_before_the_drops_are_known.em`. |
| D-335 | **A generic type's bound did not bring its parents.** `[IFC-3]`: with `interface Whole: Default`, `T: Whole` lets a body call `T.default()`. A generic function's parameters had that; a generic struct's, enum's or class's did not, because their recipes are collected before the interfaces (whose signatures may name them), when no interface had parents to bring. Found writing `NonZero[T: Integer]` with `interface Integer: Hash + Default`. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[IFC-3]`, `[TYP-17]` | **fixed** | `close_recipe_bounds`, run once the interfaces are collected, closes each recipe's parameters' bounds, and its methods' own, over their parents (`close_bounds`, which `declare_generics_from` shares). Test: `IFC-3/accept_a_generic_types_bound_brings_its_parents.em`. |
| D-334 | **A generic struct's associated function needed the struct's arguments written.** `Pair.make(5)` was `E2020`; only `Pair[i64].make(5)` compiled, though a generic function's arguments are found from its call. Found writing `NonZero.new(5)`, which `[STD-4]` writes without arguments. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[TYP-16]`, `[TYP-18]`, `[STD-4]` | **fixed** | `synth_inferred_associated_call` solves the parameters from the type the context expects, then from the arguments (checked without keeping their diagnostics, a literal taking its default type), and calls the instance's function; a parameter left unsolved is `E2020` with the fix, `Stack[…].empty(…)`. Tests: `TYP-16/accept_a_generic_types_arguments_found_at_an_associated_call.em`, `TYP-16/reject_a_generic_types_arguments_not_found.em`. |
| D-333 | **`Self` inside another type stayed unresolved in a generic type's methods.** In `struct Cell2[T]`, a method returning `Option[Self]` or taking `Array[Self]` kept the placeholder: `TypeTable::substitute_self` replaced a bare `Self` only. Found writing `NonZero.new`. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[STR-7]` | **fixed** | The checker's `substitute_self` substitutes through the whole type: `substitute_ty` replaces `Self` wherever it stands while `substituting_self` holds the instance, and all 25 of the checker's uses go through it. Test: `STR-7/accept_self_inside_another_type.em`. |
| D-332 | **`i128`, `u128` and `f16` had no `Default`.** `[TYP-36]` gives every number a `Default`, its zero; `std.core` implemented it for the numbers it had before D-272 and D-316, so `T.default()` and a `T: Default` bound refused the three. Found making `NonZero[i128]`. Present since D-272 and D-316. | `[TYP-36]` | **fixed** | `std.core` implements `Default` for the three. Test: `TYP-36/accept_every_number_has_a_default.em`. |
| D-331 | **A deep enough expression aborts the compiler.** Nothing limits how deeply an expression nests, and nothing reports it: past what the compiler's stack holds (about four thousand levels of a binary operator in a debug build of the compiler, more in a release build), the compiler ends with "has overflowed its stack" and no diagnostic. Found fixing D-330. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[GRM-39]`, `[TOOL-1]` | **fixed** | ODR-070 (`[GRM-39]`): every compiler accepts 256 levels of nesting and states its own limit; this one accepts 1,024, and passing it is `E0112`, once, never a crash. The parser counts its own nesting (expressions, blocks, types) and skips the rest of the file past the limit; the type checker counts expression depth, which catches operator and method-call chains the parser builds without recursing. Both limits sit well inside the compiler's stack (about 4,000 levels in a debug build). Tests: `GRM-39/accept_256_levels_of_nesting.em`, `GRM-39/reject_nesting_past_the_limit.em`, `GRM-39/reject_an_operator_chain_past_the_limit.em` (red without the type checker's count), `GRM-39/reject_a_type_nested_past_the_limit.em` (red without the parser's). |
| D-330 | **The compiler's stack depended on the host.** The compiler ran on the main thread: 1 MB of stack on Windows, 8 MB on Linux. Checking recurses once for each level of an expression, and a debug build of the compiler spends about 65 KB on a level of a binary operator, so about fifteen levels fitted on Windows and about a hundred on Linux. `std.math.det`'s polynomials are twelve deep: both Windows jobs failed on `a79e1a5`, with `thread 'main' has overflowed its stack` for both `DET-4` cases, and Linux passed. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[TOOL-1]`, Part XX §2 (Phase 0 CI) | **fixed** | `main` runs the compiler on a thread of its own with a 256 MB stack, reserved rather than committed, so every host holds the same depth (ADR-055); a panic on it ends the process as before. Test: `run-pass/a_deep_expression_compiles_on_every_host.em`, a sum four hundred levels deep, which overflowed on Linux too before the fix. |
| D-329 | **A `const` could not bound a range type.** `type Offset = i32 in -LIMIT ..= LIMIT` with `const LIMIT = 20` was `E2212` ("a range endpoint must be a constant of the representation type"), though the checker's own comment said a `const` is accepted: range types are collected before constants, so the lookup found nothing. Found writing D-327's test. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[RNG-1]` | **fixed** | The first pass records each item `const`'s value as written (`const_values`); `const_bound` reads a named constant's own value, in its module, through any chain of constants (sixteen deep at most, against a cycle). Test: `RNG-1/accept_a_constant_bounds_a_range.em`. |
| D-328 | **A method is callable from any module, `pub` or not.** A method declared without `pub` in a type's body or an `extend` block, including one implementing a private interface, is found and called from another module; `[MOD-2]` makes a member private to its module unless it is marked. Found writing `std.math.det`, whose private `Deterministic` interface's `det_sin` is callable as `x.det_sin()`. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[MOD-2]` | **fixed** | Method lookup checks the callee's visibility (`check_method_visible`, from `synth_registered_method` and `synth_associated_call`): `E1052` "`twice` is private to `support.methods`", as for a field or an item. `method_vis` records each method's visibility and module where it is registered (`collect_members_inner`, `register_recipe_method`); a method implementing an interface takes the interface's (`member_visibility`, and `adopt_interface_visibility` for a type's header), so `det_sin` is private to `std.math.det` and `DefaultHasher.write_u8` is as public as `Hasher`. A generic instance's method implementing an interface its owner implements is looked up through `implemented`. The compiler's own routings through `std` helpers (`sort_ord`, `sorted_ord`) are exempt (`routed_call`). The `std` audit: of the non-`pub` methods on public types, only `DefaultHasher.new` is meant for users (`pub` now); `mix` and `Map`'s `hash_of`, `find`, `place`, `rebuild`, `push_new`, `forget` are helpers. Tests: `MOD-2/reject_a_private_method_from_another_module.em` (a method, an associated function, a private interface's method, a generic type's method), `accept_a_pub_method_and_an_interface_method_anywhere.em`. ADR-057. |
| D-327 | **A negative literal constant was rejected.** `const N: i64 = -5`, `const F = -1.5` and every other `const` whose value was a minus on a number were `E2130`, "a `const` must be a literal in this phase", and each use then "cannot find" it: the checked value is a negation, not a literal node. `-(1.5)` compiled, by another path. Found writing `std.math.det`, whose coefficients are negative. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | V.7, `[LEX-24]` | **fixed** | A negation of a numeric literal is a literal constant (`is_literal_value`), copied at each use (`literal_copy`) and read as a range bound; untyped, it stays untyped (ODR-037). Test: `LEX-24/accept_a_negative_literal_constant.em`. |
| D-326 | **W2015 missed a literal under a minus or inside arithmetic.** `x: f32 = -0.1234567891234` and `x: f32 = 0.1234567891234 * 2.0` compiled without the warning: the check looked only at an expression that was itself a literal, and a literal inside untyped arithmetic takes `f32` only when the whole does. Found writing D-324's test. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[LEX-17a]` | **fixed** | The check walks into parentheses, a minus and `+ - * / // %`, whose operands take the result's type, and warns each literal once: one already warned in the sink is not warned again, so a warning a speculative check made and took back is made again. Test: `LEX-17a/accept_the_warning_names_what_the_type_keeps.em`. |
| D-325 | **The C compiler could fuse `a * b + c`.** Nothing turned floating-point contraction off: no `#pragma STDC FP_CONTRACT OFF` and no `-ffp-contract=off`, which `[CG-C-11]` requires. clang's default (`-ffp-contract=on`) fuses a multiply and an add in one C expression wherever the target has an FMA instruction (every arm64 machine; x86-64 built with `-march=native`), so a float result depended on the machine, against `[TYP-9]`. It did not show in CI, whose x86-64 builds target a processor without FMA. Found checking `[STD-3]`'s fused matrix products. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[CG-C-11]`, `[TYP-9]`, `[TYP-9a]` | **fixed** | Every generated C file and the runtime's begin with the pragma: clang's `#pragma STDC FP_CONTRACT OFF`, MSVC's `#pragma fp_contract(off)` (codegen's `FP_STRICT`); gcc does not implement the pragma and warns about it under `-Wall`, so it has the flag alone (ODR-044). clang and gcc are given `-ffp-contract=off -fno-fast-math`, MSVC `/fp:precise`. Tests: `ember_build`'s `the_c_compiler_never_fuses_a_multiply_and_an_add` (compiles `a * b + c` for a processor with FMA and looks for the instruction, with a control compile that fuses), `TYP-9/accept_a_multiply_and_an_add_are_two_roundings.em`. |
| D-324 | **W2015's label said nothing.** `0.1000000000000000055511151231257827` at `f32` was labelled `` `0.1` becomes `0.1` ``: both sides were printed as the shortest text of their own type, and the `f32`'s shortest text reads back as the literal. Found writing constants' tests. Introduced with W2015 in the 0.9.9 work. | `[LEX-17a]` | **fixed** | The label shows what the type keeps, in full: `` `0.1` becomes `0.10000000149011612` ``. Test: `LEX-17a/accept_the_warning_names_what_the_type_keeps.em`. |
| D-323 | **A type's `const` was ignored, and a `const` could only be a literal.** V.3's own example, `const ZERO: Vec2 = Vec2(0, 0)` in `struct Vec2`, did not compile: `Vec2.ZERO` was "cannot find", and an item `const` whose value was not a literal was `E2130`, "must be a literal in this phase" (`E2130` is a `const` of a type that owns heap memory). Found writing `std.math`'s vectors (A4). Present before the 0.9.9 work. | V.3, V.7, `[CT-1]`, `[CT-7]`, `[MOD-2]` | **fixed** | A `const` is any constant expression this phase can evaluate: literals, other constants, arithmetic, comparisons, `and`, `or`, `not`, tuples, arrays, and the construction of structs and enums. It is evaluated while compiling (`const_eval::fold`, ADR-052), each operation as the target does it, and each use is the value's literals; an overflow, a division by zero or a shift out of range is `E6004` at the declaration. Constants are evaluated at their first use or when collection ends (`settle_const`, `settle_consts`), so they may name each other in any order; one whose value needs itself is `E6001` (ODR-045). A type's `const` is `T.NAME`, `Self.NAME` inside it, and private to its module unless `pub` (`E1052`). A `const` of a type that is not `Copy`, or that owns heap memory, is `E2130` with a `static` for help; a value this phase cannot work out (a call) is `E1010`. Tests: `CT-1/accept_constant_expressions.em`, `CT-1/reject_a_constant_that_depends_on_itself.em`, `CT-1/reject_what_a_constant_cannot_hold.em`, `CT-7/accept_a_constant_at_the_edge_does_not_panic.em`, `CT-7/reject_a_constant_that_panics.em`, `MOD-2/reject_a_private_constant_of_a_type.em`. |
| D-322 | **`x is None` was rejected, and `is` on a value gave the wrong error.** `o is None` and `o is not None` on an `Option`, `[EXP-9]`'s option test, were `E2020` ("requires related class handles"); `a is b` on two references was rejected the same way; and `1 is 1` was `E2020` where `[EXP-9]` names `E2150`, whose help is `==`. Found writing `std.math`'s tests (`m.try_inverse() is None`). Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[EXP-9]` | **fixed** | `x is None` and `x is not None`, with `None` on either side and through a reference too, match the option's variant (`option_test`). Two references compare where they point: `synth` reads a reference through, so the reference is taken back from under that read (`referent`, `the_reference`); references to two types are `E2020`, and a reference to a class handle compares the handles. Any other operand is `E2150`, with `==` for help. Tests: `EXP-9/accept_is_tests_options_and_identity.em`, `EXP-9/reject_is_on_values.em`, `compile-fail/class_identity_nonclass.em`. |
| D-321 | **An extension whose parameter only its interface named was never applied, silently.** `extend[K: Eq + Hash, V, Q: AsKey[K]] Map[K, V] implements Index[Q]` compiled and did nothing: an instance binds an extension's parameters from its target, and one the target does not name was never bound, so the extension matched no instance and no error said so. Found writing `Map`'s `Index` (A3). Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[GRM-34]`, ODR-042 | **fixed** | Such a parameter makes a blanket implementation (ODR-042, ADR-051): the checker moves it onto each method as its own generic parameter (`desugar_blanket_extensions`), keeps a `BlanketImpl` record, and registers the implementation for a type and an interface instance when a bound needs it (`apply_blanket`), checking the methods and associated types then (`check_blanket_instance`). Tests: `GRM-34/accept_a_blanket_implementation.em`, `GRM-34/reject_a_blanket_bound_not_met.em`, `GRM-34/reject_a_blanket_implementation_that_does_not_fit.em`, `TYP-17/accept_indexing_through_a_bound.em`. |
| D-320 | **Indexing called any method named `index`.** `b[0]` on a type with an inherent `index` method compiled to it, as `a + b` did with any `add` (D-315); likewise `index_mut` for a write and `index_set` for `a[i] = v`. `[TYP-40]` makes interfaces nominal, and the prelude's `Index`, `IndexMut` and `IndexSet` (`[MOD-5]`) were not declared at all. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[TYP-21]`, `[TYP-40]`, `[STD-17]` | **fixed** | `std.core` declares the three (Part IV §8) and the prelude exports them. `a[i]` needs an `Index` implementation, written or blanket (`implements_origin`), a write in place `IndexMut`, and `a[i] = v` `IndexSet` where the type has one; `Map`'s indexing is its blanket implementations (ODR-042). Tests: `TYP-21/accept_indexing_through_the_interfaces.em`, `TYP-21/reject_indexing_without_the_interface.em`. |
| D-319 | **A float literal is rounded twice.** A literal becomes an `f64` in the lexer and is rounded again to `f32` or `f16` when it takes that type, so a literal just past a midpoint between two `f32`s, but closer to it than an `f64` can tell, lands on the midpoint and rounds to even: `x: f32 = 1.00000005960464477539062500000001` is `1.0`, where the nearest `f32` is `1.0000001` (as `parse[f32]()` gives). It takes seventeen or more significant digits, and W2015 warns about such a literal anyway. Found while fixing D-316. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[LEX-17]`, `[TYP-9]` | **fixed** | The literal keeps its text (`Lit::Float`, `Literal::Float`), and where it takes `f32` or `f16` it is rounded once from it: `f32` by Rust's correctly rounded parse, `f16` by `ember_types::f16_bits_of_decimal`, which settles the one case an `f64` cannot (its value exactly halfway between two `f16`s) by comparing the decimal with the midpoint exactly. An integer literal at a float type is rounded once too. `W2015` shows the once-rounded value. Tests: `TYP-9/accept_a_literal_is_rounded_once.em` (red with either half removed) and the unit test `f16_of_a_decimal_is_rounded_once`. |
| D-318 | **A float range type counted as `Hash`.** `type R = f32 in 0.25 ..= 0.75`: `R.checked(0.6).is_ok()` did not compile (`E1010`, "`f32` has no method named `hash`", in `std/src/collections.em`), since `Result[R, RangeError]` took `Result`'s `Hash` implementation, whose body hashes an `f32`; a float range was also a `Map` key. The checker counted every range type as hashable, though `[TYP-36]` gives floats no `Hash`. Found testing `f16` ranges (D-316). Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[TYP-36]`, `[RNG-3]` | **fixed** | A range type hashes as its representation does (`has_builtin_eq_hash`, `hashes`); its `Eq` is still the implicit one. | D-317 | **A generic extension's associated type was never recorded.** `extend[T, H] Set[T, H] implements BitOr: type Output = Set[T, H]` was `E2040` "`Set[T, H]` implements `BitOr` but does not say what `Output` is": only a non-generic block's `type Name = …` reached the checker, and a generic extension dropped it, so no generic type could implement an interface with an associated type through an extension. Found moving `Set`'s operators to their interfaces (D-315). Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[IFC-4]`, `[GRM-34]` | **fixed** | `GenericExtension.assoc` keeps each `type Name = …` over the extension's parameters, and applying the extension to an instance, concrete or open, records it for that instance and for each interface of the block that declares it (`record_extension_assoc`), before the implementation is checked. Test: `IFC-4/accept_one_associated_type_for_several_interfaces.em` (`Set[int]`'s `\|`, `&` and `-`). |
| D-316 | **`f16` arithmetic works on truncated integers.** `a: f16 = 1.5` holds 1, and `a + b`, `a * b`, `a / b` and `-a` are integer operations on the `uint16_t` the value is stored in: with `a = 1.5` and `b = 2.25`, `-a` prints `65535.0`, `a * b` `2.0` and `a / b` `0.0`. The C backend's comment says `f16` arithmetic is done in `f32` and rounded; it is not. `f16` is also missing from `std.math`'s `Number`, though ODR-038 says `std.math` takes any number type. Found writing ODR-040's memberships. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[TYP-9]`, IV.2's table (IEEE binary16), `[TYP-5]` rule 2, `[TYP-6]`, `[TYP-37]`, ODR-038 | **fixed** | ADR-050. The runtime has `ember_f16_to_f64` (exact) and `ember_f64_to_f16` (ties to even, from the bits); the backend widens every `f16` operation to `double` and rounds back once (`half`, `half_operand`, `half_cast`), a constant is its bits (`ember_types::f16_bits`), and `-x` flips the sign bit. Printing is the fewest digits that read back (five at most), `f"{x:…}"` formats the exact value, `parse[f16]()` rounds once from the text (a `double` exactly on a midpoint is settled by the text), and sorting is totalOrder (`ember_total_lt_f16`). `f16` has the float constants and is a `Number` answering in `f32` (ODR-041, Hardened_18); an `f16` range clamps between `f16` endpoints. W2015 names the value an `f16` literal becomes. Tests: `TYP-9/accept_f16_arithmetic.em`, `TYP-6/accept_f16_casts.em`, `TXT-10/accept_parse_f16.em`, `TYP-37/accept_f16_total_order.em`, `STD-27/accept_f16_is_a_number.em`, `RNG-3a/accept_clamped_on_an_f16_range.em`; `ember_types`' `f16_bits_rounds_to_nearest_even` and `f16_text_is_the_fewest_digits_that_read_back`. |

Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[TYP-21]`, `[TYP-40]` | **fixed** | An operator is found only through an implementation of its prelude interface (`operator_implemented`: `std.core.Add` in any instance) for a binary operator, `-`, `~`, `**` and `op=`, and its error notes the interface to implement. `Set`'s `\|`, `&`, `-` and `^` are declared through `BitOr`, `BitAnd`, `Sub` and `BitXor` in `std/src/collections.em`, and `tests/run-pass/methods_and_interfaces.em`, which declared an `interface Add` of its own, implements the prelude's. Test: `TYP-21/reject_an_operator_the_type_does_not_implement.em`. |

Found giving the numbers their `Neg` (ODR-040). Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[TYP-8]` | **fixed** | D-192's `-x` → `0 - x` rewrite, whose subtraction is checked, covers the unsigned types too, in the operator and in `x.neg()`: `-x` is `0` at zero and panics otherwise. | D-313 | **A type could not implement two instances of one generic interface.** `extend Mat2 implements Mul[Vec2]` beside `extend Mat2 implements Mul[Mat2]` failed: the blocks' methods were registered as inherent (`E1030`, `mul` declared twice), then as two interfaces offering one name (`E2070`), each body was checked against the other instance's signature, and a call did not choose by its argument's type. Part IV §8 declares `Mul[Rhs = Self]` for exactly this. Found writing the operator interfaces. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | Part IV §8, `[TYP-24]`, `[IFC-4]` | **fixed** | A block's interfaces are resolved as the instances they are for its type (`block_interfaces_of`; an omitted argument takes its default with `Self` the type), each method belongs to the instance that declares it, and each associated type is recorded per instance (`instance_assoc`). Test: `TYP-21/accept_operators_call_their_interfaces.em` (`m * v` and `m * m`). |
| D-312 | **A negative literal for an unsigned type compiled.** `x: u8 = -1` held 255 and `y: u32 = -5` held 4294967291, though `[LEX-24]` says a negative literal whose type is unsigned is rejected with `E2010` ("`-1` does not fit `u32`"). Found fixing D-311. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[LEX-24]` | **fixed** | With D-311: the negated literal is fitted as one constant, so an unsigned type holds only `-0`, and the message names the whole literal (`-129` for an `i8`, not `129`). Test: `LEX-24/reject_a_negative_literal_for_an_unsigned_type.em`. |
| D-311 | **A signed type's least value written as a literal was refused.** `x: i8 = -128` was `E2010` "the literal `128` does not fit in `i8`", and likewise `-9223372036854775808` for `i64` and `i128`'s least value: the checker fitted the literal before the minus, so every signed minimum had to be written `-127 - 1`. `[LEX-24]` makes a minus directly before an untyped literal part of the constant. Found writing the `i128` tests (D-272). Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[LEX-24]`, `[TYP-1]` | **fixed** | `adopt_literal` fits a negated integer literal as one constant: up to the type's least value when it is signed, only `-0` when it is not (D-312). The backend spells a negated constant as one (`int_literal`), the least value as `(-MAX - 1)`, since C reads `-9223372036854775808` as a minus on a literal too large for any signed type; a switch's values go through it too. | D-310 | **A negative literal pattern was a syntax error.** `match x: -5 => …` was `E0100` "expected a pattern", though the grammar's `literal_pattern := ["-"] INT` has the minus. A pattern that failed to check no longer makes the arms after it unreachable (`W2091`). Tests: `LEX-24/accept_negative_literal_patterns.em`, `LEX-24/reject_a_pattern_literal_outside_its_type.em`. |

| D-306 | **One type parameter name in two slots made one type.** An instance's name held its arguments' names, and a type parameter's name is only its spelling: `Option[U]` over a function's first parameter and std's `Option[U]` over `Option.map`'s second were one `Option_U`, whichever was made first. `fn wrap[U](x: int, f: fn(ref int) -> U) -> Option[U]` then returned an `Option` of the second slot, the callable's: the call inferred `Option[fn(ref int) -> int]` and the body was `E2020` "expected `U`, found `U`". Test: `TYP-16/accept_one_parameter_name_in_two_slots.em`. |
| D-305 | **A user type named like a prelude type loses to it in type position.** With `class Cell[T]` declared, `Cell[int](value=3)` builds the user's class, but `Cell[int]` as a type (or as the receiver of `Cell[int].make(4)`) is the compiler's `Cell`, which has no `make`. `resolve_type_application` checks the prelude names before the declared ones. Found while testing D-304. | `[MOD-5]` | **fixed** | The root module's names are not qualified, so its `Cell` was the very symbol the compiler's `Cell` is looked up by, and its instances shared the compiler's names (`Box_i64`, their C names too) and origin; `Option` declared by a program also broke `std`'s own `Option`. A root declaration named like a compiler-known type is now `root.Cell` (`root_qualified`), which only the root module resolves to and which diagnostics write `Cell`; a type the program declares or imports (`declared_by_program`) is looked up before the compiler-known names, in type position and in construction, while `std`'s declarations of compiler-known types (`std.mem.UnsafeCell`) keep the compiler's meaning. The compiler-known `Box` is also told by its module, `COMPILER_MODULE` (`TypeTable::compiler_box_inner`). Tests: `MOD-5/accept_a_programs_type_shadows_a_prelude_type.em`, `MOD-5/reject_a_programs_box_is_named_as_written.em`. |
| D-304 | **An enum's associated functions could not be called, and a generic enum's or class's methods could not name the type.** `Shape.unit()` was `E1010` "`Shape` has no variant `unit`": a call on an enum was looked up only as a variant. `Slot[int].make(4)` was `E1010` "cannot find `Slot`": only a generic struct was accepted as the receiver of an associated call. And `fn leaf(v: T) -> Tree[T]` in a generic enum (or class) was `E1010` "cannot find type `Tree`": D-278's own-name-is-`Self` covered generic structs only. Found by the phase-2 compiler reviewer. (C17) | Part IV §8, `[TYP-16]`, `[STR-1]` | **fixed** | A name that is no variant but an associated function of the enum is called as one (`enum_associated`), in both call forms. `associated_type_named` accepts generic classes. `collecting_generic` is set while a generic enum's and class's method signatures are read. Tests: `STR-1/accept_an_enums_associated_function.em`, `TYP-16/accept_own_name_in_a_generic_enum_and_class.em`. |
| D-303 | **`.clone()` did not exist on a tuple, an `Option` or a `Result`**, and a struct holding one was not `Clone` either: `x.clone()` on an `Option[String]` was `E1010` "has no method named `clone`". `[STR-5]` makes an enum `Clone` when its payloads are, and a tuple when its parts are. It also left no way to read a class field's `Option` except by moving it out (D-302). Found while fixing D-302. | `[STR-5]`, `[OWN-8]` | **fixed** | The compiler-made types clone part by part at the clone (`clones_by_parts`, `clone_by_parts`): a plain place is read again for each part, any other value is held once. `is_cloneable` admits them, so a struct holding one derives `Clone`. Test: `STR-5/accept_tuples_options_and_results_clone.em`. |
| D-302 | **Moving out of a class field compiled, and a match on one bound by reference with no read access.** `E3012` was declared and never emitted: `x = a.name` moved the `String` out of the object while every handle could still reach it; the object then freed it again. D-259 also made `match a.name: Some(s)` bind `s` by reference, and a write through another handle (`b.clear()`) freed the `String` under `s`; the program printed nothing, reading freed memory. The binding needs a read access the handle's aliases respect, which is D-202's missing piece. Found by the phase-2 compiler reviewer. (C15) | `[EXP-6]`, `[EXC-1]`, `[GRM-13]` | **fixed** | Every move out of a class field is `E3012` (`through_class_field` in the move checker). A match on a place reached through a class field binds by move again, so its non-`Copy` bindings are `E3012` too; `match a.name.clone():` (D-303) is the way. Tests: `EXP-6/reject_moving_out_of_a_class_field.em`, `STR-5/accept_tuples_options_and_results_clone.em`. |
| D-301 | **A generic struct's type arguments never met its bounds where it was named.** `Map[f64, int]`, `Set[f64]`, `{1.5, 2.5}` and `Set[Set[int]]` were made, and the errors appeared inside std's `Map` methods (`AsKey[f64]` "which `Q` requires", at `collections.em`), with nothing on the user's line. Found by the phase-2 compiler reviewer. (C13) | `[TYP-17]`, `[STD-11]` | **fixed** | `instantiate_struct` checks each parameter's bounds at the naming span (`struct_bounds_met`), `E2040` "`f64` does not implement `Hash`, which `Map`'s `K` requires". One named before the implementations are collected (a field type) is checked once they are, and its methods are then not checked (`unmet_instances`). `{}` and `Map()` stay quiet when their annotation already failed. Test: `TYP-17/reject_a_map_key_that_misses_hash.em`. |
| D-300 | **Whether `@view` was right depended on declaration order.** `@view struct Outer: inner: Inner` with `Inner` (holding a `str`) declared below it was `E2030` "not a view type": the check ran while `Inner` had no fields yet. The same for enums. Found by the phase-2 compiler reviewer. (C12) | `[TYP-34]` | **fixed** | Each `@view` type is recorded and judged once every type has its fields (`check_view_attributes`). Test: `TYP-34/accept_view_through_a_type_declared_later.em`. |
| D-299 | **A temporary a `for` loop iterated was dropped before the loop ran.** `for x in {3, 1, 2}:`, `for k in make():` and `for k, v in make().items():` were `E3060` "this temporary is dropped at the end of its statement while it is still borrowed": the map or set died with the hidden iterator's declaration, which borrows it. Found by the phase-2 compiler reviewer. (C11) | `[EXP-4]`, `[CTL-1]` | **fixed** | MIR lowering hands the temporaries of a `for` iterator's declaration to the loop's block, dropped after the loop and before the iterator, on every exit. Test: `EXP-4/accept_a_loop_over_a_temporary_collection.em`. |
| D-298 | **A literal key never matched a key type other than `int`.** `1 in s` for a `Set[i32]` and `m[1]` for a `Map[i16, V]` were `E2040` "`i64` does not implement `AsKey[i32]`": lookups take `Q: AsKey[K]`, and the untyped literal fixed `Q` as `int`. The generic-method path had no deferred-literal step at all. Found by the phase-2 compiler reviewer. (C14) | `[STD-12]`, `[TYP-23]` | **fixed** | Generic methods defer untyped literals as generic functions do, and an untyped literal passed for a type parameter still open takes a type the parameter's bounds name when its default misses them (`literal_for_bounds`). A `Map[u8, V]` works since D-201's fix (`{1: 'one'}`; G6-4). Test: `STD-12/accept_a_literal_key_takes_the_key_type.em`. |
| D-297 | **`m[k] = []`, `m[k] = None` and `m[k] = {}` were `E2060`.** A generic method's arguments were synthesised with no expected type, even where the parameter's type was known (`index_set`'s `v: V`), so an empty literal had nothing to take its type from; `get_or(k, None)` failed the same way. Found by the phase-2 compiler reviewer. (C8) | `[TYP-23]`, `[TYP-38]`, `[STD-17]` | **fixed** | An argument whose parameter type no type parameter reaches is checked against it, quietly, for generic functions and methods alike; the check after inference reports. Test: `TYP-23/accept_a_known_argument_type_reaches_a_literal.em`. |
| D-296 | **`a[i] op= x` evaluated its place twice, and `m["a"] += m["b"]` was a borrow error.** `a[f()] += 10` called `f` twice (Arrays too: present before the 0.9.9 work). `m[k].n += 1` read through `index` and wrote through `index_mut`, a borrow conflict (`E3021`); `m["a"] += m["b"]` held `index_mut("a")` while reading `m["b"]`. Found by the phase-2 compiler reviewer. (C5, C6) | `[EXP-2]`, `[STD-17]` | **fixed** | The operand is evaluated first; then a place whose path computes something (a call's reference, an index that is not a local or a literal) is held once (`hoist_place`) and read and written through the same locals (`copy_place`). Test: `EXP-2/accept_an_update_evaluates_its_place_once.em`. |
| D-295 | **A `ref mut` pattern binding skipped every write check.** `match o: Some(ref mut s): s += "!"` on a borrowed parameter `o` compiled and changed the caller's value; `match m[k]: Some(ref mut s)` wrote through the shared reference `index` returns. `ref mut e` as an expression is checked. Found by the phase-2 compiler reviewer. (C2) | `[GRM-13]`, `[FN-1]`, `[BRW-1]` | **fixed** | A pattern binding `ref mut` makes the match a write of its scrutinee: `index_mut` for `m[k]`, and the checks `ref mut e` runs. Tests: `GRM-13/reject_ref_mut_binding_of_a_borrowed_parameter.em`, `GRM-13/accept_ref_mut_binding_into_a_map_value.em`. |
| D-294 | **`x in xs`, `contains` and `index_of` ignored a written `eq`.** They compared field by field, so a type whose `eq` ignores a field was not found, and two equal maps in another insertion order differed; before D-268 the same scan was `E2226`. Found by the phase-2 compiler reviewer. (C3) | `[STD-8]`, `[STR-5]` | **fixed** | The scan builds its comparison as `==` does, through `synth_eq_of` for a type with a written `eq` inside. Test: `STD-8/accept_a_scan_calls_a_written_eq.em`. |
| D-293 | **`==` on a type holding a written `eq` beside a part without `Eq` crashed the compiler or compared by bytes.** `[W] == [W]` where `W` holds an `S` with a written `eq` and a `@no_derive(Eq)` `Tag` panicked ("no entry found for key"); the tuple `(S, Tag) == (S, Tag)` compiled and compared `Tag` by bytes. Found by the phase-2 compiler reviewer. (C1) | `[STR-5]`, `[TYP-17]` | **fixed** | A written `eq` inside a type is used only when the whole type has `Eq` (`written_eq_applies`); otherwise `==` is `E2040`. Test: `STR-5/reject_a_written_eq_beside_a_part_without_eq.em`. |
| D-292 | **`s: Set[int] = {}` gave a misleading `E2060`.** "cannot tell what `{}` holds", with a help to add the type the declaration already had. Found by the phase-2 library reviewer. | `[TYP-38]`, ODR-034 | **fixed** | `{}` where a `Set` is expected is `E2020` "`{}` is an empty `Map`, not a `Set`", with help `Set[int]()`. Test: `TYP-38/reject_an_empty_map_literal_as_a_set.em`. |
| D-291 | **Reading a map inside an index made it a write.** The assignment flag that picked `index_mut` reached every sub-expression, so `xs[m["b"]] = 7` called `m.index_mut`: `E3023` on a borrowed `m`, and a needless mutable borrow of a local one. Found by the phase-2 library reviewer. | `[STD-17]` | **fixed** | With D-290's fix, `index` is always what a read calls; only the written place is rewritten. Test: in `STD-17/accept_writes_through_nested_indexing.em` (red before). |
| D-290 | **Writes through nested indexing were refused.** `m["x"]["y"] = 2`, `lists["a"].push(5)` and similar were `E3021` "cannot write through a shared reference": the inner `m[k]` always used `index`, and nothing turned it into `index_mut` when the place was written. Found by the phase-2 library reviewer. | `[STD-17]`, `[TYP-20]` | **fixed** | `a[i]` is read through `index`; `index_place_for_write` rewrites a written place's `index` call as the matching `index_mut` instance and passes its receiver as a `mut` receiver, down a chain, at assignment targets, `mut self` receivers and `ref mut`. Test: `STD-17/accept_writes_through_nested_indexing.em` (red before), `reject_writing_a_borrowed_map.em`. |
| D-289 | **`{}`, `Map()` and `Set()` were not fixed by later uses.** `[TYP-23]` names `Map()` among the open values fixed by later uses; `d = {}; d[k] = v` and `m = Map(); m.insert(k, v)` were `E2060`. Found by the phase-2 library reviewer. | `[TYP-23]`, `[TYP-38]` | **fixed** | Open locals carry a kind (`OpenKind`): `{}` and `Map()` are fixed by `m[k] = v`, `m.insert(k, v)` or a typed context, `Set()` by `s.add(x)`; a constructor with an `init` takes its type arguments from the expected type. Tests: `TYP-23/accept_an_open_map_fixed_by_later_uses.em` (red before), `TYP-38/reject_an_open_empty_map.em`. |
| D-288 | **A map at a steady load near its limit rebuilt on about every second insertion.** A rebuild picked the smallest table that fitted, so removed places filled it again within a few operations: O(n) per insertion, 70 s for 200,000 operations at 7,167 entries. Found by the phase-2 library reviewer. | `[STD-11]`, ODR-033 | **fixed** | A rebuild leaves at least half the usable room free (`reserve`), so the next one comes only after as many operations again. The reproduction now runs in 0.4 s. Test: `STD-11/accept_steady_load_near_the_limit.em`. |
| D-287 | **`entry(k).or_insert(v)` could index past the end.** It read the new entry's position before `push_new`, which may first close up removed entries: `index 7 is out of bounds for a length of 7`. `or_default` and `or_insert_with` too. Found by the phase-2 library reviewer. | `[STD-16]` | **fixed** | The position is read after the push. Test: `STD-16/accept_entry_after_a_close_up.em` (red before). |
| D-286 | **`println`'s arguments are not borrow-checked as a call's.** `println(xs, xs.pop())` compiles and prints the array after the `pop`, where a user function `show(ys, ys.pop())` is `E3021` ("`ys` is borrowed here and mutably borrowed elsewhere"). Found writing the `Set` tests. | `[EXP-1]`, `[BRW-5]` | **fixed** | Each `print` argument is evaluated where it is written: the checker wraps a place that is not `Copy` in a shared `ref`, which lowering borrows into a temporary on the spot (`lower_print_argument`), and a `Copy` place is read into a temporary on the spot. `println(xs, xs.pop())` is now `E3021`, and `println(n, bump(n))` prints the old `n`. Printing a moved value is `E3050`, as borrowing one in a call is (two tests' expected codes followed). Tests: `EXP-1/reject_a_later_print_argument_changing_an_earlier_one.em`, `accept_print_arguments_are_read_where_written.em` (both red before). |
| D-285 | **An instance made by a signature missed bounded extensions.** `fn total(m: Map[String, int])` made `Map[String, int]` while signatures were collected, before std's `implements` blocks were; the extension holding `keys`/`iter` needs `K: Hash`, `String`'s `Hash` was not known yet, and it was never offered again: "`Map[…]` has no method named `keys`". Found writing `Map` tests. | `[GRM-34]` | **fixed** | Every instance is offered the extensions again once methods and implementations are collected (`extend_early_instances`, idempotent). Test: `GRM-34/accept_an_instance_from_a_signature_gets_bounded_extensions.em` (red before). An opaque instance inside a generic body (`Map[T, bool, H]` in `Set`) still misses an extension bounded by `Ord`; `Set.sorted_elements` does not rely on it. |
| D-284 | **A generic body is region-checked again for each instance, under the instance's rules.** With a view type argument the passing rules change: `Map[str, V]` is itself a view, passed as a copy, so its view-returning methods (`get`, `keys`, `items`) point into the callee's copy (`E3060`), and `entry(k)` borrows `k`, which the declared signature does not name (`E3062`). The opaque check passed; only instances fail. | `[LT-1]`, `[TYP-34]`, `[BRW-8]`, ODR-048 | **fixed** | The instance keeps the generic's declared signature (ODR-048). A parameter the result may point into (the receiver, or a declared source, ODR-024) is passed as the declaration passes it wherever the result is a reference or view (`param_by_address`, from the declared types: `generic_of` for a generic function's or method's instance, `declared_params` for a method made from a generic type's recipe, over the owner's own parameters); the callee's parameter locals and every call with a known callee use it. An instance's sources are the declaration's plus each parameter its own type makes a view (`declared_sources`), so `Tree[str].leaf(v: T)` may return a view of `v`'s. Such an instance is not a value of the callable type of its parameters, which would pass a copy: `fn_value_of` reports `E2020` (before, the C compiler refused the function pointer). Tests: `LT-1/accept_a_generic_body_means_the_same_for_every_instance.em` (a `Holder[str]` receiver, `Wrap[str]` and a `Copy` `Pt[str]`), `TYP-16/accept_own_name_in_a_generic_enum_and_class.em`, `BRW-8/reject_an_instance_value_that_takes_by_address.em`. |
| D-283 | **A struct's `init` was not its constructor.** `[STR-6]` (new in 0.9.9) makes `fn init(self, …)` a struct's constructor; `Map[K, V]()` with a declared `init` still went to the memberwise constructor and was `E1052` (private fields). | `[STR-6]` | **fixed** (part) | A struct with an `init` is built from its field defaults, then `init` runs with the arguments (`construct_through_init`), for plain and generic structs; `init`'s visibility governs. An `init` that must assign a field with no default needs `E2100`'s definite-assignment check, which is not built: that is `E0900`. Tests: `STR-6/accept_an_init_constructor.em`, `reject_init_with_a_field_without_a_default.em`. |
| D-282 | **`@borrows(self)` was refused.** `[LT-1a]`: "`self` names the receiver"; the checker reported `E2031` "each `@borrows` argument must be a parameter name". Found writing `MapEntry`. | `[LT-1a]` | **fixed** | `self` is accepted as the receiver's name. A generic type's method is checked with its receiver in the parameter list (`generic_method_signature` passes the `Self` placeholder, then drops it), so the positions count it and no longer need shifting; that receiver is a source parameter. `MapEntry.key()` stays out: naming `self` does not change that a borrowed view receiver is passed as a copy. Test: `LT-1a/accept_borrows_naming_the_receiver.em` (red before). |
| D-281 | **`[TYP-36]`'s `Default` column was missing.** `T.default()` with `T` = `int`, `bool`, `String`, `Array[int]` or `Option[int]` was `E2040` "does not implement `std.core.Default`". Found writing `or_default`. | `[TYP-36]` | **fixed** (part) | std implements `Default` for the integers, floats, `bool`, `char`, `str`, `String`, `Array[T]` and `Option[T]`; a built-in instance takes its extensions at an associated call too, as at a method call. Tuples, fixed arrays and `void` are not reached yet. `ARN-11/accept_zeroable_precedes_default.em` lost its non-zero case (a source `Default` for `i32` is now `E2041`) until `@derive(Zeroable)` exists. Test: `TYP-36/accept_default_of_builtin_types.em` (red before). |
| D-280 | **A lambda passed from a generic type's method to a callable parameter failed.** In `Map`'s body, `self.entries.retain(fn(e) => e.is_some())` was `E2020` "`retain` cannot take `fn(Option[MapSlot[Callable1, V]]) -> bool`", and `or_insert_with(fn() => V.default())` "expects `Callable3`": the callee's hidden callable parameter is numbered after the owner's parameters and collides with them. Found writing `Map`. | `[CLO-3]`, `[TYP-17]` | **fixed** | Over an opaque owner, a generic method's own parameters are numbered after the caller's parameters the owner's arguments mention (`register_recipe_method`, `params_in`); the signature's `generics` starts with a placeholder per caller slot and `generic_prefix` holds what each stands for, solved and fixed before the call is inferred (`synth_generic_method_call`). Interface conformance compares on the same slots, mapping the interface's parameters before `Self` becomes the owner. With the ranges apart, a generic type's generic methods are checked once opaquely too (`check_opaque_methods`), and the per-owner validation drops what that check already reported. `Map.rebuild` uses `retain` again. Tests: `TYP-16/accept_a_lambda_to_a_callable_parameter_in_a_generic_type.em`, `TYP-17/reject_an_uncalled_generic_method_of_a_generic_type.em`. |
| D-279 | **A generic type's method could not name a generic type declared after it.** `Map.entry(…) -> MapEntry[K, V, H]` with `MapEntry` later in the file was `E1010` "cannot find type `MapEntry`": a generic type's method signatures are resolved while the generic types are still being collected, in file order. A field had the same problem (`b: Array[B[T]]`). Found writing `Map`. | `[MOD-1]` | **fixed** | Every generic struct is noted before any is collected (`note_generic_structs`, the AST items now `Clone`), and a type application naming one not collected yet collects it on the spot (`collect_generic_struct_on_demand`), across modules too. Two generic structs whose declarations name each other still need an order. Test: `TYP-16/accept_a_generic_type_named_before_its_declaration.em` (red before). |
| D-278 | **A generic type's methods could not name the type.** In `struct W[T]`'s methods, `Self` was `E2020` "`Self` names the type being declared, and there is none here" and `W[T]` was `E1010` "cannot find type `W`"; `Self(…)` as a constructor was `E1010` in any type, though Part IV §8's constructors are written that way. Found writing `Map.with_capacity`. | Part IV §8, `[STR-1]` | **fixed** | A generic type's method signatures are collected with `Self`, and the type's own name applied to exactly its parameters, as the `SELF_PARAM` placeholder, which each instance replaces with itself (`generic_method_signature`, `register_recipe_method`, `collecting_generic`); `Self(…)` is the memberwise constructor (or `init`) of the type being declared. Test: `TYP-16/accept_self_in_a_generic_types_methods.em` (red before). |
| D-277 | **An unused binding in a generic function was reported once per instance.** `L1001` ran over each monomorphised body, so `k = 1` in `f[T]` called at two types gave two identical warnings. Found writing the D-261 test. | `[LNT-1]`, `[DIA-14]` | **fixed** | The lint reports each declaration once across all bodies. Test: `LNT-1/accept_an_unused_binding_in_a_generic_is_reported_once.em` (red before: two warnings). |
| D-276 | **`o == None` could not type `None`.** A comparison synthesised both operands without context, so `None` (and `[]`) was `E2060` "cannot tell which `Option` this `None` is" beside a typed `Option`. Found writing the D-268 test. | `[TYP-23]` | **fixed** | In a comparison, an open `None` or `[]` beside a typed operand is checked against that operand's type, on either side. Test: `TYP-23/accept_none_and_empty_list_beside_a_typed_operand.em` (red before). |
| D-275 | **`!=` on a type with a written `eq` was refused.** `Eq.eq` gives `==` and `!=` (the interface's own comment in the spec), but `!=` looked for a method named `ne`, and without one fell to implicit equality, which a written `eq` turned off: `E2040` "`S` does not implement `Eq`, which `!=` needs". Found fixing D-268. | `[TYP-21]`, `[STR-5]` | **fixed** | `a != b` is `not a.eq(b)`. Test: in `STR-5/accept_implicit_eq_over_a_written_eq.em` (red before). |
| D-274 | **A struct carrying a borrow still had to say `@view`.** `[TYP-34]` (new in 0.9.9, replacing 0.9.8's requirement) infers view types, makes `@view` documentation, and gives `E2030` to `@view` on a type that is not a view; the checker still reported `E2030` "`Holder` carries a borrow, so it is a view type" for an undeclared view struct, and said nothing about `@view` on a plain one. Found writing the D-262 test. | `[TYP-34]` | **fixed** | The requirement is gone; `@view` on a struct or enum with no view field is `E2030` "`@view` on `Point`, which is not a view type". The two tests that pinned 0.9.8's rule moved to `TYP-34/accept_a_view_struct_needs_no_attribute.em` and `reject_view_on_a_type_that_is_not_a_view.em`. |
| D-273 | **Class handles are not `Hash`.** `[TYP-36]` gives a class handle `Hash` by identity; a `K: Hash` bound on one is `E2040`, because HIR has no operation that reads a handle's address. Found fixing D-265. | `[TYP-36]` | **fixed** | A class handle is `Hash` (`hashes`), provided by the compiler as a tuple's or an array's is (`hash_provided`); `synth_hash_of` hashes the object's address, the handle cast to `usize`, as `is` compares it. A class-interface value is not included: it is not one pointer. Test: `TYP-36/accept_a_class_handle_hashes_by_identity.em`. |
| D-272 | **`i128` and `u128` do not reach C.** Code generation names `ember_i128`/`ember_u128`, which no runtime header declares, so any program using them fails in the C compiler (MSVC has no 128-bit integer at all; GCC and Clang have `__int128`). No test used them. Found fixing D-265. | `[TYP-1]`, `[TYP-6]`, `[FFI-8]` | **fixed** | The runtime carries both types as C's `__int128` where the compiler has it and as two 64-bit halves elsewhere (MSVC, clang-cl), and the generated C reaches a 128-bit value only through the runtime's helpers (`wide_int`, `wide_binary`, `wide_cast`, `wide_constant` in `ember_codegen_c`; ADR-048). `parse`, `len`, `get` and `drain` over 128-bit values, refused with `E0900` before, work too; `i128` and `u128` joined `std.math`'s `Number`. | D-268 | **A type holding a field with a written `eq` had no `Eq`.** `struct Outer: s: S[int]` where `S` implements `Eq` by hand: `Outer(..) == Outer(..)` was `E2040` "`Outer` does not implement `Eq`", though `[STR-5]` gives a type `Eq` when every field has it. Found probing what `Map` needs. | `[STR-5]` | **fixed** | A component with a written `eq` has `Eq`; comparing a type that holds one is built part by part in the checker (`synth_eq_of`: the written `eq` is called, tuples and structs compare fields in order, enums the variant then the payload, sequences go to std's `Span.eq_elements`), and anything with none inside compares as before. `call_operator` now also releases a generic extension's deferred method body (a compiler panic otherwise). | D-266 | **A type parameter's default was ignored.** `struct Holder[K, H = DefaultHasher]` parses (`generic_param := IDENT [":" bound_list] ["=" type]`), but `Holder[i32]` was `E2020` "takes 2 type arguments, found 1"; `Map[K, V, H = DefaultHasher]` (`[STD-11]`) needs it. Found probing what `Map` needs. | `[STD-11]`, grammar `generic_param` | **fixed** | A parameter's default is resolved where the declaration is (`GenericParam.default`, in terms of the parameters before it); an omitted trailing argument takes it in a type (`instantiate_struct`/`_enum`/`_class`) and in a constructor whose inference left it open. | D-265 | **`Hash` was promised but missing.** `char`, `str`, `i128`, `u128` and unit-only enums passed a `K: Hash` bound, then `k.hash(h)` was `E1010` "has no method named `hash`"; `String` and tuples failed the bound although the type table gives them `Hash`. Found probing what `Map` needs. | `[HASH-1]`, `[ENM-3]`, `[TYP-36]` | **fixed** (class handles: D-273) | std implements `Hash` for `char`, `i128`/`u128`, `str`, `String` (exactly as its `str`), `Span[T]`, `Array[T]`, `Option[T]` and `Result[T, E]`; the compiler provides it (`synth_hash_of`) for tuples, fixed arrays (as their `Span`), unit-only enums, range types and `void`, and for `@derive(Hash)` (`[DRV-1]`: fields in order, a variant's index first, `E2080` naming a field that is not `Hash`). `implements` walks the same kinds (`hashes`). `DefaultHasher` became FxHash's class (`[HASH-2]`: rotate, xor, multiply per word, bytes eight to a word) and `Default`. | D-263 | **`()` was not the value of `void`.** `[TYP-27]` makes `()` the value of `void` and `Ok(())` the success value of `Result[void, E]`; `return Ok(())`, `return ()`, `x: void = ()` and `Some((1, ()))` were `E2020` "expected `void`, found `()`", `Some(f())` for a `void` `f` made C that did not compile (`C2065`), a tuple with a `void` element declared a `void` member (`C2182`), and printing a `Result[void, E]` was `E0900`. Found probing what `Set` needs. | `[TYP-27]`, `[TYP-36]` | **fixed** | `()` is typed `void` (`void_value`, which `[FN-10]`'s implicit `Ok(())` uses too); a `void` member of a tuple, struct or array is one byte, as an enum payload's was, and a `void` operand is that byte's `0` (`c_member_type`, `operand`); `void` formats as `()`. Test: `TYP-27/accept_the_void_value.em` (red before). |
