| D-261 | **A bound naming a generic interface with arguments gave no methods.** `fn conv[K, Q: AsKey[K]](q: Q) -> K: return q.to_key()` was `E2040` "`Q` has no method `to_key`; its bounds do not provide one", with the help "add the bound: `Q: AsKey`". `[STD-12]`'s borrowed keys need it. Found probing what `Map` needs. | `[TYP-17]`, `[STD-12]` | **fixed** | A bound with arguments names an interface instance (`bound_interface`); one over parameters is numbered, since two parameters may share a name (`interface_instance_name`, `open_interfaces`), and remembered, so a call and an owner's instance substitute into it (`substitute_bound`). A generic type's methods declare their bounds before interfaces are collected: those are named at once and instantiated right after (`resolve_pending_bound_interfaces`). Diagnostics print the bound as written, `AsKey[String]`. Tests: `TYP-17/accept_a_bound_on_a_generic_interface.em` (the probe was `E2040` before), `reject_a_generic_interface_bound_not_met.em`. |
| D-260 | **A name assigned in an arm that ends in a leaving `match` was hoisted.** `[CTL-10]` hoists names only out of branches that complete, but the check read an arm ending in a statement `match` whose arms all `return` as completing, declared an unused copy of the name after the outer `match`, and `L1001` said "`old` is never read". Found writing a `Map` probe. | `[CTL-10]`, `[LNT-1]` | **fixed** | The hoisting check's `block_diverges` also treats a statement `match` whose every arm leaves as leaving. Test: `CTL-10/accept_an_arm_ending_in_a_leaving_match_hoists_nothing.em` (red before the fix). |
| D-259 | **`match` moved out of the place it matched.** `[GRM-13]`: "Matching a place that is not consumed binds `Copy` fields by value and other fields by reference; `match owned e:` binds by move." Every binding moved or copied, so `match x: Some(s)` on a borrowed `Option[String]` parameter was `E3013` "cannot move out of a borrowed parameter", a local was left partly moved, an array element was matched as a copy in a temporary, `ref x`/`ref mut x` in a pattern were parsed and ignored, and `match owned e:` was `E1010` "not supported yet". Found writing a `Map` probe; `Map` cannot be written without it. | `[GRM-13]`, `[GRM-15]` | **fixed** | A binding in a `match` on a place not written `owned` is a `ref T` local when its type is not `Copy`; `ref`/`ref mut` always borrow (HIR `Bind { by_ref }`, MIR `Rvalue::Ref`); an indexed scrutinee is matched in place; `match owned e:` binds by move. std's `option_*`/`result_*` helpers and five tests that moved out of a matched local now say `match owned`. Tests: `GRM-13/accept_matching_a_place_binds_by_reference.em`, `reject_moving_out_of_a_matched_place.em`, `reject_changing_a_place_while_a_binding_borrows_it.em`, `reject_using_a_place_after_match_owned.em` (all four red before the fix). |
| D-258 | **A `drop` written in an `extend` block did not count under ODR-026.** A type with its own `drop` is `Clone` only when it says so, but `has_own_drop` read the flag set only for a `drop` in the type's own body, so `extend Handle: fn drop(mut self)` left `Handle` implicitly `Clone`: a field-wise copy of something its `drop` releases. Found writing the `sorted` rejection test. | `[STR-5]`, `[DRP-1]` | **fixed** | `has_own_drop` also counts a registered `drop` method. Test: `STD-15/reject_sorted_needs_clone.em` (compiled before the fix). |
| D-257 | **A callable parameter could not be passed on.** `fn twice(x, f: fn(int) -> int): apply(x, f)` failed in `apply` with "`f` has type `ref Callable0`, which cannot be called" (`E2020`), from a generic caller or a plain one; `retain` with a capturing closure failed the same way. Found writing `sort_by`. | `[CLO-3]`, `[TYP-17]` | **fixed** | Four causes, four fixes, the last two revised after a read-only review found that the first version crashed the compiler on a lambda capturing only concrete locals in a generic body and gave two closures one C name. (1) ODR-024 makes a borrowed non-`Copy` parameter a `ref T` local, and a callable's type is usually non-`Copy`: `through_callable_ref` reads through it before the call is chosen. (2) Inference solved the callee's hidden generic with `ref F`: a callable-bound parameter is solved with `F`. (3) An instance over another generic's own parameters had its body checked where their bounds are not in scope: it is a signature only, for functions and methods alike (D-250 did this for generic types). (4) A lambda over opaque parameters, or one whose body made such an instance, is marked and not emitted; a lambda over concrete types only is kept, because an instance made during the same check may call it. Closures are numbered from a counter that never goes back, so no two share a name. Tests: `CLO-3/accept_passing_a_callable_parameter_on.em` and `accept_lambdas_in_generic_bodies.em` (each fails without its fix). |
| D-256 | **`sort()` and `sorted()` refused element types that implement `Ord`.** `[STD-15]`'s `sort` takes `T: Ord`; the compiler's `sort` ordered only numbers and text, and `sorted` (the method and `[STD-26]`'s function) only `Copy` numbers and text, so a struct with an `Ord`, or `String` elements, got `E2040`/`E0900`. Found by the phase-2 map of `[STD-15]` and its review. | `[STD-15]`, `[STD-26]` | **fixed** | The compiler still sorts numbers and text itself; another `T: Ord` goes to std's `sort_ord`, and a `sorted` of anything else `Ord` and `Clone` to `sorted_ord` (`std/src/core.em`), the stable index sort `sort_by` uses. Cloning an `Array[T]` and `extend` in a generic body now honour a `T: Clone` bound (`implements`). Tests: `STD-15/accept_sort_of_ord_types.em`, `reject_sorted_needs_clone.em`. |
| D-255 | **`vcvars64.bat` fails on a long PATH, so MSVC is not found.** `cmd` lines stop at 8,191 characters, and the batch file builds PATH on one. Measured here: with PATH at 5,300 characters it works once and fails when run again over its own result (exit 255, no `INCLUDE`); at 7,000 it fails the first time. CI hit the first case: `msvc-dev-cmd` had applied the environment, `d01cbac` ran the batch file again, and both Windows jobs failed. | `[MAN-1]`, `[TOOL-3]` | **fixed** | An environment that is already a Visual Studio developer environment (`VCINSTALLDIR` and `INCLUDE` set, `cl.exe` on PATH) is used as it is, with no batch file run (`6f57fa2`, CI's case). Otherwise the batch file runs on Windows' own short PATH, and each build appends its own PATH to what the batch file added; the cache keeps only that part, so a later PATH change is never masked (cache key `v2`). A read-only agent traced what the batch files launch: only `reg.exe` needs PATH, and the closing telemetry call, `START powershell.exe`, is what opened the owner's "cannot find 'powershell.exe'" window when a probe ran the files with an empty environment; `VSCMD_SKIP_SENDTELEMETRY=1` skips it. Checked once: with PATH at 8,051 characters and a cold cache, MSVC is found and hello builds (1.4 s). A read-only skeptic then found three more, all fixed. (1) A developer environment was reused whatever it targets, and the plain Developer Prompt targets x86: programs would have been 32-bit, and `isize` overflow checks wrong. Only an x64 one (`VSCMD_ARG_TGT_ARCH`) is reused now. (2) The batch files extend inherited `INCLUDE`/`LIB`/`VSINSTALLDIR`, so a shell's own Visual Studio variables could reach the machine-wide cache. The child gets none of them, and what it sets is measured against its own input (cache key `v3`). (3) The runtime object's key did not cover `INCLUDE` and `LIB`, so another Windows SDK could reuse an object; they are hashed in. Checked: in an x64 prompt the environment is reused, and in an x86 one `vcvars64.bat` is captured instead; both build 64-bit (`size_of[usize]` 8). |
| D-254 | **The parallel harness's output paths passed Windows' 260-character limit in CI.** `a32d0b7` named each case's output folder after its whole relative path, and the harness also added the profile's folder the driver adds again (`shipping\shipping\bin`). With the executable named after the file, the longest `TYP-22` cases reached about 262 characters on the CI runner, and the linker failed (`LNK1104: cannot open file`) in `release` and `shipping`; both Windows jobs failed. Locally the paths were 6 characters shorter and passed. The job log needs a signed-in viewer, so CI now copies a failure into an annotation (`tools/ci_failure_annotation.py`, `tools/run_logged.py`). | test harness | **fixed** | `4398dc0`: each case builds in `ember-tests/<16-hex hash of its relative path>`, and only the driver adds the profile; the longest path a run writes is 216 characters, the linker's about 150. `annotations.py` names its folders the same way. |
| D-251 | **CI's four C compilers are one: clang.** `.github/workflows/ci.yml` sets `EMBER_CC` to `clang`, `gcc`, `msvc` or `clang-cl`, and nothing reads it: the tests call `ember` without `--cc`, so every job takes `Toolchain::detect(None)`. That never finds MSVC: `capture_environment` passes `call "<vcvars64.bat>" >nul 2>&1 && set` to `cmd /c` as one argument, Rust escapes its quotes as `\"`, which `cmd` does not understand, and the batch file never runs. With VS 2022 BuildTools installed (MSVC 14.44), `ember run --cc msvc` reports "no C compiler found (looked for: msvc)"; the same command line passed verbatim sets `INCLUDE`. Detection then falls through to clang on both hosts. So MSVC, gcc and clang-cl have never compiled the emitted C, in CI or here, and the MSVC flags in `compile_and_link` have never run. `clang-cl` is also not a toolchain `--cc` accepts. Found planning the cached runtime object. | Part XX §2 (Phase 0 CI), `[MAN-1]`, `[CG-C-1]` | **fixed** | `capture_environment` hands `cmd` its command line as written (`raw_arg`), so MSVC is found, and becomes the default on Windows as `[MAN-1]`'s `auto` says; that exposed D-252 and D-253. The driver reads `EMBER_CC` (`ember_branding::cc_var()`) when `--cc` is not given, and `--cc` accepts `clang-cl` (MSVC's environment and flags, clang's front end, which ignores `/GL`) and `auto`. MSVC, clang-cl and clang each pass the full suite here; gcc runs only in CI. `[MAN-1]`'s `c_compiler` lists no `clang-cl`: it is chosen only through `--cc` or `EMBER_CC`, which the specification does not list either. |
| D-252 | **A panic in a program MSVC built in `debug` opened a window and waited for a click.** MSVC's debug C runtime (`/MDd`) reports `abort()` in a "Debug Error! abort() has been called" window (`_WRITE_ABORT_MSG`); `panic_with` switched off only the Windows Error Reporting dialog (`_CALL_REPORTFAULT`), and `debug_allocation_failure` switched off neither. The first parallel run with MSVC opened one window per panicking test and flooded the owner's screen. | `[PAN-1]` | **fixed** | Every panic and fatal runtime error ends in `runtime_abort`, which switches off both reports before `abort()` (status 3). MSVC's `debug` profile links the release C runtime (`/MD`), as clang builds and Rust's do; the debug one also needs Visual Studio's own DLLs to start. `annotations.py` sets `SEM_NOGPFAULTERRORBOX`, which the programs it runs inherit. Checked on one panicking program first (under `/MDd` directly, then through the driver: exit 3, no window), then the suites under MSVC and clang-cl with a watchdog ready to close any window: none opened. |
| D-253 | **The emitted C did not compile with MSVC.** A float division by a literal zero (`1.0 / 0.0`, which `[TYP-29]` gives IEEE's infinity) became a C constant division by zero, which MSVC refuses (`C2124`; `STD-20`, `TYP-6`). A class vtable adapter cast every argument to the method's parameter type, and C has no cast to a struct type (`C2440`; 14 `TYP-16` files). The runtime's arena named `max_align_t`, which MSVC's C mode does not declare (`C2061`; every program). Hidden by D-251. | `[TYP-29]`, `[CG-C-1]` | **fixed** | A float `/` by a literal zero calls the runtime's `ember_fdiv_f32`/`ember_fdiv_f64`, which take the zero as a parameter. The adapter casts only where the C types differ (the receiver). The arena's alignment is a union of the fundamental types (`arena_max_align`), equal to `max_align_t` where that exists. |
| D-250 | **A generic signature naming a generic type could reject a valid program**: `fn through[T: Sized](w: Wrapper[T])` made an instance of `Wrapper` over `through`'s own `T`, and that instance's method bodies were checked with the wrong bounds in scope, so `self.item.len()` inside `Wrapper[T: Sized]` was "`T` has no method `len`" (`E2040`). The same instances also ran per-instance declaration checks (`@derive(Copy)`, `Clone`) against an opaque `T`, and a class one emitted C glue calling bodies that did not exist. Found building D-248; reproduced at c2f0bd4. | `[TYP-17]`, `[TYP-16]` | **fixed** | With D-248: such an instance has signatures only (`is_opaque_instance`), and its declaration checks and class glue are skipped. Test: `TYP-17/accept_a_generic_types_method_calls_its_bound.em` (`through`). |
| D-249 | **A bound on `Display`, `Debug` or `Copy` accepted every type**: the standard library does not declare these prelude interfaces yet, and a bound naming an undeclared interface was waved through, so `fn show[T: Display](x: T)` took a struct and `fn f[T: Copy]` a `String`; `[TYP-36]`'s table says a missing implementation makes the bound `E2040`. Conversely a generic body could not format its `T: Display` parameter (`E1010 T cannot be formatted yet`). Found giving `Array` its `join`. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[TYP-36]`, `[TYP-17]` | **fixed** | `implements` answers the three from the table while they are undeclared: `Display` for the scalars, text and collections, tuples, `Option` and `Result` of `Debug` parts (`has_display`; a struct only when it says so, which cannot be written yet), `Debug` for what can be formatted, `Copy` for `Copy` types. A parameter bounded by `Display` or `Debug` formats (`param_bound_named`). Tests: `TYP-36/accept_display_debug_and_copy_bounds.em`, `TYP-36/reject_bounds_the_table_does_not_meet.em`. |
| D-248 | **A generic type's methods were duck-typed**: the body of a method of `struct Wrapper[T: Sized]` was checked only for each concrete instance, never with `T` opaque, so it could call what `Sized` does not provide (`self.item.frob()` compiled when every `T` used had `frob`), and a bound's method reached an inherent one of the name (D-245's case inside a type). `[TYP-17]`: "there is no duck typing". Found testing D-245. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[TYP-17]` | **fixed** | `check_generic_type_methods` checks each generic struct's, enum's and class's methods, and each generic extension's, once against the type over its own opaque parameters with their bounds in scope, before any concrete instance; the calls it makes through a bound are recorded (`bound_calls`). A concrete instance of a method so checked reports only what that check did not at the same place. An opaque instance has no run-time existence: no method or default-method bodies (`is_opaque_instance`), no derive or abstract-method checks, and no class glue (`TypeTable::runtime_classes`). Two tests that relied on the duck typing were corrected (`TYP-22/…shared_weak_field.em`), and `generic_class_let_field_reassign.em` now expects its error once, against `Version[T]`. Tests: `TYP-17/reject_a_generic_types_method_uses_only_its_bounds.em`, `TYP-17/accept_a_generic_types_method_calls_its_bound.em`. |
| D-247 | **A public method of a generic extension broke the interface cache**: such a method has no body of its own, only one specialization per instance, and with no declaration record each specialization was recorded as its module's interface. Found when `std.core` gained `extend[T] Array[T]:` methods. Present before the 0.9.9 work for `pub` methods of a generic class's extension. | `[BLD-2]`, `[LT-40]` | **fixed** | A generic extension's public members are declared over its own parameters (`callable_declarations`, as a generic struct's are), so a specialization is never the module's record. |
| D-246 | **What the compiler provides did not meet a bound**: `fn f[T: Ord]` refused `i64` and `str`, `T: Clone` refused `i64` and `String`, and `T: Eq` refused a struct's implicit `Eq` (`[STR-5]`), though `sort`, `clone` and `==` accept them. `x.clone()` did not exist on a `Copy` value, `cmp` on the numbers and text, and `Ordering` was not in the prelude (`[MOD-5]`), so generic code bounded by `Ord` could not be written as `[TYP-37]` says. Found writing `[STD-15]` methods in Ember. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[TYP-17]`, `[TYP-37]`, `[STR-5]`, `[MOD-5]` | **fixed** | `implements` asks the compiler's own tests: `sortable` for `Ord`, `is_cloneable` for `Clone`, `has_implicit_eq` for `Eq`. `x.clone()` of a `Copy` value is the value; `a.cmp(b)` on the numbers and text is built from `TotalLess` (a `String` compared as its `str`); `Ordering` is exported. On the way: `min`/`max`/`clamp` refused text (`"b"`); they take `str` now. Tests: `TYP-17/accept_compiler_provided_capabilities_meet_bounds.em`, `STD-26/accept_min_max_of_text.em`; `HASH-4/reject_arena_map_key_without_eq.em` now opts its key out of `Eq`, which 0.9.9 would otherwise give it. |
| D-245 | **A bound's method in a generic function could reach an inherent method of the same name**: in `fn size[S: Sized](s: S) -> int: return s.len()`, `s.len()` is `Sized.len` (`[TYP-17]`: only what the bound provides), but an instantiation re-checks the body with `S` replaced by the concrete type, where a plain `s.len()` found that type's own `len` first (`[TYP-24]`). A type with both, or an `Array` given `Sized` by an extension, got its inherent `len`. Found building D-242. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[TYP-17]`, `[TYP-24]` | **fixed** | The generic check records, for each call made through a bound, the interface it went to (`bound_calls`, by the call's span); an instantiation checking the call again takes that interface's method when the concrete type has one (`interface_methods`), and otherwise the compiler's (`i64`'s `cmp`). Test: `TYP-17/accept_a_bound_call_reaches_the_interface_method.em`. Methods of a generic *type* are D-248. |
| D-244 | **An interface method added after an inherent method of the same name was dropped**: `register_method` returned nothing for it, so `I.m(x)` was `E2040` ("has no `m` from `I`"), and its body was compiled under the inherent method's C symbol, which the C compiler rejects as a redefinition. `[TYP-24]` lets the two coexist. Found building D-242 (the D-241 fix covered two interfaces, not an interface and the type). Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[TYP-24]`, `[MNG-1]` | **fixed** | The interface method is registered for its interface alone (`interface_methods`), leaving the inherent one in the method table; its symbol names the interface (`method_symbol_for`). `I.m(recv)` goes to the interface's method even where the compiler knows a method `m` (`Sized.len(xs)` on an `Array`). Test: `TYP-24/accept_an_inherent_and_an_interface_method_share_a_name.em`. |
| D-243 | **`E2120` was never reported**: user code could add inherent methods to a type of another package (`extend i64: fn twice(self)`, `extend[T] Array[T]: …`); `[IFC-2]` makes it `E2120`. Found building D-242. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[IFC-2]` | **fixed** | An `extend` with no `implements` whose type belongs to the other package (`declared_in_this_package`: a struct or class by its declaring module, an enum by its module path, everything compiler-known is the standard library's; `generic_target_in_this_package` for `extend[T]`) is `E2120`, with the interface and wrapper routes as help. Error page `docs/errors/E2120.md`. Tests: `IFC-2/` (two). |
| D-242 | **`extend[T]` worked only on a generic class**: on a generic struct or enum, or on `Array`, `Span`, `Option` or `Result` (`[GRM-34]`'s own example is `extend [T: B] Array[T] implements I:`), `T` was "not in scope" (`E1010`); and an instance of a generic class named before the extensions were read (in an interface's signature) never got their methods. Even a concrete `extend Array[i64]:` was unreachable: every method call on an `Array` went to the compiler's own methods. Found while testing ODR-030. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[GRM-34]`, `[IFC-1]` | **fixed** | Generic extensions of structs, enums and classes share one path (`matching_generic_extensions`, `apply_generic_extensions`, `register_recipe_method`), recording what each instance was given (`applied_extensions`) so `extend_early_instances` can give the early ones theirs. A built-in generic type has no instantiation step, so an instance takes its extensions when a method is first looked up on it or it is made a `dyn` (`extend_builtin_instance`), and `implements` reads the recipes (`builtin_extension_implements`). `Array` and `Span` try the compiler's methods first, then the registered ones (`synth_registered_method`, split out of `synth_method_call`). Not built: `Array[u8]`, which the checker cannot tell from `String`; an `Array` or `Span` behind `dyn`. Tests: `GRM-34/` (three). |
| D-241 | **Two interfaces offering one method name for a type shared one implementation**: with `extend S implements A: fn m` and `extend S implements B: fn m`, the method table kept one entry per name, so both bodies were checked against the later definition and the other was never checked; a `dyn A` and a `dyn B` of `S` both called the later one, and naming the earlier one crashed the compiler ("every DefId names a function that was checked"). Direct calls were `E2070`, which hid it. Found building `I.m(recv)` (`[TYP-24]`). Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[TYP-24]`, `[OBJ-1]` | **fixed** | Each interface's methods are also kept per interface (`interface_methods`): a body is checked into its own interface's definition, a `dyn` adapter takes each slot's own interface's method, an implementation is checked against its own, and two such methods get C symbols that name their interface (`method_symbol_for`). Test: `TYP-24/accept_an_interface_names_its_method.em`. |
| D-240 | **A name declared twice in one block was accepted**: `z: int = 1` then `z: int = 2` in the same block compiled, the second silently shadowing the first; `[GRM-4]` makes it `E1020`. Found when the code registry check reported `E1020` untested after privacy moved to `E1052`. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[GRM-4]` | **fixed** | An annotated declaration of a name already declared in the innermost block is `E1020`, pointing at the first; a parameter or an enclosing block's name may still be shadowed. Tests: `GRM-4/` (red without the fix). |
| D-239 | **An `override` of an `override` was refused**: `class C(B)` overriding `speak`, which `B` overrode from `A`'s `virtual fn speak`, was `E2110` "override of a method that is not virtual": the check looked at the nearest declaration, which said `override`, not `virtual`. Found probing `[CLS-4]`. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[CLS-4]` | **fixed** | An inherited `override` counts as virtual (ODR-028 writes it into `[CLS-4]`); dispatch through the base picks the grandchild's method. Test: `CLS-4/accept_an_override_may_be_overridden.em`. |
| D-238 | **A struct field default was never evaluated, and an omitted aggregate field scrambled the fields after it**: every construction that omitted a field put a zero in its place (a comment called it a Phase 0 stand-in), so `struct T: n: int = 3` gave `T().n == 0`; for a `String` or `Array` field the "zero" was an error expression the C backend printed as `0`, and C's brace elision spread the following initialisers over that field's own members: `S(n=4)` with `items: Array[int] = []` first stored the 4 in `items.len`. Silent wrong values in Safe code. Found probing `[STR-2]` for the audit. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[STR-2]` | **fixed** | Each struct, and each generic instance, keeps its fields' default expressions (`struct_default_exprs`); a construction that omits a field checks the default there, in field order (`check_field_default`: names resolve in the declaring module, and the constructing function's locals are not in scope), reporting a mistake in it once however many constructions evaluate it (`[DIA-14]`). A default in a struct never constructed is still not checked. Tests: `STR-2/accept_a_default_is_evaluated_at_each_construction.em` (red without the fix: `0 0`, and `4` in the wrong field), `STR-2/reject_a_default_does_not_see_the_constructors_locals.em`. |
| D-237 | **A generic class inferred its type arguments from its fields, even when `init` takes other parameters**: `class Box2[T]` with `items: Array[T]` and `init(mut self, first: T)` made `Box2(5)` `E2060` "cannot tell what `T` is". Found fixing D-236. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[TYP-18]`, `[CLS-1]` | **fixed** | The inference reads `init`'s parameters when the class has one, and the fields otherwise, by name as D-236's constructor does; its own diagnostics are dropped, since the constructor checks the arguments again (so each error is reported once; the second check is D-235's). Test: `TYP-18/accept_a_generic_class_infers_from_init.em`. |
| D-236 | **A generic struct's constructor inferred by position and checked its arguments twice**: `P(b=1.5, a=true)` for `struct P[A, B]` was rejected (the first argument was unified with the first field whatever its name); an error inside an argument was reported twice (`K(f=nope + 1)`, against `[DIA-14]`); a lambda written in the constructor was checked twice, became two closures, and failed (`expected closure0_env, found closure1_env`). Found building D-222. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[TYP-18]`, `[STR-1]`, `[DIA-14]` | **fixed** | One mapping from arguments to fields (`constructor_slots`) serves every memberwise constructor; the generic one checks each argument once (against its field's type when that is concrete), infers from those values, and coerces them to the instance's fields. | D-233 | **A help ran two clauses together with a run of spaces**: `E1020` on a private struct field said "to read it anywhere," then 18 spaces, then "or `pub(read)` …": the indentation of a source line joined into the message. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[DIA-2]` | **fixed** | One space. |
| D-232 | **Diagnostics name a generic instance by its internal name**: `Wrap_i64` for `Wrap[int]`, `Cell_i32` for `Cell[i32]` (`E1020`'s "private to `Cell_i32`'s module", `E2040` on a `@no_derive(Debug)` generic). `[DIA-2]` says never to show the user a compiler-internal name. `TypeTable::display` prints a struct's interned name; it also feeds `type_stem` and `method_symbol`, so changing it moves generated names too. Four messages that printed a definition's name directly (a private field, a missing abstract override, a `let` field reassigned) now use `display`. Test: `tests/compile-fail/generic_instances_are_named_as_written.em`; eight tests that had pinned the internal names were corrected. |
| D-231 | **Dropping an `Array` of structs that own an `Array` of dropped values crashed**: the drop glue nested two loops over the same index name, `_di`, so the inner loop's index shadowed the outer and `((Bag*)v.ptr)[_di].items` freed the wrong memory (`Array[Bag]`, `Bag` holding `Array[String]`: a segfault at scope end, before buffered output was flushed). Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[TYP-23]`, `[TYP-16]` | **fixed** | The unifier unifies two instances of the same generic enum by their origin, and two `Option_`/`Result_` instances by their payloads; `is_generic` looks into enum payloads. | D-228 | **`Clone` is not built for `Array` or `String`**: `xs.clone()` is `E1010 has no method named clone`, and `@derive(Clone)` on a struct with an `Array` or `String` field is `E2040`. The type checker has one test (`is_cloneable`) and one way to clone a value (`clone_value`), which derived `Clone` uses too, so a struct with `Array` or `String` fields derives it. Tests: `OWN-8/accept_cloning_arrays_and_strings.em` (red without the array case, and without the handle retain), `OWN-8/reject_derive_clone_for_noncopy_field.em` (retargeted: it had pinned this gap). |

Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[DIA-14]`, `[BRW-7]` | **fixed** | The borrow report skips a local no move reaches, as the use report does; conflicts are reported once per loan and source span. | D-225 | **An `owned self` view took `[LT-1]` rule 1**, which is for a borrowed receiver: a method returning another source parameter was rejected (`E3062`), and a caller was not kept borrowing the other source arguments while the result lived, although rule 3 requires it. Tests: `LT-1/accept_an_owned_receiver_is_under_rule_3.em`, `LT-1/reject_mutating_an_argument_of_an_owned_receiver_call.em` (red without the fix). |
| D-224 | **A reference into a view parameter's own `Array` or `Box` escaped**: `fn first(owned h: H) -> ref int: return ref h.arr[0]` (a `@view` struct with an `Array` field) compiled and read freed memory; a borrowed `h` let the caller write the element while the reference lived; the same through a `Box` field. Found by the 2026-09-24 review (A1, critical). Present before the 0.9.9 work: D-217's first narrowing still counted an `Array` index or a `Box` dereference as reaching the caller's memory. | `[BRW-8]`, `[LT-1]`, `[BRW-9]` | **fixed** | Only a dereference of a `ref`, or an element of a `Span` or `str`, reaches what a view's regions cover; anything owned before that is the parameter's own storage, `E3060`. | D-223 | **Two-phase borrows were checked at the wrong moment ([BRW-3])**: a shared borrow taken as another argument of the same call as a `mut` argument was never checked when the call activated the mutable borrow, so `refill(xs, xs[0])` with `mut dst` first and `r: ref String` (or a borrowed `String`, passed by address since ODR-024) compiled and `dst.clear()` left `r` reading a freed element; `s.add(s.n)` let the method's write show through the shared `r`. The opposite also held: `[BRW-3]`'s own accepted example, `f(v, v[0])` with `x: int`, was `E3021`, because the argument's copy was checked after activation. Found by the 2026-09-24 review (B2, critical, and two new items). Present before the 0.9.9 work; `[TYP-5]` rule 7 made the first form reachable without writing `ref`. | `[BRW-3]`, `[BCK-5]`, `[BCK-3]` | **fixed** | At a call, the arguments are read with the loans the call activates still reserved; each activated loan is then checked as a new mutable borrow against the shared loans taken during its reservation (`check_call_activation`). Tests: `BRW-3/accept_an_argument_copied_before_activation.em` (red without the first half), `BRW-3/reject_a_borrow_live_at_activation.em` (red without the second). |

Found by the 2026-09-24 review. | `[CLO-11]` | **fixed** | With no method of the name, a field of callable type (through references and boxes) is read and called by its type (`call_value`: a function value, a callable bound, or a closure). Any callee that is not a name is a value called the same way, so `(h.f)(5)`, `pick(b)(x)` and a lambda written in place work; `fs[1](x)` with a local `fs` indexes rather than instantiates, since a local shadows every item of its name. The struct/class field read is one function (`field_of`). Test: `CLO-11/accept_a_callable_field_is_called.em`. |

Found by the 2026-09-24 review (C5). | `[TYP-18]`, `[FN-6]` | **fixed** | `name[types]` where `name` is a generic function no local shadows instantiates it and is that instance's function value (`instantiation_value`), through the same `fn_value_of` a plain function value uses, so D-219's `@borrows` guard applies. Every type parameter must be given, a callable parameter's implicit one included, or it is `E2060` saying how many. The help for a bare generic name now offers `id[i32]`. `fs[1](x)` with a local `fs` still indexes (D-222). Present before the 0.9.9 work; over-strict, not unsound. | `[LT-7]`, `[LT-42]` | **fixed** | By ODR-048 (Hardened_23, D-284): in the callee's instance for the closure, the callable parameter's type is the closure's environment, a view, so it is a source of that instance (`[LT-1]`'s first kind) and `return f(s)` is accepted; the caller keeps the closure's captures borrowed through the result. Confirmed 2026-09-26 while closing the queue; the example compiled at `a778c99`. Tests: `LT-7/accept_a_capturing_closure_passed_to_a_callable_parameter.em`, `LT-7/reject_a_result_that_holds_a_closures_capture.em` (both red with ODR-048's instance sources removed). |
| D-219 | **A call through a callable value borrowed every argument**: `g(pos, "abc")` with `g: fn(mut int, str) -> str` kept `pos` mutably borrowed while the token lived (`E3021` on `println(pos)`), which is D-212 on the indirect path; `[LT-7]` gives the result only the callable type's source parameters. The conservative reading was also hiding a gap: a function whose `@borrows` names a `mut` `Copy` parameter (possible since ODR-024) became a value of `fn(mut int) -> ref int`, whose calls would not have kept the argument borrowed. Found building ODR-024's follow-ups. | `[LT-7]`, `[LT-1a]` | **fixed** | MIR records the callable type's source parameters on each indirect call (`FuncRef::Indirect.sources`, from `TypeTable::callable_sources`, the one definition of a source parameter the type checker also uses), and the borrow checker ties the result to those. A named function whose `@borrows` reaches past its sources is not a value: `E2020`, with the help to call it directly. | D-218 | **A class getter cannot return a view of a field**: `fn name(self) -> str: return self.name` on a class is `E3060`. A class handle is `Copy`, so it is not a source parameter, and the receiver rule is left out for class handles until `[EXC-18]` exists. Found by ODR-024. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[LT-1]`, `[EXC-18]` | **fixed** | `[LT-1]` rule 1 applies to a class receiver, passed by address (`[BRW-8]`; `receiver_by_address`, `param_by_address`), and the view carries the field's access to its caller (ADR-060): each body's summary says which fields of which arguments its result may borrow (`field_return_accesses`, computed to a fixpoint with the `Shared` summaries), and a caller holds those accesses from the call's return to the result's last use; through a virtual or interface call it reads every field of the receiver object (`ember_object_begin_read`). Verified: `EXC-18/accept_a_getter_returns_a_view_of_a_field.em` (was `E3060`), `EXC-18/run_fail_a_returned_view_holds_its_field.em` (through a second getter), `EXC-18/run_fail_a_view_from_a_virtual_getter_holds_the_object.em`; all three fail before the fix. |
| D-217 | **A reference to a view parameter's own slot can be returned**: `fn keep(x: str) -> ref str: return ref x` compiles, and the result points into the callee's copy of `x`, which ends with the frame. `check_escapes` exempts every loan rooted at a view parameter. Requiring the loan to go through the view (a `Deref`, or an element of a `Span`/`str`/`Array`) closes it but rejects `span.iter_mut()` on a `mut span: MutSpan[T]` (`SPN-5`), whose items point behind the span while the loan is on its slot: the regions do not yet separate a view's slot from what it points to. Found by the ODR-024 probes. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[LT-1]`, `[BRW-8]`, `[LT-3]` | **fixed** | A loan taken only to feed a view reborrow (`&mut span` into `SpanReborrow`) no longer carries the slot's storage obligation, because the reborrowed view points where the original does; the loan still excludes other uses. The view-parameter exemption then covers only loans that go through the view (a `Deref`, or an element of a `Span`, `str`, `Array` buffer or pointer), so `ref x` is `E3060` with the help to return `x` itself. Where `E3062` already reports the parameter (a generic `x: T` at `T = str`), `E3060` stands aside (`[DIA-14]`). Tests: `LT-1/reject_a_reference_to_a_view_parameter_itself.em` (red without the slot check), `LT-1/reject_a_reborrowed_local_view_escaping.em` (one error, naming the array), `SPN-5/accept_iterator_items_preserve_source_region.em` (red without the reborrow rule). Revised after the 2026-09-24 review: any span built-in's receiver loan hands out the target (`split_at` was `E3060`), a drop conflicts only with loans on its own storage (an `owned` view struct's drop was `E3021`), a slot reference is always `E3060` with a help that follows the result type (it was `E3062` with a help leading to `E3060`), and views held in locals are exempt as parameters' are (`q: ref Person = r; return q.age`). Tests: `SPN-5/accept_views_of_a_view_parameters_target.em`, `LT-1a/reject_a_reference_to_a_named_view_parameters_slot.em`, `LT-1/accept_a_local_view_returns_what_it_views.em`, `LT-1/reject_a_local_views_target_escaping.em`, each red without its part. |
| D-216 | **`[TYP-5]` rule 7 (auto-borrow to `ref T` at an argument) is not built**: against `fn name_of(p: ref Person)`, the call `name_of(p)` is `E2020`; the caller must write `name_of(ref p)`. Several helps ODR-024 asked for ("take `p: ref Point`; callers still write `f(p)`") depend on it, so the built helps say "take `x` as a `ref` parameter" without the second half. Found by the ODR-024 panel. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[TYP-5]`, `[TYP-14]` | **fixed** | Rule 7 is one step in the coercion every site uses: a place of type `T` where a shared `ref T` is expected becomes `ref place`, so arguments, initialisers, returns and view-struct fields all get it, and the borrow checker sees an ordinary loan. Generic inference reads `ref T` against a `T` argument through the borrow (the unifier had accepted the mismatch without solving `T`, so `same(r)` with `r: ref int` was `E2060` as well). A temporary, or a `ref mut` site, stays `E2020` with a help. The `E3062` help now says callers keep the same call. Tests: `TYP-5/accept_a_place_is_auto_borrowed_for_a_ref.em` (red without the coercion, and red without the inference step), `TYP-5/reject_a_temporary_is_not_auto_borrowed.em`, `TYP-5/reject_writing_a_place_its_auto_borrow_still_reads.em`. After the 2026-09-24 review: a `mut` parameter or `mut self` (a `ref mut T` local) auto-borrows as a shared reborrow of its target, and the `E3062` help promises the same call only to callers passing a place, with its own wording for a callable parameter and for a lambda. Tests: `TYP-5/accept_a_mut_parameter_is_auto_borrowed.em` (red without it), `TYP-5/reject_writing_through_mut_self_while_it_is_auto_borrowed.em`, `LT-7/reject_a_lambda_viewing_its_mut_parameter.em`. |
| D-215 | **The interface cache outlived the compiler that wrote it**: a rebuilt compiler of the same version loaded records written under the old parameter-passing ABI and stopped with `[LT-40] cannot load interface cache` (found while building ODR-024, whose ABI change is exactly that). `compiler_identity()` was the version string alone. | `[LT-40]`, `[BLD-2]` | **fixed** | `compiler_identity()` adds the running executable's size and modification time, and `SCHEMA_VERSION` is 8 for the ODR-024 ABI. Verified: the stale cache that stopped the build is now ignored and rebuilt. |
| D-214 | **A view outliving a temporary argument was reported as `E3021` about `_15`**: `t = head(make())` then a use of `t` named a compiler temporary and a write that the program does not contain. Found by ODR-024. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[EXP-4]`, `[LT-1]` | **fixed** | `E3060` "this temporary is dropped at the end of its statement while it is still borrowed", with the help to bind it to a local first. Test: `LT-1/reject_a_view_outliving_a_temporary_argument.em`. |
| D-213 | **Whether a parameter was a source depended on the instantiation**: the borrow checker read each instantiation's concrete types, so `fn pick[T](xs: Array[T], x: T) -> ref T` tied its result to `x` as well when `T` was `str`, and a generic body's return was accepted for some `T` and not others. ODR-024 fixes the sources by the declared signature, with each type parameter taken to be `Copy`. Found building ODR-024. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[LT-1]`, `[MONO-1]` | **fixed** | The type checker computes the source set once from the declared (for an instantiation, the generic) signature and carries it on the HIR function and MIR body as `sources`; rules 2 and 3 read it. Tests: `LT-1/accept_a_generic_parameter_counts_as_copy.em` (goes red when the instantiation's own signature is used), `LT-1/reject_a_view_of_a_generic_parameter.em`, `LT-1a/reject_borrows_names_a_copy_struct_holding_a_cell.em`. Not covered: a method of a generic type is instantiated with its owner, so its non-receiver parameters are still read from the owner's instantiation (rule 1 takes the receiver, so only a static or `owned self` method can differ). |
| D-212 | **Every `mut` parameter counted as a source**, so `fn next_token(mut pos: int, src: str) -> str` tied its result to `pos` too, and `println(pos)` while the token lived was `E3021`. `[LT-1]` counts only views. Found by the ODR-024 panel. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[LT-1]` | **fixed** | A `mut` parameter of a `Copy` type is not a source; `@borrows` may still name it. Tests: `LT-1/accept_a_mut_copy_parameter_is_not_a_source.em`, `LT-1/reject_a_view_of_a_mut_copy_parameter_without_borrows.em`, `LT-1a/accept_borrows_names_a_mut_copy_parameter.em`. |
| D-211 | **`[BRW-8]`'s "cannot be observed" was false** for a `@derive(Copy)` struct holding a `Cell`: passed as a copy, `advance` twice printed `0`. A specification defect: the rule let a borrowed `Copy` value be copied without excepting interior mutability. Found by the ODR-024 panel. | `[BRW-8]`, `[FN-1]` | **fixed** | ODR-024: a `Copy` value holding a `Cell` or `UnsafeCell` is passed by address; Hardened_5 restates `[BRW-8]`. Test: `BRW-8/accept_a_copy_struct_holding_a_cell_is_passed_by_address.em`, broken once. |
| D-210 | **A borrowed `RefCell`, or an `Array` pushed through one, corrupted the heap**: the spec's own IX.7 `Sprite`/`Scene` shape with twenty `add` calls exited `0xC0000374`. The callee's `push` reallocated a buffer the caller's header still pointed to, because a borrowed parameter arrived as a shallow copy. Found by the ODR-024 panel. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[FN-1]`, `[BRW-8]` | **fixed** | ODR-024: a borrowed parameter whose type is not `Copy` is passed by address (`T*` in C), everywhere a call is made: direct and generic calls, receivers, `dyn` adapters, callable values, defaults, operators, derived `clone`, and the calls MIR builds itself (`Cell.update` callbacks, the arena map's `eq`). Test: `FN-1/accept_cell_and_refcell_through_borrowed_parameters.em` (prints `2` and `24 1`). |
| D-209 | **`Cell` writes through a borrowed parameter were lost**: the IX.7 example printed `0` and `0` instead of `2` and `100`, because the callee wrote into its own copy. Found by the ODR-024 panel. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[FN-1]`, `[CELL-1]` | **fixed** | As D-210: the parameter is the caller's place. Shared references are emitted without `const`, so a `Cell` write through one is valid C. Test: `FN-1/accept_cell_and_refcell_through_borrowed_parameters.em`. |
| D-208 | **One mistake, several errors (`[DIA-14]`)**, found when the harness began failing on unexpected diagnostics: `E2130` after a `static`/`const` initialiser that already failed; `E3027` after a `mut` argument that already failed; a capturing closure's body reported twice (its capture probe was not quiet); a generic call with a failed argument reported the argument twice and instantiated a body around it (`cannot find f`); a generic call or method whose argument misses a bound instantiated the body anyway (`E1010 has no method`); `E3040` on the same read as `E3050` for a local never given a value; `E2100` after an unsupported conditional overwrite or a failed `super.init(…)`; three `E0100`s after `E0106`'s multi-statement closure in brackets; `E2020`'s "numeric types" note on non-numeric types (`[TYP-4]`: the note is for two numbers); and FN-5's `needs a value` after an argument that did not bind (in 891642e). Present before the 0.9.9 work, except the last. | `[DIA-14]`, `[TYP-4]` | **fixed** | Each follow-on is skipped once the first error is reported; the parser skips the rest of a multi-statement closure to the enclosing `,` or bracket. Tests: the migrated corpus, which now fails on any of them. |
| D-207 | **`E3041` pointed at the use, not at the move**: in `if c: consume(a); a = …` / `else: consume(a)` inside a loop, the error landed on the first branch's `consume(a)` (which puts `a` back) with the label `moved here`, rather than on the move that leaves `a` gone for the next iteration. Found by D-205. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[OWN-4]` | **fixed** | When `E3041` is reported, a small per-variable pass finds the moves that reach the use with no assignment between; the error points at that move and names the use as a secondary label. Test: `OWN-4/reject_a_move_missing_on_some_path.em`, now tied to its line. |
| D-206 | **`L3011` fired across `print`**: every program printing under a `RefCell` guard got the lint (twice, once per printed piece), though `[CELL-7]` (F-187) says it fires only for calls that can reach the cell, never across `print`. Found by D-205 (57 occurrences). Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[CELL-7]` | **fixed** | Compiler-known operations that run no Ember code (printing, formatting, text and view queries, `push`, comparisons) are skipped; any other call still lints. Test: `CELL-7/warning_guard_live_across_call_l3011.em` (a user call warns, `println` does not) and every `CELL-*` case, which now fail on an unexpected `L3011`. |
| D-205 | **The harness passed missing and unexpected diagnostics.** `[TST-1]` says both fail. The harness matched an annotation's code as a substring of stderr, so an annotation shown in a diagnostic's source excerpt satisfied itself (`RNG-4`'s `E2215` passed while the compiler reported `E2240`), and a diagnostic no annotation named was never noticed (72 files). | `[TST-1]` | **fixed** | The harness reads `ember check --json`: each `#$ error[…]`/`#$ warning[…]` claims one diagnostic whose code matches and whose text (message, labels, notes or helps) contains the annotation's, on the annotated line when the annotation trails code; any diagnostic left unclaimed fails. `tasks/impl-0.9.9/annotations.py` checks the same in seconds. The corpus was migrated: unused bindings renamed `_x`, cycle lints declared, annotations moved to the line the diagnostic points at, stale 0.9.9 tests updated (`RNG-4` used `/`, `FN-6`/`CLO-2` relied on `i32` literals), and D-206–D-208 fixed. |
| D-204 | **An assignment whose target is not a place was accepted and did nothing**: `xs.as_span() = …` (and, with slicing, `xs[0..2] = [5, 6]`) wrote a temporary and dropped it. Destructuring already refused it. Found by the 2026-09-24 review. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[EXP-5]` | **fixed** | `E2140 assignment target is not a place`; a slice target's help says to assign the elements one at a time. Tests: `EXP-5/reject_a_slice_as_an_assignment_target.em`, `accept_assigning_to_places.em`, broken once. |
| D-203 | **Writing through a shared `Span` was accepted**: `xs.as_span()[1] = 42` (and `xs[1..][0] = 42`) wrote into a borrowed parameter's caller or a `for` loop's own iterable, getting past `E3023` and `E3020`. `[SPN-1]` makes `Span` the shared view. Found by the 2026-09-24 review. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[SPN-1]`, `[BRW-1]`, `[CTL-2]` | **fixed** | An index into a `Span` is a write through a shared borrow: `E3021`. Tests: `SPN-1/reject_writing_through_a_shared_span.em`, `reject_writing_through_a_slice_inside_its_loop.em`, broken once. |
| D-202 | **A view of a class field begins no read access.** `[EXC-1]`/`[EXC-19]` make a view of a field a long-term read access; `v = b.items[1..]` (or `v: Span[int] = b.items`) starts none, so `c.items.clear()` or `c.items.push(…)` through an aliasing handle `c = b` reallocates the buffer `v` points into (ASan: heap-use-after-free). The static checker only catches the same-handle case. Found by the 2026-09-24 review. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[EXC-1]`, `[EXC-19]` | **fixed** | `[EXC-19]` is built (ADR-060): every non-`Copy` field of a class has an access word in front of it, listed in the class's type info; a loan of a place through such a field (a view, a `ref`, the field passed or iterated) holds that word for the loan's NLL region, and a read or write through one without a loan holds it for its statement or call (`field_accesses`, `borrows.rs`); a `mut self` call's whole-object write is taken by its caller over every word of the object's dynamic class (`ember_object_begin_write`). Verified: `EXC-1/run_fail_a_write_while_a_view_of_the_field_lives.em`, `EXC-1/run_fail_clearing_while_a_span_of_the_field_lives.em`, `EXC-1/run_fail_pushing_while_iterating_the_field.em`, `EXC-19/run_fail_a_mut_self_call_while_a_field_is_viewed.em` and `EXC-2/run_fail_a_read_while_a_mut_self_call_writes.em` each printed freed memory or ran on before the fix, and panic with `[EXC-6]`'s message after it; accept cases for different fields and for reads sharing one. |
| D-201 | **`String` and `Array[u8]` are one type in the compiler**, so an `Array[u8]` slices as text (`bytes[0..2]` is a `str`, with character-boundary panics), converts to `str` without validation (`[TXT-2]`), and prints nested as quoted text (`[bytes]` shows `['hi']`). Generic code over `Array[T]` fails at `T = u8`. Found by the 2026-09-24 review. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[TXT-2]`, `[TXT-4]`, `[SPN-2]` | **fixed** | `String` is `TyKind::Vec { elem: u8, text: true }` (`common.string`) and `Array[u8]` is `text: false`: one buffer and one drop, two types (ADR-061). Text paths key on the flag (`is_text`, slicing, printing, the method split); collection paths take only `text: false`; a `String` unifies with no `Array[T]`. An `Array[u8]` slices to a `Span[u8]`, prints `[104, 105]`, works as `Array[T]`'s `T = u8`, and converts to neither `str` nor `String` (`E2020`). Verified: `TXT-2/accept_bytes_stay_bytes.em` and `TXT-2/reject_bytes_becoming_text_without_validation.em` both fail before the fix. Built since (the second review, G6-4, found this note stale): `String.push(c: char)` (`[TXT-11]`, `TXT-11/accept_pushing_unicode_chars`) and the validating `Span[u8]` to `str` conversion (`[TXT-2]`, `[TXT-5]`, `TXT-2/accept_validating_bytes_as_text`). |
| D-200 | **A class handle in a list literal, `Box` or `Shared` was released twice**: `xs = [token]`, `Box(token)`, `Shared(token)` panicked `release of object with no strong references` at scope end. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[TYP-38]`, `[HEAP-1]`, `[OWN-3]` | **fixed** | A `Copy` value that owns something reaches these builtins as a move: a fresh value as it is, a place through a temporary copy (which retains). Test: `TYP-38/accept_class_handles_in_literals_and_boxes.em`, broken once. |
| D-199 | **An inferred `Array` of views skipped `[TYP-15]`**: `vs = [xs[2..]]` (or `[xs.as_span()]`) was accepted with no loan kept on `xs`, so reassigning `xs` freed what `vs[0]` pointed into; `names.push(word[1..])` likewise. Only a written `Array[Span[int]]` was checked. Found by the 2026-09-24 review. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[TYP-15]`, `[LT-3]` | **fixed** | The borrow checker requires every view stored into an `Array` (list literal, `push`, `insert`, `a[i] = v`, `a[i] = f(…)`) to be `static`, as `Box` contents were already; a view read out of an `Array` whose elements are exactly its type is one of those elements. `[TYP-15]`'s `names = ["ann", "bob"]` still compiles. Tests: `TYP-15/reject_a_slice_stored_in_an_array.em`, `reject_a_borrowed_str_pushed_into_an_array.em`, `accept_static_strings_and_element_reads_in_arrays.em`, broken once. A `mut` parameter or `mem.replace` aimed at an element stays D-198's half. |
| D-198 | **`Array[str]` is rejected where it is formed** (`E3063 str is a view, so it may not be stored in a container element`), so `[TYP-15]`'s own example `names = ["ann", "bob"]` does not compile; `Span[str]` likewise. 0.9.9 made `[TYP-15]` a rule about the stored values' regions, and `Box` already follows it. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[TYP-15]`, `[LT-3]` | **fixed** | With D-352 and D-353: every write path into an `Array`'s element meets the `static` requirement, and a view read out of one is `static` (`heap_reads`). `Array[str]()` and `Span[str]` are types like any other (ODR-069). Tests: `TYP-15/reject_a_non_static_view_through_an_element.em`, `TYP-15/accept_a_view_copied_out_of_an_array_is_static.em`, `TYP-15/accept_an_owning_container_at_a_view_type.em`. |
| D-197 | **`println(xs)` of an `Array` (or any value with no printer) was accepted and emitted C the C compiler rejected** (`passing 'ember_vec' to parameter of incompatible type 'ember_str'`): one argument took a separate path that skipped the check the several-argument path makes. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[STD-9]`, `[TYP-39]` | **fixed** | Every `print`/`println` goes through one path; a class handle, which has no `Display` (`[TYP-36]`), is `E2040`, and a type whose printer is not built yet is `E0900`. `compile-fail/class_drop_self_escape_owned_capture.em` printed a class handle inside its closure; it now passes the handle to a function, so the test still shows only its `E3016`. Each printed piece keeps its own span, so a borrow still reads as used at the argument. Test: `STD-9/reject_printing_a_value_with_no_printer.em`, broken once. |
| D-196 | **A temporary made in one arm of a `match` or conditional was reported as `E3050` on a compiler temporary** (`` `_9` may not have a value here ``): the temporary lives to the end of the statement (`[EXP-4]`), so its drop sits after the join, where definite initialisation treated the drop as a read. `x if c else width(f"{v}")` and `Err(e) => panic(f"{e}")` were both rejected. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | Part XVIII §4.6, `[DRP-3]` | **fixed** | A drop is not a read: drop elaboration already removes the drop of an empty place and gives a maybe-empty one a flag, as §4.6 says. Test: `DRP-3/accept_temporary_made_in_one_arm.em`, broken once. |
| D-195 | **`s += t` on a `String` was accepted and emitted C the C compiler rejected** (`invalid operands to binary expression ('ember_vec' and 'ember_vec')`): every `a op= b` became `a = a op b` without the operator's own check that `+` applies to numbers. `String + str` was rejected instead of extending the `String`. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[TXT-11]`, `[TYP-21]` | **fixed** | `s += t` on a `String` appends its text (`push_str`); any other non-numeric `op=` is `E2020`; `a + b` with a `String` on the left moves it into a block, appends and returns it; `str + str` is `E2040` with an f-string help. Tests: `TXT-11/accept_appending_text.em`, `TXT-11/reject_adding_two_strs.em`, broken once. |
| D-194 | **An f-string could not write a `String` twice, and could not write it at all.** `f"{s} {s}"` was `E3040 s has been moved out of`: each hole lowered its value as a move. With that removed, the hole passed the `Array[u8]` itself to `ember_fmt_str`, which the C compiler rejected, the same shape as D-191. Present before the 0.9.9 work (same at `b597d52`). | `[LEX-19]`, `[STD-9]` | **fixed** | A `String` in a hole is borrowed as the `str` it holds, as `print` does since D-191, and MIR lowers every hole as a borrow. Test: `LEX-19/accept_a_string_is_borrowed_by_an_fstring.em`, broken once. |
| D-193 | **`for x in [1, 2]:` was rejected** with `E3020 cannot mutate _5 while it is borrowed by this loop`. The loop viewed its container through a hidden `Span`, and a temporary container (a list literal, a call's result) was dropped at the end of the statement that made the view, which the checker saw as a write under the loop's borrow. Present before the 0.9.9 work. | `[CTL-1]`, `[EXP-4]` | **fixed** | A container that is not a place is bound to a local of the loop's own block, so it is dropped after the loop (`keep_alive`); `sum`, `any`, `all`, `enumerate`, `zip` and `reversed` do the same. Test: `CTL-1/accept_a_list_literal_and_a_view_iterate.em`, broken once. |
| D-192 | **Negating a signed integer at its minimum did not panic**: `-x` with `x = i64.MIN` printed `-9223372036854775808`, and was C's undefined `-INT64_MIN`. `[TYP-8]`: overflow panics in every profile. | `[TYP-8]` | **fixed** | A non-literal `-x` on a signed integer is the checked `0 - x`, whose checking follows the function's overflow policy (`@overflow(wrap)` wraps). Tests: `TYP-8/run_fail_negating_the_minimum.em`, `STD-26/run_fail_abs_of_the_minimum.em`. |
| D-191 | **`println(s)` with one `String` argument produced C the C compiler rejected** (`passing 'ember_vec' to parameter of incompatible type 'ember_str'`). Present before the 0.9.9 work (same at `a9294b9`); `[TXT-9]` makes it common, since string literals now initialise `String`s. | `[STD-9]` | **fixed** | The one-argument path borrows a `String` as its `str`, as the other path does. Test: `STD-9/accept_a_string_prints_as_its_text.em`. |
| D-190 | **Returning a view of a local reports two errors.** `fn leak() -> Span[int]: tmp = [41]; return view(tmp)` gives `E3021 tmp cannot be written while it is borrowed` (nothing writes `tmp`: the end-of-function drop is counted as a write) and then the right `E3060 tmp does not live long enough`. Present before the 0.9.9 work (same at `a9294b9`). | `[DIA-14]`, `[LT-3]` | **fixed** | At a `return`, a drop of storage that a still-live loan points into is left to `check_escapes`, which reports the escape (B7), as the existing Arena case already was. Tests: `LT-3/reject_a_returned_view_of_a_local.em`, and `ui.rs` `a_returned_view_of_a_local_is_one_error` for the exact code list (the conformance harness does not yet fail on unexpected diagnostics), broken once. |
| D-189 | **Compilations run side by side could fail with an internal compiler error, `[LT-40] cannot load interface cache`.** Each wrote its interface records with a plain `fs::write` into a directory shared by every program in the same folder (the std modules are in every program), so another compilation could read a half-written record. Seen as rare "rejected" files with no error in the parallel corpus survey; `tasks/impl-0.9.9/cache_stress.py` reproduced it, 30 internal errors in 596 compiles. | `[LT-40]` | **fixed** | A record is written to a temporary file beside it and renamed into place, so a reader sees the old record or the new one; a rename lost to a simultaneous writer keeps that writer's complete record. The stress script: 0 in 1,192 compiles. |
| D-188 | **A float-to-integer `as` compiled to C's plain conversion.** `1e20 as i32` and `NaN as i32` printed `-2147483648`; C leaves an out-of-range float-to-integer conversion undefined. The adopted specification already required saturation with NaN to 0 (Part IV, "float→int saturating with NaN→0"), as does 0.9.9 `[TYP-6]`. | `[TYP-6]`, `[CG-C-1]` | **fixed** | The backend calls `ember_ftoi_<T>` (runtime template), which tests NaN and both bounds before converting. Found by the 0.9.9 audit (`docs/AUDIT-0.9.9.md`). No specification change. |
| D-187 | **`==` on a `str`, `String`, struct, tuple, array or payload enum was accepted and produced C the C compiler rejected** (`invalid operands to binary expression`). The checker let every comparison through for non-numeric operands; C compares only scalars, pointers and unit-only enums. | `[STR-5]`, `[TYP-37]` | **fixed** | A comparison C cannot do is a `ValueCompare` builtin: text by bytes (runtime `ember_str_cmp`, all six operators), aggregates whose components all have `Eq` through a generated field-wise `static bool` function per type. Anything else is `E2040` naming `Eq` or `Ord`. Tests: `STR-5/accept_field_wise_equality.em`, `STR-5/reject_ordering_a_struct.em`, `TYP-37/accept_text_compares_by_bytes.em`, each broken once. |
| D-186 | **A function declared to return a value could fall off the end of its body.** `fn f(x) -> int: if x > 0: return 1` compiled, and the C returned the uninitialised return slot (undefined behaviour; `f(-1)` printed `0` by chance). The same path gave `Result[void, E]` functions a garbage result instead of `Ok(())`. | `[FN-10]`, `[CG-C-1]` | **fixed** | The checker completes every body: `void` returns, `Result[void, E]` gets `return Ok(())` (`[FN-10]`), and any other return type that can reach the end is `E2182` (ODR-023), decided by a conservative definite-return analysis (`return`, `Never`-typed expressions, `if`/`else` and `match` that all diverge, `while true` without `break`). `tests/conformance/FN-10/` covers all three; the unmodified compiler accepted the reject case and printed `0`. |

## 2026-09-23 — 0.9.9 implementation, first slices

| # | Defect | Rule | Status | Fixed in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D-185 | **Calling a callable local inside a closure reported `E1010`.** A local of callable type called by name in a lambda body (`fn() => f(1)`) was looked up only in the closure's own scope; other uses of the same name were captured. With that fixed, a plain closure's shared-borrow capture (`ref fn(A) -> R`) then failed in C, because the type planner emitted a function-pointer `typedef` only for a function type held by value, and a `typedef` cannot be forward-declared. Found when D-184 made `LT-10/reject_latebound_owned_capture_publication.em` assert its code. | `[CLO-2]`, `[CLO-3]` | **fixed** | `synth_call` resolves a capture candidate through `resolve_capture` and calls through the captured value; the C planner requires a function type behind `ref`/`*`. `tests/conformance/CLO-2/accept_calling_a_captured_callable_local.em` covers the plain and `owned fn` closures; before the fix both were `E1010`. No specification change. |
| D-184 | **Eight conformance cases asserted no diagnostic code.** The harness read `#$ error[CODE]: message` and silently dropped the bare form `#$ error[CODE]`, so every case written that way passed on any rejection. One of them (`LT-10/reject_latebound_owned_capture_publication.em`) was being rejected for the wrong reason (D-185). | `[TST-6]`, `[TST-1]` | **fixed** | `code_and_message` in `compiler/ember_driver/tests/milestones.rs` accepts both forms, and `parse_expectations_reads_the_annotation_forms` pins them. With the fix, seven of the eight report their code and the eighth exposed D-185. |
| D-183 | **A nested untyped literal kept a type the backend lowered as `int32_t`.** Defaulting and adoption set the type of an operator tree's root only; `-7.5` inside `-7.5 * 2.0` stayed untyped, its temporary was declared `int32_t`, and `println(-7.5 * 2.0)` printed `-14`. Present in the 0.8/0.9.8 compiler, silent. | `[LEX-16]`, `[LEX-17]` | **fixed** | `adopt_literal` recurses through untyped unary and non-comparison binary nodes, and `commit` defaults through it. `tests/conformance/TYP-29/accept_float_floor_is_pythons.em` pins `-15`; the unmodified compiler printed `-14`. No specification change. |
| D-182 | **A type that owns itself through an indirection overflowed the compiler's stack.** `TypeTable::is_generic` and `has_unsized_by_value` walked struct, class and enum fields with no cycle guard, so `struct Tree: kids: Array[Tree]` (and the Part VIII `Node` example) crashed the compiler as soon as a method was called on the `Array`; code generation then expanded the element drop inline without end. | `[TYP-1]`, `[OWN-2]`, `[DRP-2]` | **fixed** | Both walks carry a visited set of nominal types. `Array` elements and `Box` payloads of aggregate type drop through a generated `static` glue function per type (`em_drop_glue_N`), defined to a fixpoint after the bodies, so the recursion is a runtime call. `tests/run-pass/recursive_types_through_array.em` covers a struct tree and a class list; before the fix both overflowed the compiler's stack. No specification change. |

## 2026-09-22 — field lookup skipped ordinary reference auto-dereference

| # | Defect | Rule | Status | Fixed in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D-181 | **The MIR verifier treated `Unreachable` as though it were a source-level return.** An exhaustive `match` in a `mut self` virtual method may leave the method-duration dynamic access open on its compiler-created impossible fallback edge. That edge has no runtime continuation, but verification rejected it for lacking a cleanup that no execution can observe. | `[MIR-3]`, `[ENM-2]`, `[CLS-4]`, `[DSP-1]`, `[EXC-1]` | **fixed** | Dynamic-access closure is now required only at `Return`; `Unreachable` is correctly treated as a dead control-flow edge. The MIR unit regression proves an access may remain open only on `Unreachable`, while the existing return regression still rejects an open access at a real return. `tests/conformance/TYP-16/accept_generic_class_virtual_mut_weak_field_dispatch.em` was red before the correction and now prints `9` in debug, release, and shipping while pinning virtual dispatch, the write-access operation, and weak upgrade. The frozen target is explicit, so no specification change, ADR, ODR, owner decision, or version change was needed. |
| D-180 | **Field lookup adjusted `RefCell` guards and `Box[T]` values, but not ordinary `ref T` / `ref mut T` values before selecting a field owner.** Thus `Shared.get()` correctly returned `ref Token`, while the explicitly valid `live.get().value` attempted to find `value` on `ref Token` and failed with `E2020`. | `[TYP-14]`, `[HEAP-4]`, `[WK-12]`, `[TST-26]` | **fixed** | Field selection now reads through ordinary reference layers before nominal-field lookup, using the same adjustment already required for method receivers. It does not read through `Shared[T]` itself: `.get()` remains the explicit `[HEAP-4]` payload boundary. The focused class-held `Weak[Shared[Token]]` case failed before the correction and now prints `7` in debug, release, and shipping; its generated C assertion confirms the weak upgrade path. The frozen target is explicit, so no specification change, ADR, ODR, owner decision, or version change was needed. |

| # | Defect | Rule | Status | Fixed in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D-179 | **`Cell.set` reached its compiler-known receiver through `ref Cell[T]`, but Cell lowering erased that operation into an ordinary MIR field assignment.** The C backend therefore emitted a store through `const Cell*`, which its C compiler correctly rejected even though `[CELL-1]` explicitly gives the shared `Cell` API permission to mutate its private payload. | `[CELL-1]`, `[CELL-2]`, `[TYP-14]` | **fixed** | C place rendering now removes `const` only while dereferencing a compiler-created `Cell[T]` wrapper. The wrapper's private, no-source-module identity makes this a compiler capability boundary; other shared references retain their C `const` representation, and source code cannot reach Cell's payload field. The focused `ref Cell` probe failed in all profiles before the correction and now prints `42` in debug, release, and shipping; its C assertion pins the casted Cell store and excludes a runtime Cell helper. The frozen target is explicit, so no specification change, ADR, ODR, owner decision, or version change was needed. |

## 2026-09-22 — borrowed `Shared` receivers skipped auto-dereference

| # | Defect | Rule | Status | Fixed in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D-178 | **Method resolution recognized compiler-known `Shared[T]` only before removing an outer ordinary reference.** A normal closure that captured a loop-yielded `Shared[T]` by reference therefore saw `ref Shared[T]` and rejected `token.get()` with `E1010`, even though the language requires method receivers to auto-dereference ordinary `ref`/`ref mut` layers. | `[TYP-14]`, IV.11, `[HEAP-4]`, `[RC-2e]`, `[CLO-1]`, `[CLO-2]` | **fixed** | Method lookup now reads through ordinary reference layers before built-in or inherent lookup, while preserving `ref dyn`/class-interface values as their dynamic-call carriers. This reaches the `Shared[T]` owner but never reads through it to the payload, so `.get()` remains the explicit `[HEAP-4]` boundary. The focused normal-closure loop probe was rejected before the correction and now prints `7` in debug, release, and shipping; its generated C contains exactly one retain (the original Array insertion), proving the non-`owned` capture remains an `[RC-2e]` elision. The frozen target is explicit, so no specification change, ADR, ODR, owner decision, or version change was needed. |

## 2026-09-22 — owned closures captured borrowed loop references

| # | Defect | Rule | Status | Fixed in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D-177 | **An `owned fn` directly capturing a class or `Shared[T]` handle yielded by a source `for` loop preserved the compiler-internal `ref` instead of copying the handle into its owning environment.** A `Shared` capture consequently failed method lookup as `ref Shared[T]`; a class capture could store a reference whose loop/container lifetime ends before the closure is used. | `[RC-1]`, `[RC-2e]`, `[CLO-1]`, `[CLO-2]`, `[TYP-14]` | **fixed** | HIR marks borrowed counted-handle loop yields. During `owned fn` capture only, type checking reads through that marked reference so the environment stores the class/Shared owner, while ordinary explicit reference captures keep their reference type. The normal aggregate copy emits the retain at environment construction. The focused Shared probe was rejected before the correction and now prints `7`; its class companion does likewise. Both all-profile cases pin exactly two retains—source Array insertion and capture. `[RC-2e]` explicitly requires this escape and retain site, so no specification change, ADR, ODR, owner decision, or version change was needed. |

## 2026-09-22 — loop tuple patterns lost their bindings

| # | Defect | Rule | Status | Fixed in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D-176 | **The `for` parser stopped after its first pattern, so the specified `for a, b in q` syntax produced an unexpected-comma error.** Parenthesizing the tuple only reached a second defect: array and iterator lowering created one anonymous item local but never introduced the tuple's leaf bindings, making `a` and `b` unknown in the body. | `[CTL-1]`, `[CTL-2]`, `[RC-1]`, `[RC-2e]`, `[TYP-14]` | **fixed** | `for` headers now normalize a comma-separated pattern list to a tuple pattern. Array and borrowed-iterator lowering retain their hidden reference to the element and create leaf locals as references into it, preserving the required borrow for the whole loop and retaining class handles only at an explicit owned use. Value-yielding iterators instead use the ordinary pattern matcher, transferring their payload fields into the loop body. The Array and Span-iterator cases each print `7`, `8` in every profile and pin six retains: two source values, two Array transfers, and the two owned-call boundaries. `accept_value_iterator_destructured_class_handles_owned_parameters_transfer.em` separately proves value transfer with eight retains through tuple and `Option` construction, pattern binding, and the two owned calls. `[CTL-1]` specifies both the syntax and borrowed-yield meaning, so no specification change, ADR, ODR, owner decision, or version change was needed. |

|---|---|---|---|---|

The override correctly released its owned parameter and then the loop's Array cleanup underflowed the same strong reference. | `[FN-1]`, `[DSP-2]`, `[RC-1]`, `[RC-2e]` | **fixed** | Virtual call records now carry the already checked full parameter-mode vector, including the receiver, solely as compiler metadata. The C backend retains copied explicit owned arguments before dispatch; moved arguments still transfer. `accept_loop_class_handle_owned_virtual_parameter_retains.em` printed `7` then panicked before the correction and now passes in every profile, pinning the three retains from source insertion, the Worker-to-Consumer upcast, and the virtual owned-call transfer. The rules define the virtual ABI and ownership transfer unambiguously, so no specification change, ADR, ODR, owner decision, or version change was needed. |

The implementation then correctly released its parameter, after which loop cleanup underflowed the Array's sole reference. | `[FN-1]`, `[TYP-22]`, `[RC-1]`, `[RC-2e]` | **fixed** | Dynamic interface call records now retain the checked explicit-parameter mode vector alongside the slot's type signature; the MIR verifier rejects a mismatched vector, and C generation retains every copied `owned` argument at the erased call boundary. `accept_loop_class_handle_owned_interface_parameter_retains.em` printed `7` and then panicked before the correction; it now passes in debug, release, and shipping and pins exactly two retains: source insertion and dynamic owned-call transfer. ## 2026-09-22 — indirect calls erased `owned` argument semantics

| # | Defect | Rule | Status | Fixed in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D-173 | **Indirect-call lowering preserved `mut` parameter modes but treated every other argument as borrowed.** An `fn(owned T)` value therefore copied a move-only argument instead of transferring it, and passed a copied class handle without retaining it. The latter made `operation(token)` from a class-handle loop yield print successfully then underflow the reference count when the Array cleaned up; the former could run a move-only value's destructor in both caller and callee. | `[FN-1]`, `[FN-6a]`, `[OWN-2]`, `[OWN-3]`, `[RC-1]`, `[RC-2e]` | **fixed** | MIR lowering now uses the callable type's `owned` parameter mode to preserve the normal move-or-copy operand. The C backend reads the same mode vector from the indirect callee's function type and retains copied owning arguments before the call; moved arguments stay transfers. `accept_loop_class_handle_owned_indirect_parameter_retains.em` was red before the correction (it printed `7` then panicked) and now passes in every profile with the two required retains. `accept_owned_indirect_parameter_is_transferred.em` proves a move-only `Resource` reaches exactly one destructor. The rules explicitly require ordinary ownership modes to survive callable indirection, so no specification change, ADR, ODR, owner decision, or version change was needed. |

The caller's Array then released the sole reference at loop exit, producing a runtime `release of object with no strong references` panic. | `[RC-1]`, `[RC-2e]`, `[FN-1]`, `[OWN-2]` | **fixed** | The backend now reads the already-verified parameter-mode metadata for direct calls and retains every copied owning argument before transferring it to an `owned` parameter. ## 2026-09-20 — generic-class substitutions did not rebuild nested classes

| # | Defect | Rule | Status | Fixed in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D-159 | **Substitution rebuilt instantiated generic structs but not generic classes.** A method on `Factory[T]` with a `Payload[T]` parameter or result retained the symbolic materialization `Payload_T` when `Factory[i32]` was checked, so a real `Payload[i32]` argument or return was treated as a different type. The same gap prevented a generic class from exercising an inherited generic interface default through that method. | `[TYP-16]`, `[IFC-1]` | **fixed** | `substitute_ty` now reconstructs an instantiated class from its recorded generic origin after substituting its arguments, exactly as it already reconstructs an instantiated struct. `tests/run-pass/generic_class_interface_default_generic_method.em` was rejected before the correction and now verifies direct and late calls to the specialized interface default, plus a nested generic-class parameter and result, in debug, release, and shipping. `generic_class_interface_default_dyn_box.em` covers the complementary non-generic default through borrowed and owned dynamic interface adapters. The specification already composes these constructs; no specification, ADR, owner decision, or version changed. |
